# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1990

## Introduction

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam (*11.17.27) Lord Kṛṣṇa describes the *ācārya* as *sarva-deva-māyāḥ,*

*“*the representative of all the demigods.” As ordinary demigod-worshipers perform fire sacrifices to please their *Iṣṭa-deva* and win some material boon, every year the disciples and granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda perform a great worldwide fire sacrifice known as the Vyāsa-pūjā book to please him and evoke his mercy. In this sacrifice the devotees kindle the firewood of their literary talents with the spark of remembrance of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s pastimes and qualities. As the oblations of their homages pour forth onto page or computer screen, the fires of faith and devotion blaze high, lighting up the sacrificial arena, the International Society for Krishna Cosnciousness.

The Vyāsa-pūjā book is also a worldwide flower offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda. Some devotees, perhaps hailing form foreign lands and struggling with the English language, offer a few fresh, delicate petals- a few words of praise or prayer to their beloved *guru* that are accepted with affection because offered with deep devotion and love. Others, more adept at English literary expressions, offer a single exquisite lotus, rose, or gardenia- a short poem or work of prose filled with the sweet fragrance of heartfelt gratitude and glorification of their eternal master, teacher, and friend. And some string together garlands of aromatic *campaka, mallikā,* and *mālatī* flowers- long essays or poems filled with recollections of Prabhupāda and words of profound thankfulness, praise, and entreaty. Then the devotees lovingly offer these petals, flowers, and garlands to Śrīla Prabhupāda until they’re piled high.

The Vyāsa-pūjā book is also a multicourse feast offered to Śrīla Prabhupāda on the anniversary of his glorious appearance day, the most blessed event. In their individual corners of the big kitchen, ISKCON, disciples work hard preparing dozens of varieties of *puṣpānna* rice and *sabjī; purīs* and *capātīs* cooked to perfection; spicy *kacauris, pakorās,*and *samosās*; a hundred hot-and-sweet chutneys; fragrant nectar drinks and rich sweet rice; *sandeśa, rasagullās, gulābjāmuns* and countless other delectable sweets. The main ingredients in all these preparations are faith in and love for Śrīla Prabhupāda, and the spices are an understanding of the *guru’s* glories from the *śāstra,* dedication to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *saṅkīrtana* mission, and a desire to rid oneself of *anarthas* once and for all and surrender fully to Lord Kṛṣṇa. When all is ready, the BBT staff makes up the plates-the Vyāsa-pūjā book – and offers the feast to Śrīla Prabhupāda for his pleasure.

But most of all, the Vyāsa-pūjā book is a *mahā-kīrtana* echoing from all corners of the ISKCON world. Prayers, praise, and *pranāms* blend into a harmonious hymn to Śrīla Prabhupāda. The *bṛhat mṛdaṅa* powers the chanting with its steady beat, while the devotees play *karatāla* rhythms on their computer and typewriter keyboards. And as with the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, there is no fault but rather great merit in repetition, for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s glories are absolute and thus ever fresh, just like those of Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself.

As we offer this twenty-third annual Vyāsa-pūjā book to Śrīla Prabhupāda, we reflect on the miracle of his influence over so many people in this dark age of Kali. From small beginnings, he built his ISKCON into a worldwide movement with the care and concern of a loving father raising a child. Through thousands of letters and lectures, conversations and meticulously dictated translations and purports, Śrīla Prabhupāda taught, chastised, encouraged, and inspired millions, both inside and outside the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. And through his words, recorded in books and on tapes, he still makes his precious association available to whoever wants it. Certainly this Vyāsa-pūjā book is a testament to his living presence in the hearts of his followers. As Śrīla Prabhupāda wrote about his own spiritual master in his dedication of the first volume of *Śrīmad Bhāgavatam,* “He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.”

Draviḍa dāsa  
North American BBT

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Ātma-tattva dāsa

“Since Vyasadeva is the original *guru* (spiritual master) of all who follow the Vedic principles, worship of the spiritual master is called Vyāsa-pūjā.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, ādi-līlā* 17.16 purport)

Śrīla Vyasadeva blessed this world with his *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the natural commentary on the *Vedanta-sutra* and the conclusive essence of all knowledge contained in the vast ocean of Vedic literature. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* instructs that the *yuga-dharma* of this age of Kali is the chanting of the holy names of Kṛṣṇa:

*Kṛte yad dhyāyate viṣṇuṁ treatāyāṁ yajato makhaiḥ*  
*Dvāpare praicaryāyāṁ kalau tad dhari-kīrtanāt*

**“Whatever result was obtained in Satya-yuga by meditating on Viṣṇu, in Tretā-yuga by performing sacrifices, and in Dvāpara-yuga by serving the Lord’s lotus feet can be obtained in Kali-yuga simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra.” (Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **12.3.52)**

Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare, Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare, is the surest path to perfection and the cream of all the knowledge taught in the *Vedas* and complied by Śrīla Vyasadeva.

How should this chanting be done? This is also mentioned in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.5.32):

*Kṛṣṇa-varṇaṁ tviṣākṛṣṇaṁ sāñgopāñgāstra-pārṣadam*  
*Yajñaiḥ saṅkīrtana-prāyair yajanti hi su-medhasaḥ*

**“In the age of Kali, intelligent persons perform congregational chanting to worship the incarnation of Godhead who constantly sings the names of Kṛṣṇa. Although His complexion is not blackish, He is Kṛṣṇa Himself. He is accompanied by His associates, servants, weapons, and confidential companions.”**

This nonblackish incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa is Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the *yuga-avatāra.* He inaugurated the *saṅkīrtana* movement during His manifested pastimes on this planet and predicted that in every town and village His name would be propagated.

In the *Bhaviṣya Purāṇa, Pratisarga Parva,* Lord Jagannatha makes this prediction: ”In the fearful age of Kali, *mlecchas* born in many countries will become cultured by attaining the brahminical standards. They will wear *śikhās* and  *brāhmaṇa* threads, read *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and worship Me by performing the sacrifice of congregational chanting, as enunciated by the protector of Śacī.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda fulfilled all these prophecies by founding and sustaining his International Society for Krishna Consciousness (ISKCON). Therefore, as the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, the society that will usher in the golden age of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, Śrīla Prabhupāda has gloriously fulfilled all the desires of the previous *acharyas* in all the *sampradāyas* and has proved that he is the mercy incarnation of Śrīla Vyasadeva. It is thus most fitting that throughout the world his appearance day is celebrated as Vyāsa-pūjā.

In this way the great Vaisnava tradition of Vyāsa-pūjā is being carried on in the ISKCON society. It is essential for every sincere follower of Śrīla Prabhupāda to formally observe this occasion. As Śrīla Prabhupāda himself described the significance of this day, “You cannot surpass the spiritual master. If you think you have become so learned and advanced that now you can avoid the spiritual master and understand Kṛṣṇa, that is bogus…….We should always remember, *yasya prasādad bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi:* Only by the grace of the spiritual master can we achieve the grace, or mercy, of Kṛṣṇa. This is the meaning of this Vyāsa-pūjā-it is an offering of obeisances to the *paramparā* system.”

Thus Śrīla Prabhupāda, the representative of Śrīla Vyasadeva, is worshiped on his appearance day, and this worship passes up through the disciplic succession to Śrīla Vyasadeva himself. This is how the Vaisnava school understands the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā.

Ātma-tattva dāsa  
Māyāpura Gurukula Instructor

## Vyāsa-Pūjā homages from the G.B.C.

## Agrāṇi Swami

*Nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya Kṛṣṇa-presthaya bhū-tale*  
*Śrīmate bhaktivedanta-svamin iti namine*

*Namas te sarasvate deve gaura-vani-pracarine*  
*Nirvisesa-sunyavadi-pascatya-desa-tarine*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances on the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on this ninety-fourth anniversary of your auspicious appearance within this material world.

Being very poor and lowly, I have nothing to offer you on this blessed occasion except unlimited thanks for your being so kind and tolerant, for had you been otherwise, what would my position be? You once said that although your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura, was very strict, you were ninety per cent lenient. Caitanya Mahāprabhu was very strict. He banished Junior Haridāsa from His association for a minor offence. Nityānanda Prabhu, though, was very lenient, granting forgiveness and mercy even to the sinful Jagai and Madhai.

As a perfect representative of Lord Nityānanda, the original spiritual master, you have granted mercy to hundreds and thousands of Jagāis and Mādhāis, my foolish self so fortunately being among them. That we have come to the shelter of your lotus feet is your causeless mercy alone. No one can estimate the extant of your kindness upon the world. You have given the greatest boons, the benediction moon of *Śrī-Kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtana.* By your mercy the whole world has become ecstatic.

Among your jubilant disciples and followers, however, I alone am unhappy. Not that your mercy has somehow eluded me, but unfortunate as I am, I simply cannot take advantage of it. Unlike Jagāi and Madhāi, who, after being pardoned by Śrī Caitanya and Nityānanda Prabhu, gave up all sinful activity and completely rectified themselves, I remain hopelessly fallen and unrectified. This great misfortune, a result of my insincerity, is causing me tremendous pain and anguish. Since I am a clever cheat and a great pretender, people sometimes have a different impression of me, but actually I am a great fool and of absolutely no value to your glorious mission of distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

Despite my fallen condition, you do not reject me. This, however, causes me even greater lamentation. I think there is no more unbearable pain than knowing I do not meet your expectations and am thus disappointing to you. And what’s worse is that I am at a loss as to what to do. Although I find it increasingly more difficult to bear material existence, I am powerless to help myself. In this helpless condition I can only hope and pray for your mercy.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am your fallen son. Please pick me up from this deplorable condition and forgive me for my many offenses and foolish mistakes. Kindly always protect and maintain me, and no matter what, never let me leave the shelter of your lotus feet. Actually, the only comfort and happiness this tired soul has ever known has been in your service to your *saṅkīrtana*

Movement. If I were to lost that, I will have lost everything. I admit that my mind is obsessed with all kinds of nonsensical, unwanted desires, but I know that these are external, a result of many lifetimes of material conditioning. Within the inner chamber of my covered heart lies my real yearning, and that is to assist you in distributing the holy name for the benefit of all the suffering living entities of the world. I beg to remain

*Your eternal servant,*  
Agrāṇi Swami

## Badrīnārāyana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam (*11.2.28-30*)* contains the prayers that Mahāraja Nimi offered when he saw the nine Yogendras. With a little adjustment from plural to singular, these verses apply perfectly to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda:

“I think that you must be a direct associate of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is famous as the enemy of the demon Madhu. Indeed, the pure devotees of Lord Viṣṇu wander throughout the universe not for their personal, selfish interests, but to purify all the conditioned souls.

“For the conditioned souls, the human body is most difficult to achieve, and it can be lost at any moment. But I think that even those who have achieved human life rarely gain the association of a pure devotee, who is dear to the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha.

“Therefore, O completely sinless one, I ask you to kindly tell me what the supreme good is. After all, even half a moment’s association with a pure devotee within this world of birth and death is a priceless treasure for any man.”

You once told the story of Dr. Beecham’s Powder. It seems there was a British doctor who owned a pharmacy in Calcutta. He had invented a digestive powder that worked wonders on indigestion and a number of other ailments; but no matter how he tried, it just wouldn’t sell. He gave up and years later moved back to London, where he opened a small corner chemist shop.

One day, an old lady came in and asked, “Do you sell Dr. Beecham’s Powder? Her husband had been stationed in India, and she had tried it long ago. She began to go on and on extolling the glories of Beecham’s Powder and complaining about how she couldn’t find it anywhere. Dr. Beecham, by this time an old man himself, became so excited that someone finally wanted his powder that he died of a heart attack on the spot.

Your point was that despite all the inevitable struggles and setbacks in preaching, we should be eager and satisfied to find even one person who wants to hear about Kṛṣṇa. You describe in the

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* how just as the *karmīs* are always disturbed by the alluring call of Māyā, the devotees are also disturbed by the cries of the conditioned souls being crushed by the material nature.

Once a devotee found you looking off the veranda at Māyāpura, watching a poor woman and her child pick through the abandoned leaf plates in search of a morsel of *prasādam.* The devotee commented, “Prabhupāda, sometimes I feel so sorry for these people.” With a penetrating look of overwhelming compassion, you said, ‘Why just sometimes?”

Now my heart is full of desires for my own comfort and concern. I pray that someday I may develop even a drop of your compassion, determination, and enthusiasm to preach. Then I will become a useful servant of Your Divine Grace. Your mercy is all I am made of.

*Trying to be your servant,*  
Badrīnārāyaṇa dāsa

## Bhakti-bhūṣaṇa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your holy lotus feet.

When I think of you, it is especially one quality of yours that strikes me most. It was your total and absolute faith, which was the expression of deep, genuine love for your Guru Mahāraja and Kṛṣṇa. This faith made you stand out like a giant in comparison to all other Vaiṣṇavas. It made you bold and fearless. You would not tolerate any concepts which were contrary to the conclusions of the ś*astras* and the previous *acharyas.* By your strong, uncompromising preaching, Kṛṣṇa consciousness became an irrevocable fact even in the most remote corners of the world. You showed us how to surrender-how to give up all cares for material security and just completely depend on Kṛṣṇa. It was his faith that made you leave your family, the shelter of Vṛndāvana, and finally India. And it was due to this firm faith that the teachings of Lord Caitanya came to the West, as predicted in the *śastras.* Others had already given up and declared this prediction a myth. Despite the unlimited obstacles, you would never waver in your determination to push your preaching mission on and on to newer heights, beyond all expectations. You showed the world that the word *impossible* exists only in the dictionaries of fools and rascals. You were the perfect *ācārya,* showing us every aspect of Kṛṣṇa conscious life by your own example. You were a real hero, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Nobody had ever done anything like this before in the history of mankind, and nobody will ever do it again.

You injected this faith into the hearts of your followers and made them act most wonderfully, assisting you in your mission. This faith still keeps your movement together and is spreading more and more, and it will also inspire future generations to take up devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. All we have to do is keep your association available to the world, especially through the powerful medium of your books.

You kindly picked me up from hellish life, Śrīla Prabhupāda. If it weren’t for you, I would still be suffering in the material world or most probably, be already completely destroyed. On this most auspicious day of your appearance, I beg you to grant me a drop of the faith you had so I never become discouraged or deviated from your lotus feet. Please let me always be guided by your divine instructions and those who have strong faith in them. In this way let me be determined in the execution of my duties and also inspire others to develop the same faith.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Bhakti-bhūṣaṇa Swami

## Bhakti Charu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

My spiritual life is completely dependent upon my love for you. When my love for you becomes perfect and complete, then only will I be able to enter into the abode of Kṛṣṇa. But often I wonder whether my love for you is increasing or decreasing. When I first started to read your books, I experienced a deep love for you. As I joined ISKCON, hearing about your transcendental glories from your devotees, that love increased. When I first saw you in Prayag during the Kumbh-mela, I realized for the first time what love actually means.

When, in Māyāpura, you asked me to become your secretary and move into the room next to yours, I felt that this was the perfection of my existence. When you chastised me for my countless mistakes, I experienced tears of joy. And when you praised me for whatever little I did for you, my heart melted in sublime submission and I wanted to wash your feet with my tears.

Our lives revolved around you during those days in Vṛndāvana. With your divine power, you made a fountain of love flow from our barren hearts, and we were ready to offer our lives to you. When you were preparing to go back to the spiritual sky, so many devotees silently prayed to Kṛṣṇa to let you stay here in exchange for their lives. But Kṛṣṇa was missing your association so much that despite all our prayers He took you back to the spiritual sky.

Damodar Prasad Sastri, the Āyurvedic doctor who was treating you, told me not to cry. He said, “Śrīla Prabhupāda is now going back to Kṛṣṇa; if you cry, your tears will make his journey back to Godhead difficult.” Your sister, our Pisima, was sitting next to your bed, and tears came streaming down from her eyes as if her heart had broken. Maṇihara Prabhu started to sob, and I repeated the words of Damodar Prasad: ”Please do not cry. Śrīla Prabhupāda is now going back to Kṛṣṇa. Let us not hinder his journey back to the spiritual sky with our tears.” Then I added, “We have our whole life to cry for him.”

Although I thought that I would cry throughout my entire life, my desertlike heart could not shed any tears for a long time. Then one day, as I was sitting in front of your *samādhi,* which was then still small room with a simple wooden door, all of a sudden I realized that I wouldn’t be able to put my arm around your waist and help you walk, I wouldn’t be able to sit in front of you while you ate the *prasadam* I had cooked for you, I wouldn’t be able to give you the medicines that Vanamali Kaviraj so meticulously taught me to prepare, I wouldn’t be able to turn you on your side after you had lain on your back for such a long time that it had become painful for you. Then I realized that I had made a mistake: when you entered into your *samadhi* and I went down to help you, I should have stayed there with you. I should have gone with you to serve you forever. But instead I came out to maintain my miserable body. I realized that I missed the train to go with you to the spiritual sky.

When you arrived in Vṛndāvana from Hṛṣīkeśa, you informed the devotees that you had come to give up your body. Hearing that, everyone started to cry, but you reminded them that the spirit soul never dies. Now I realize that it was a mistake to limit you to your body. Now I can see that you are everywhere, in everything in ISKCON.

In the beginning, at the neophyte stage, I became attached to you without realizing that your ISKCON is nondifferent from you. Now, by your mercy, I am realizing that ISKCON is your body and that you are its soul. ISKCON is the temple, and you are the deity. Although your physical form disappeared from my material vision, you are still present in front of me as your ISKCON.

Kṛṣṇa appeared as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to distribute *Kṛṣṇa-prema,* the most precious gift of the spiritual sky. Therefore He is known as the most munificent incarnation. He predicted that the *saṅkīrtana* movement would spread throughout the world, to every town and village. The most munificent incarnation left that even more munificent mission to be accomplished by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and ISKCON is the embodiment of that most rare mercy.

As a neophyte devotee, I wanted to go back to the spiritual sky with you, but now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to stay here, life after life, to serve your ISKCON with my body, mind, and words. Please bless me that I can render that service properly.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bhakti Charu Swami

## Bhaktisvarupa Dāmodara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances to my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.”**

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedānta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day, kindly bless me so that I can carry out your divine order. As your centennial appearance celebration nears, we are making a humble attempt to explain the science of the self and the science of *bhāgavata* culture, as you taught them to us, to the intellectual community of the world. Fortunately, by your divine blessings you have sent some very capable devotees who can carry out your divine mission very nicely. I am quite confident that Your Divine Grace is extremely pleased with all of them. By your divine blessings, the BBT trustees are fully engineering the mechanisms of your Bhaktivedānta Institute. We are most grateful to all of them for their support, which is the proof that your divine instructions will always be fulfilled.

On this most important day, please allow me to reinforce my commitment to serve your divine order.

All glories to Your Divine Grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servant  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti-Tirtha Swami

His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda – Our Śrīla Vyāsadeva

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like Śrīla Vyasadeva because you so painstakingly made the highest teachings available in the most common denominator. As the years go by we all can appreciate much more what great, grand, glorious, splendid, wonderful, magnanimous treasures you gave us to imbibe and to distribute indiscriminately to the world.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like Śrīla Vyasadeva because you so painstakingly allowed us to distribute your books, which contain the greatest secrets, so as to give everyone-every man, woman and child on this planet- a chance to contact the Absolute Truth.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like Śrīla Vyasadeva because you so painstakingly helped import the holy name of the Lord from the spiritual world down to the material universes, getting all of your disciples to vow to chant at least sixteen rounds of *japa* every day for the rest of our lives.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like Śrīla Vyasadeva because you so painstakingly convinced us of the importance of *harināma,* spreading the holy names literally to every town and village and instructing everyone to chant and be happy and take *bhāgavata-prasādam.*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like Śrīla Vyasadeva because you so painstakingly allowed us to read and hear about the dynamic activities of the six Gosvāmis and their assistants, even though we were so full of doubt and enviousness.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like Śrīla Vyasadeva because you so painstakingly taught us how to serve the Deities with great opulence and devotion, thus allowing us to appreciate how the Godhead is personal, despite our devastating impersonal backgrounds.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like Śrīla Vyasadeva because you so painstakingly wrote about and spoke on the importance of *sādhu-saṅga.* You were the perfect *sādhu* who so gracefully gave everyone his association. All of us were blessed who heard from you or even gazed upon your transcendental from, even though we were offenders.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like Śrīla Vyasadeva because you are the Jagat Guru, who came to this world with such a high commission that it will take lifetimes before we and the rest of the world will be able to appreciate the great, grand, glorious, splendid, wonderful, magnanimous treasures you gave us to imbibe and to distribute to the world indiscriminately.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like Śrīla Vyasadeva, you have given the perfect example and made the highest teachings available in the most common denominator. We who are such common men will fall behind if we don’t constantly remind ourselves about the exaltedness of you and your contributions to mankind. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us imbibe the *essence* of your life and mission, for you have given us so much that it is now our turn to give back to you. Our stock is very low, but by your divine intervention we can acquire something worthy to give back to you. We owe you so much; we will always be indebted. Thank you for being like Śrīla Vyāsadeva- compassionate and so expert in bringing down the highest teachings for the benefit of all mankind. We are eternally indebted.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Your big mission was born from the compassionate desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. It was preserved, nurtured, and expanded by great *acharyas* as Rūpa Gosvāmi, Sanātana Gosvāmi, Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmi, Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmi, Jīva Gosvāmi, Gopāla Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmi, Narottama dāsa Thākura, Viśvanātha Cakravarti Thākura, Kedarnath Datta, carried and conceptualized if for modern times. Great achievements from the lion *guru,* his son, cleared all obstacles to expanding Mahāprabhu’s mercy worldwide. All glories to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda and his vision of powerful *sannyāsīs* preaching through Gauḍīya Maths!

And you, our master–golden-hearted, fearless, empowered, fully surrendered, pure Prabhupāda-finally did it all by yourself, on time, by the mercy and strength of the disciplic succession.

Who can still argue that you are not “Prabhupāda,” at whose feet all masters fall? Your mission, Mahāprabhu’s mission, thrives alive throughout the world, still.

“I have done my part,” you said. And that “part” is huge, dear Prabhupāda, and earns you well-deserved veneration, dedication, admiration, and love from all.

Here is my understanding of my part, as your servant:

To try in all circumstances to serve Kṛṣṇa as best I can, humbly, as a servant, based on your order.

To surrender and persevere. To pass your test of faithful service, knowing that even I whose desire to become Kṛṣṇa’s devotee is but weak, still exist fully within His mercy.

I must stop trying to be a big devotee. Damn being noticed by all. I aspire to become your humble, loyal, and devoted servant, eternally, and through serving you, to serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Syāmasundara in the spiritual world.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Bhūrijana dāsa

## Gaura Govinda Swami

*Om ajnana-timirandhasya jnananjana-salakaya*  
*Caksur unmilitam yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namah*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of wisdom.”**

O my beloved Gurudeva, Śrīla Prabhupāda, O eternal associate of Lord Jagannātha, on this auspicious occasion of the sacred appearance day of Your Divine Grace, kindly accept millions of prostrated *daṇḍavat praṇāmas* from this offender at the holy dust of your divine lotus feet. O lord, may you grant a drop of causeless mercy on this poor sinful soul out of the ocean of your ever-soothing divine blessings.

Life after life may I remain in prostration at your divine lotus feet, O my eternal spiritual master, as the sacred Mahodadhi (the Bay of Bengal at Śrī Jagannātha Puri) has since time immemorial been unfolding itself in thousands of prostrations at the divine lotus feet of the Supreme Lord, Jagannatha.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, how long shall I go on crying and crying and crying to get a glimpse for your divinity? This sinful soul has been traveling from millions of lives to millions of lives, and again millions of lives, but yet is as thirsty as a *cāṭaka* bird eager to quench his long-suffered spiritual thirst. (A *cāṭaka* will remain thirsty for months together waiting to quench its thirst with the water showering from a cloud.)

O my worshipable beloved spiritual master, how grateful is humanity for this day, when out of your causeless mercy Your Divine Grace appeared in order to reclaim thousands of your servants and serious seekers of your spiritual association and thus surcharge this otherwise morbid material world with pure, transcendental love of Godhead!

The world had become a dark tunnel of *pāpa* (sins), and Your Divine Grace lighted the torch of divinity and flooded this mother earth with ever-nectarean *Kṛṣṇa-prema.*

O Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa, *he parāma-puruṣa,* save us from the *adharma* sweeping across mother earth. O Lord, when shall Thee *sambhavāmi* to *vināśāya duṣ\\krtam and paritrānāya sādhūnām?*

Your enlightened *amṛtasya-putra,* His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedānta Swami Prabhupāda, after playing the role of distributor of the nectar of pure *Kṛṣṇa-prema,* has gone back to Your divine lap, O Lord.

O Lord’s dear child, Śrīla Gurudeva, Your Divine Grace came, saw and conquered the city of Bhubaneswar and thus once again flooded this temple city with pure *bhāgavata-prema.* But, O Gurudeva, the temple that Your Divine Grace visualized as the great center of worship and brilliant promoter of Mahāprabhu‘s *saṅkīrtana* movement has not yet been completed. O dear Gurudeva, I am crying here-we are crying here- lest our beloved Gurudeva has taken away the umbrella of his divine blessings form above our head.

O Gurudeva, please tell us what our sin is that we earthly worms could not complete this last-founded temple project of Your Divine Grace and start the *udaṇḍa saṅkīrtana* of Their Lordships. O Guru-deva, please bless us, bless all your children around the world, to come and complete this temple as visualized by Your Divine Grace and start Kṛṣṇa Gātha (the glory of Śrī Kṛṣṇa) in every month of the year, every day of the month, every hour of the day, every minute of the hour, and every second of the minute.

Once again offering my millions of prostrated *daṇḍavat praṇāms* at the holy dust of your divine lotus feet, I beg to remain,

*Your unworthy but eternal servant,*  
Gaura Govinda Swami

## Gopala Kṛṣṇa Goswami

On this most revered and auspicious day, I beg to offer my most humble obeisances at the divine lotus feet of my eternal father and guide, Oṁ Vishnupada Parāmahāmsa Parivrajakācārya Aṣṭottra-śata Śrī Śrīmad Abhay Charanaravinda Bhaktivedānta Swami Mahāraja, founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and the Bhaktivedānta Book Trust.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the beloved disciple of your Guru Mahāraja. In dedicating the First Canto to your Guru Mahāraja you stated, “He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.” Only by remaining faithful to your instructions can I develop attachment to your lotus feet and be fixed on the platform of devotional service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the real spiritual master. Because of your purity and brilliance, you have been blessed with the most exalted position on this planet. Because of your dedication to the words of your Guru Mahāraja and the Supreme Lord, you have become the first Vaisnava World ācārya on this planet. Only you could be entrusted to fulfill Lord Caitanya’s prediction that the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* would be chanted in every town and village. Due to your tireless efforts, there is not a single country on this planet where the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* is not being chanted or where your books are not being read.

Your transcendental realizations have been transmitted through your authoritative Bhaktivedānta purports, which will guide humanity for the next thousand years. The potency of your words is the same as that of the Supreme Lord. You are His bona fide representative. Your books are real ”timeless bombs.” They can explode at any moment. You once remarked that as long as book distribution goes on, you will live forever. Through your purports, you will continue preaching to the conditioned souls who have forgotten the real business of human life. Just as the *Vedas* are called a desire tree, you were also a desire tree. Everyone who ever talked to you walked away satisfied. Following the tradition of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you converted so many Māyāvadis to the path of devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Thākura Bhaktivinoda has said that a Vaiṣṇava is recognized by his preaching work. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have lifted an unlimited number of *mlecchas* like myself to the elevated standard of devotional service. While preaching in India, I meet so many people who recall the wonderful way you inspired them to serve your beloved Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the real *parāmahāmsa.* You had complete faith in the instructions of your spiritual master. You struggled for twenty-years to establish the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement on this planet in order to save humanity from going to hell. By your personal example, you showed how a devotee should always be involved in reducing the suffering of the living entities. In the face of all kinds of adversity, and even when the doctors advised you to rest, you never stopped preaching.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you exhibited all the twenty-six qualities of a devotee. You were ever merciful, humble, truthful, equal to all, faultless, magnanimous, mild, and clean. You did not have any material possessions. Whatever donations you received were used to print books and build projects to propagate Lord Caitanya’s mission. You performed welfare work for everyone. You were always peaceful. After an Indian man insulted you verbally at your Montreal lecture, you remained undisturbed, or *sānta.* You were completely surrendered to Kṛṣṇa and desireless. You were indifferent to material acquisitions and fixed in devotional service. You had complete control over the six bad qualities. You ate as much as required to keep body and soul together. You were not inebriated. You were always respectful, grave, compassionate, and without false prestige. You were friendly, poetic, expert, and silent.

The devotees of Lord Caitanya are likened to clouds pouring forth water that nourishes the fruit of love of God. As a sincere follower of Lord Caitanya, you have instructed your followers to increase the number of Vaiṣṇavas and to preach the importance of human life throughout the world. At present the eternal occupation of most of the population is being misused. You have put into our hands the business of helping saintly persons like yourself reestablish the principles of religion. You have ordered us to render the greatest service to the Lord: to infuse devotional service into the hearts of the conditioned souls so that they may be released from the bondage of conditioned life.

In the *ādi-līlā* of the *Caitanya-caritām,* it is described how the spiritual master of Lord Caitanya said to Him, “My dear child, continue dancing, chanting, and performing *saṅkīrtana* in association with devotees. Furthermore, go out and preach the value of chanting *Kṛṣṇa-nama,* for by this process You will be able to deliver all fallen souls. “ Śrīla Prabhupāda, I know this is also your desire. On this auspicious day I beg for your causeless mercy so that I may be fixed in executing your divine instructions and helping to propagate the *saṅkīrtana* movement. One hundred eight glories to the wandering mendicant and topmost swanlike devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your eternal servant,*  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Guru Gaurāṅga dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this most auspicious day, we joyfully gather to celebrate your appearance among us as Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee and our eternal spiritual master.

Unlike ordinary living entities who must take birth in this material world, you appeared of your own volition to spread Lord Caitanya’s sacred mission. Convinced that the spiritual rehabilitation of the world is assured only by the mercy of the disciplic succession, you sought out the most fallen, pitiful souls. You patiently guided and trained us how to serve the lotus feet of the Lord. No effort was too strenuous, no lesson unworthy. Whatever good there is in us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is simply the fruit of the endeavor you made.

Although you are not physically with us, your transcendental presence is keenly felt by your followers. You continue through the vivid memory of your example. Please bless us with a fraction of your determination and enthusiasm to serve Śrī Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master. Our only desire is to remain engaged in your devotional service.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Guru Gaurāṅga dāsa

## Girirāja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories, all glories to Your Divine Grace.

We have heard that the service of the spiritual master is the all-in-all for the disciple. Actually it is so. There is no doubt about it. But for some of us disciples who are less intelligent, direct service to the spiritual master is not easy. We need to be engaged by your dear and expert disciples, who know well how to please you. We long to serve in their association. So on this most auspicious occasion, we pray that by your causeless mercy we may be placed under the care and guidance of devotees who can engage our energies expertly in your dearmost projects. This would be your greatest favor to us now.

Thanking you for everything, I beg to remain

*Your eternal servant,*  
Girirāja Swami

## Guru-prasāda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet, which are giving refute to countless conditioned souls. Once again I shall try to offer some words of praise, which amount to less than an atom of your fathomless glories.

As we sat in your room at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple this year, we reflected on how you had humbly sat there, working tirelessly to inaugurate the *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world. Thousands passed by that tiny cubicle, unaware that the most important spiritual revolution was unfolding in their presence. In the atomic age we understand that a huge amount of energy can be released from a tiny source. But the most amazing reaction began in this room: the unleashing of Kṛṣṇa’s internal energy in the form of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* We could imagine how one day world leaders, politicians, intellectuals, and other influential persons will flock to that sacred place and stand in long lines to pay homage to this monument. They will solemnly bow and pray in the room where you enacted your final manifest pastimes.

Please allow me to be an instrument in spreading your glorious activities throughout the world. It is my duty to help everyone get an opportunity to receive your blessings, which have been so mercifully bestowed upon everyone. They can come and visit your Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir and prostrate themselves before your *samādhi.*

I can only marvel at how much more I have to discover about your transcendental personality. Every moment you allow me to participate in this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is a new chapter in the ever-manifesting and greatest adventure that can possibly be experienced. The scene is the most volatile imaginable, revealing the purest, most intimate truth in the most degraded age and making it palatable and desirable.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for such infinite mercy. Please excuse my unwillingness to accept it. I’ll try my best to become a more worthy recipient.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Guru-prasāda Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you who are the savior of the fallen souls in Kaliyuga. You have kindly come to the Western countries and delivered us from the pit of impersonalism and voidism.

The most significant feature in my remembrance of you is the simple fact that you saved me. I was wandering for thousands of years through the network of *māyā’s* illusions without any hope of breaking free from her clutches. Who was I, simply an insignificant speck in the ocean of conditioned souls? But you made something out of me by gently lifting me up from that ocean.

You once compared the chances of being saved by the spiritual master to the chances of a turtle in the ocean sticking its head through a hole in a piece of floating wood. The chances of this happening are very slim, and similarly the chances of a conditioned soul finding shelter under the lotus feet of a spiritual master are equally slim. Who can estimate the boundless grace that you have bestowed upon the world by your coming to the Western countries and delivering us from the stinking pit of material existence? Your Divine Grace is immeasurable and incomparable to anything I have ever experienced in this material world. All I know is that you saved me, and that is worth the world to me.

I have forgotten most of the pastimes that you have performed whole on this planet. The past is beginning to merge into a cloud of forgetfulness, and I am feeling very sorry that the divine images of your activities are becoming lost to my sight. Time causes one to forget so many things, but I can never forget how you picked me up from the ocean of birth and death and situated me as a humble servant at your lotus feet. This fact will remain with me always and will keep me strictly in line with your desires and plans.

I may have forgotten some details here and there, but I can never forget that you wanted your books printed in every language of the world. I can never forget that day in Sanand, Gujerat, when you were sitting on the roof of the palace of the king, getting your daily massage, and you turned to me and said, “Make sure that all my books are published in every language of the world.” This statement entered my heart like a golden arrow and has directed my life ever since. Hand in hand with producing these books is selling them. When I showed you some new books in Vṛndāvana in June of 1977, just before you left this world, the first thing you asked me was, ”Can you sell them? “ When I said yes, you immediately replied, “Then reprint them and sell them some more.” Your main goal was to produce and distribute these transcendental books, and this has become my main goal in life as well.

I must admit that I am fairly useless at all other forms of spiritual activity. I am not attracted to austerities and penances like some of my Godbrothers are. For me to follow the strict Ekādaśī is out of the question. Neither am I ready to renounce the world in favor of entering into the divine pastimes of the Supreme Lord and His associates. I am not fit for such exalted activities. I think I can do only one thing-print and distribute your books- and I pray to you that you will be satisfied with that activity and grant me *kṛpa-siddhi,* the honorary degree enabling me to go back, back to Godhead, directly after finishing publishing all your books in as many languages as I can in this life.

**Books**

Books.  
Not just any books  
But divine books,  
Transcendental books.  
Living books for living people.

Books-  
They’re even for crooks.  
We’re all crooks  
Stealing from Kṛṣṇa;  
Therefore these books-  
Waking us up,  
Shaking us up,  
Making us come up to the highest platform.

Books.  
Prabhupāda’s books.  
Delivered to all dark nooks  
All over the world.  
Look at these books!  
These are the hooks  
Catching you by the heart,  
The head and the pocket.  
Picking you up and moving you over,  
Making you wonder,  
Making you see,  
Making you feel,  
That love of the Lord,  
That love the devotee feels inside.  
And all of this comes from the  
Books.

Prabhupāda’s books.  
Contamination cooks and boils away.  
These books are like the sun  
Giving light and heat,  
Warming our hearts,  
Moving our feet,  
Moving us along the path of perfection,  
Making us realize with great introspection  
That books are the basis,  
The basis of all,  
Spiritual life is the final call.  
For books and books and books and so on  
Making me wake up and move right along.

Books.  
Prabhupāda’s books.  
Wonderful books  
Like little fish hooks.  
Catching our collars,  
Lifting our feet,  
Stopping our karma,  
Our karmic retreat.  
Books and books and books and so on.  
O Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda,  
You wrote so sublime,  
A miracle chime,  
Māyā stopped on a dime,  
Karma limited to mime,  
Spirit will climb,  
Life now has rhyme,  
And everything’s fine,  
‘cause you wrote so sublime,  
your books for all time.  
And books and books and books and so on.  
What need to say more?  
Good future’s in store.  
Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes galore.  
Your message implores me  
Picks me up and sends me on  
To Goloka Vrindāvan,  
The Lord’s holy dhām.

Books.  
Prabhupāda’s books.  
I want to make books,  
And books and books and books and so on.  
Prabhupāda save me  
Save me, a fallen, Your Divine Grace!

Harikeśa Swami

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you and to the *saṅkīrtana* mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, which you have preached and expanded so perfectly all over the world.

On the material plane, small children naturally worship their father, but as they grow up, they manifest their own desires and come to pursue their own path. But the blessed child of a learned father learns to deeply love, and to follow, his worthy father.

How fortunate we are to have you as our eternal father, for your spiritual qualities truly have no limit. There is no end to your purity and depth, your simple honesty that springs mightily from the fully realized vision of an eternally liberated soul.

Let us, your spiritual children, boldly prove to the world that you are the Supreme Lord’s own and dearest agent, the merciful rain cloud come to extinguish the blazing fire of birth and death that engulfs the stupid world.

If only, by your kindness, we may transcend our internal conflicts and again become lions in the chase, and not merely at home, relentlessly stroking the great *mṛdaṅga* till its divine booming tones save every innocent soul, and put the demonic ones to flight.

As I grow older, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I see more and more that all I could ever desire is to follow your footsteps. All the qualities I aspire for, I see in you, and the solution to all my problems is to listen seriously to your instructions and obey them. You are a perfect person, an ideal person, a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Praying to be useful in your mission, I remain

*Your servant forever,*  
Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jagadīśa Goswami

**Prabhupāda, You Are the Lord of My Life**

Prabhupāda, you are the lord of my life.  
Please guide me always to serve Kṛṣṇa’s plan.  
That which is mine, like mind, body, and soul,  
Belong now to you and are yours to command.  
I have no value but that which you give.  
Give me your order, for I am your man.  
Cleaning the dishes or preaching to kings,  
I am prepared: if you want, then I can.  
Everyone here is searching for love,  
Wandering, hoping, but finding just sand.  
You have presented the genuine gold,  
Giving them life by extending your hand.  
How many years will it take till I know  
How to fulfill your most wonderful plan,  
How to receive and distribute your love,  
Giving all access to Lord Kṛṣṇa’s land?

*Your servant,*  
Jagadīsa Goswami

## Kavicandra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Just as Nārada Muni initiated Mṛgāri the hunter and instructed him just to depend upon Kṛṣṇa, you initiated thousands like myself and saved us from all sinful habits. You are the perfect follower of Nārada Muni and therefore the perfect representative of the Brahma-Madhva-Gaudīya Sampradāya. I only pray that I may have as much faith as Mrgāri in following the order of my spiritual master.

You gave us everything. By translating and distributing the most important Vaisnava literatures, you presented conclusive knowledge concerning who the bona fide spiritual master is. And you perfectly exhibited superefficient spiritual qualities and behavior. You also gave us all information concerning who the bona fide disciple is. And you also perfectly exhibited the qualities of a perfect disciple of your spiritual master. You kindly accepted us even though we weren’t always qualified. You depended upon the mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to make us qualified. In my case I must still pray that I may become a qualified disciple. You always said, “My only qualification is that I always followed the order of my spiritual master.” You demonstrated the power of that order.

Even now the remembrances of your activities are the most relishable thing for the devotees, both old and new. People always ask me, “Did you meet Prabhupāda?” You are the perfect servant of Kṛṣṇa, so you are also most attractive. You have given us so much to do in order to fulfill your intense desire to see Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement spread all over the world. Simply by recalling how selflessly you worked for that goal, we are able to carry on. So many demons are trying to impede our progress, but you had so many “good fights,” and so we get strength.

Your books are the basis, and they are being distributed and appreciated more and more every day. I remember how pleased you were when someone distributed 108 big books in one day, but recently one devotee distributed over 900 in one day! So especially your books and their distribution are showing the inconceivable, unlimited nature of the Lord. Your “personal ecstasies,” the Bhaktivedānta purports, are your special mercy for the world.

*Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Madhya* 25.122*)* states:

*Sarva-deśa-kāla-daśāya janera kartavaya*  
*Guru-pāśe sei bhakti praṣṭavya śrotavya*

**It is therefore the duty of every man in every country- in every circumstance and at all times- to approach the bona fide spiritual master, question him about devotional service, and listen to him explain the process.”**

Your books make this possible. They are going everywhere to give ś*ikṣā* to everyone. And they give full guidance to make others into spiritual masters. If anyone can follow without deviating, they can also explain the process. So please let us go on remembering your orders and your activities. And let us understand that remembering means following. You started something wonderful, and as long as we keep connected with you, it will continue expanding wonderfully. I fervently pray that I may remain in the association of those who constantly remember and serve you, and that I may someday become their proper servant.

*Your aspiring servant.*  
Kavicandra Swami

## Kīrtirāja dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and your causeless mercy upon the fallen conditioned souls of this material world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, certainly you are the dearmost servant of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Nityānanda Prabhu. Through your ISKCON movement you continue to spread Their mission, for even though you are not physically present with us in your Society, you are certainly spiritually present. This can be seen by the practical result- namely, that your movement is spreading throughout the world and spiritually uplifting those who are fortunate enough to come in contact with it and the message of Lord Caitanya, on which ISKCON is based. Of course, there are many organizations that are also spreading throughout the world, and some of them are becoming very firmly established. But your ISKCON movement is the ONLY organization that is delivering the message of pure love of Godhead. And, by your mercy, one who contacts your Society not only learns about pure love of Godhead but actually experiences pure love of Godhead as it really is.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’ve given us not only the responsibility for becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious ourselves, but also the responsibility for helping others become Kṛṣṇa conscious. The first task is difficult enough in this material world during the age of Kali. And you have certainly said that your mission would be a success if even one of your disciples would become a pure devotee. But we all know that you would not be truly satisfied if only one person became a pure devotee. You want this entire world to be filled up with pure devotees. We all know that only then would you be satisfied. We know that you will never be satisfied until ALL living entities, not just human beings, are delivered back to the lotus feet of your dearmost Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that by your mercy and kindness, I may be able to assist you in some way or other in your mission of delivering the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the long-lost souls suffering in this material world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have said that in this materialistic modern world there is only one thing lacking- Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have nothing in this material world except the faint hope of becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have also said on many occasions that all the material opulences in this world are like so many zeroes-absolutely useless, no matter how many of them one may have. However, if a person adds a one (Kṛṣṇa) in front of all those zeroes, he will be a very rich man. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the one who provides that “one.” I pray that I may become a fit candidate to receive that one from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and that I may also help others receive that one from you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that I may be engaged in your service without end, and I thank you for allowing me to be engaged up till now.

I beg to eternally remain  
*Your servant,*  
Kīrtirāja dāsa

## Loka-śākṣī dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Your glorious movement has been going on for nearly twenty-five years now, and in the near future it will reveal wonderful manifestations. What you are demanding of each and every one of your faithful and surrendered disciples is unalloyed devotion. Otherwise, how could you still guide this movement? But if we do not take this into account and instead go on with our tiny and silly domestic lives, then our link with you will become slackened, and it will not be possible for us to help you in your mission.

Personally I beg you for your causeless mercy so that I can follow strictly all your instructions and do everything demanded of me. In this way I myself can try to become a pure devotee and stop acting according to my own whims. I just pray to be able to render whatever service you ask of me.

*Your most fallen servant,*  
Loka-śākṣī dāsa

## Mukunda Goswami

“The lotus feet of our spiritual master are the only way by which we can obtain pure devotional service.”

*Brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*Guru-Kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-lata-bīja*

After passing through countless universe and struggling through innumerable lifetimes, one who is most fortunate meets a spiritual master who can deliver devotional service. My one and only good fortune is that I was able to meet and somehow accept and serve you as my eternal spiritual master,

“I bow down to his lotus feet with great awe and reverence.”

*Tad viddhi pranipātena paripraśnene sevayā*  
*Upadekṣyanti te jñānaṁ jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ*

Although I am thoroughly polluted, I’m learning to worship you. Gradually weaning myself from the childish familiarity in which I indulged during our early times together, I’m learning to treat you with proper respect. By constantly offering you obeisances and worshipping you daily during *guru-pūjā,* I’m beginning to appreciate your unlimited qualities and my own insignificance. And I can feel my stony heart begin to soften. You once said that the relationship between *guru* and disciple grows and deepens, regardless of changing material bodies. By repeatedly offering your respects, I’m feeling more and more gratitude for your infinite kindness on this fallen soul.

“By the grace one can cross over the ocean of material suffering and obtain the mercy of Kṛṣṇa.”

*Kalim āgatam āñāya kṣetre ‘smin vaisṇave vayam*  
*āsīna dīrgha-satreṇa kath-ayāṁ sakṣaṇā hareḥ*

Just as the sages of Naimiṣāranya proclaimed Sūta Goswami the competent captain of the ship to guide them over the menacing ocean of Kali, you are my only hope of surviving *māyā’s* relentless and calculating attacks as I proceed, under your order, through the age of madness.

“My only wish is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from your mouth.”

One of the six divisions of surrender, as described in the *Caitanya Caritāmṛta (Madhya* 22.100*),* is *ātma-nikṣepa,* to dovetail all of one’s desires with those of the Lord. To regularly hear and live by your teachings (which are non-different from those of the Lord) is purifying my lusty desires, gradually aligning them with those of the Lord. Absorption in your words is my armor against illusion’s weapons.

“Attachment to his lotus feet is the perfection that fulfills all desires.”

*Visayā vinivartante nirāhārasya dehinah*  
*Rasa-varjaṁ raso ‘py asya paraṁ dṛṣṭvā nivartate*

Although desires for material enjoyment assail me at every moment, my aspiration to achieve your constant mercy, your shelter, and your blessings is gradually lifting me out of this material world. I pray that Kṛṣṇa will give me the strength to undeviatingly remember you and to continuously meditate on your lotus feet.

“He opens my darkened eyes and fills my heart with transcendental knowledge.”

*Tene brahma hṛdā.* You repeatedly awaken me when I succumb to *māyā’s* sedatives and give me the knowledge and strength to continue fighting the never-ending war with the material energy. This extraordinary knowledge comprehends spiritual realization and the power to deliver the whole world from misery and delusion.

“He is my Lord, birth after birth.”

You used this simile: relationships in this material world are like the meeting of two straws floating in an ocean. Haphazardly they may touch each another for a fleeting moment, only to separate forever. But you will be with me eternally, just as Lord Paramātmā perpetually resides within the heart of every living being. This is the perfection of all relationships.

“From him ecstatic prema emanates.”

*Mahāprabhoḥ kirtana-nṛtya-gīta*  
*Vāditra-mādyan-manaso rasena*  
*Romāṅca-kampāśru-taranga-bhājo*  
*Vande guroḥ Śrī-caraṇāravindam*

You are always overflowing with transcendental love for Kṛṣṇa, like the powerful Ganga, who flows incessantly from the toe of Viṣṇu into the universe to counteract the myriad sins of the *jivas.* Everything you said, did, and taught us, from Deity worship to bookkeeping, was suffused with intense love of Kṛṣṇa.

“By him ignorance is destroyed.”

*Tad-vāg-visargo janatāgha-viplavo*  
*Yasmin pratī-ślokam abahhavaty api*  
*Nāmāny anantasya yaśo ‘nkitāni yat*  
*Śṛṇvanti gāyanti gṛṇanti sādhavaḥ*

You fracture Kali’s fatal grip on the souls of this age with your devastating dialectic against pseudo-science, counterfeit religion, pointless philosophies, and irresponsible governments. You expose all kinds of cheating and the many alluring dangers that delude humanity and keep it imprisoned in nescience.

The integrity of Yudhisthira, the austerity of Dhruva Mahāraja, the judgement of Yamarāja. Your affection for your devotees is like that of āṣabhadeva for his sons, and your constant instructions are like Lord Kapailadeva’s teachings on devotional service.

Our spiritual master is the ocean of mercy, the friend of the poor, and the lord and master of the devotees.”

Your kindness has no limit. You accept any sincere soul in any condition of life. You are the balm, consolation, and hope of all who suffer, and you control the minds of your devotees.

“O master, please be merciful unto me.”

*Yasya prasād/ad bhagavat-prasādo*  
*Yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*  
*Dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyam*  
*Vande guroḥ Śrī-caraṇāravindam*

This material world is like a vast hospital in which I’m one of the millions of patients, or spiritual casualties suffering form the fever of material enjoyment. My disease is acute, and the cure is slow- only because I’m reluctant to take it. Therefore I always seek and need your blessings.

“Give me the shade of your lotus feet.”

*Saṁsāra-dāvānala-līdha-loka-*  
*Traṇaya kāruṇya ghanāghanatvam*  
*Prāptasya kalyāṇa guṇārnavasya*  
*Vande guroḥ Śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Please allow me to take shelter of your holy feet and thus get the only possible relief from the scorching heat of material existence.

“Your fame is spread all over the three worlds.”

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
*When thou art living still in sound.*  
*The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try*  
*To spread the holy name around!*

Once you said that a devotee of Kṛṣṇa is famous all over the world. To establish your mission (implanting pure knowledge of Kṛṣṇa in this world), you performed rigorous austerities, overcame incredible obstacles, and threw down the gauntlet before *māyā’s* legions. For

“The Vedic literatures sing of his character.”

*āhus tvāṁ ṛṣayaḥ sarve devarṣir nāradas thatā*  
*Asito devalo vyāsaḥ svayaṁ caiva bravīṣi me*

You fully embody all twenty-six qualifications of a devotee. You possess the tolerance of Prahlāda Mahāraja, the courage of Arjuna. Once you said that a devotee of Kṛṣṇa is famous all over the world. To establish your mission (implanting pure knowledge of Kṛṣṇa in this world), you performed rigorous austerities, overcame incredible obstacles, and threw down the gauntlet before *māyā’s* legions. For this you are known and glorified throughout the universe.

*Your servant,*  
Mukunda Goswami

## Nirañjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

I could feel somewhat justified for this last-minute Vyāsa-pūjā offering if I had been so fully absorbed in preaching that I didn’t get sufficient time to write one until now. But, unfortunately, I can’t use that excuse with a clear consciousness. The fact is that I’ve had numerous opportunities to sit down and write an offering just befitting your exalted character, but the real problem is that I’m incapable of doing so for that very reason- I’m just not sufficiently absorbed in preaching. This is my lamentation. If only I could imbibe a tiny fraction of your enthusiasm for preaching, I know that I could produce volumes of ever-fresh realizations praising your divine qualities within a very short time.

You could sit down at a moment’s notice and immediately begin expounding the glories of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, because your consciousness was always fixed on his instructions to preach the message of Lord Caitanya all over the world. Therefore every word that you spoke, and every act that you performed, was indirectly a glorification of him. Any reasonable person could see how you had taken his order as your prime duty in life.

But even though you taught by your perfect example how a disciple should mold his life around the order of his *guru,* we observed much more from your example than that. You also preached because you had genuine compassion and therefore could not bear to see others suffering due to forgetfulness of Kṛṣṇa. Perhaps it’s this quality of compassion that inspires me the most, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because I know it was this compassion that brought me to take shelter at your lotus feet. I know that it was this compassion which drew, and continues to draw thousands of *jīvas* from all corners of the world to take up the process of devotional service. And it was this compassion that binds us in a loving pact, an eternal obligation to develop that same sense of compassion you so kindly bestowed upon us. Without compassion, how can we hope to be successful in our preaching and thus begin to repay the eternal debt that we owe you?

So now you can see that I have a genuine cause for lamentation. In comparison to you, I don’t have an ounce of compassion. Whenever I preach, it’s not because I can’t bear to see others’ suffering but rather because I can’t bear my own suffering, which is most often inflicted by an unbridled mind. I preach because it’s the only way that I can begin to take control it. And although I also preach because it’s my duty, unfortunately I still find myself compromised with a half-hearted effort, feeling too inconvenienced to accept the full burden of a genuine preacher.

It’s your shining example, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that exposes this lack of compassion in me. How can I be so complacent and insensitive to the anxieties of others, when such a recent example as yours is still there to constantly remind me? You never felt inconvenienced by preaching to anyone. And beyond that, you were prepared to engage everyone in the whole world, and to take personal responsibility for enlightening and delivering them from the clutched of illusion. You would never think of retreating for a moment due to some personal concern.

You were always surrounded by followers, and you tirelessly preached to us, encouraged us, and most of all tolerated even the most neophyte in us. You carefully weaned us from *māyā’s* embraces and diverted our affections to the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa. How can I ever repay you for this unlimited mercy?

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance in this world, I humbly petition you to never allow me to forget this eternal debt that I owe you. If I can somehow always remember this debt, I’ll be able to maintain a small ray of hope that someday I may develop a tiny drop of the compassion you so perfectly displayed, and thus I can also hope to become a genuine preacher- one who is able to properly glorify his spiritual master not only by his words, but more importantly by his example.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Nirañjana Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

O Prabhupāda*, eternal master,*  
*You have saved us from disaster.*  
*We were drowning in the material sea;*  
*You pulled us out, and set us free*  
*From entanglement in fruitive action,*  
*And gave us eternal satisfaction*  
*Through service to Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme One,*  
*Who drives out darkness like the shining sun.*  
*The more devotion with which one serves,*  
*To such extant can one observe*  
*Lord Kṛṣṇa’s presence in everything,*  
*Which naturally makes one joyfully sing*  
*The Supreme Lord’s unlimited glories,*  
*Which are described in Vedic stories*  
*Coming to us through disciplic succession.*  
*By Your Divine Grace they’ve made an impression*  
*On our dull brains, which were clogged with dirt.*  
*You warned us to become alert*  
*To guard against Māyā, who’s very subtle*  
*And very expert at making trouble*  
*For one who tries to spiritually advance.*  
*She’ll drab him down if she’s given a chance.*  
*So we must hold firm to your instruction*  
*And avoid all kinds of mental concoction.*  
*Please keep us engaged with devotion deep*  
*To Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.*  
*As years pass by, it’s clear to see*  
*That you give us more responsibility*  
*To guide others’ lives-this is a great test.*  
*Please show us how to represent you best.*  
*We only hope to be a credit to you.*  
*“Like father, like son” – it can become true.*  
*If we endeavor with all our might,*  
*You’ll give us the strength to win the fight.*  
*Armed with Kṛṣṇa’s names, the greatest weapons,*  
*We can make devotees from sinful demons,*  
*Transforming their lives by making them pure,*  
*And thus expanding ISKCON more and more,*  
*Till the world becomes flooded with love of God.*  
*This is your desire, O merciful lord.*  
*To be a small cog in your preaching machine-*  
*It’s my only wish, to assist your mission.*  
*And so it is on this auspicious day,*  
*At your lotus feet, I humbly pray,*  
*Begging you’ll always engage in your service*  
*This fallen and lowly PrabhaViṣṇu dāsa.*

*Your eternal servant.*  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Sivarāma Swami

**Following Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I write this offering sitting on a balcony overlooking your house and garden in the Krishna-Balaram Mandir, Vṛndāvana. Spring weather still lingers, although April has begun. In the early afternoon heat, a few birds accompany the silence with their song.

Just now I was reading *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and came across this quote: “When the *ācārya* disappears, things once again become discorded. The perfect disciples of the *ācārya* try to relieve the situation by sincerely following the instructions of the spiritual master.”

The verse appears insignificant. I put down the book and meditate on your statements.

“Life has certainly been ‘disordered’.”

Not in reference to some ISKCONian sociological context but within my own framework….The last years surge through my mind, a wave of shadows, vampirine *anarthas* flood in their wake….

..”Exposed again.”

What was simplicity has turned into a web of doubts and misapprehensions. Beset with duplicity and faithfulness, somehow I cling to your purports. What is the remedy for my disorder?

You write about the “perfect disciple”….

..(a voice) “A natural aspiration of your followers. By the spiritual master’s mercy, one easily attains perfection. Just continue working; everything will work out….”

Just about to close the book….I think (for a change). “Well, what does it mean to be perfect anyway? What *does* it meant to achieve the mercy of the spiritual master?”

From the corner of the eye a becloaked figure retreats.

The last line answers the question….”sincerely following the instructions of the spiritual master.”

….(another voice) ”That should be easy. After all, I am a sincere devotee. Aren’t we all?… What do you think I’d be doing here for twenty years if I weren’t? “

My intelligence pauses. Perhaps it is the effect of the *dhāma.*

…(voice continues) ”…doing my best to follow… A *guru* follows his *guru….*What would I be doing as a *guru* if I weren’t?”

*Buddhi* perseveres. I need a more comprehensive description. What does it mean to be a sincere follower?

By using the word *perfect* you have qualified the characteristics of a disciple. As there are grades of devotees, so there are degrees of following and sincerity, each establishing the caliber of a disciple’s devotion. There are beginners and intermediates, and variations of both. But here you speak of the perfect disciple. Not a beginner, not an intermediate, but a perfect disciple.

Such exacting standards are an embarrassment. Self-analysis, historical evidence, the testimony of Vaiṣṇavas – evidence that I continue to germinate as one “not perfect”. No secret.

Two noisy parrots fly into a tree. They jump effortlessly from one branch to another. How am I to move from the stages of imperfection to perfection?

In a following purport you further describe the perfect disciple: “It is the duty of the disciples to take charge of the mission of the spiritual master and execute it properly. In other words, to execute the will of the spiritual master, the disciple should be prepared to lay down his life and abandon all personal considerations.”

Three answers take charge of the mission of the spiritual master, execute it properly, and lay down all personal considerations. By aspiring to perfect an attitude of service to the *guru,* the disciple achieves the means of perfection by the grace of the Lord.

A little introspection at this point. Time to reflect on what elements stand in the way between perfect and imperfect. In a moment countless arguments arise.

….(*Karma* speaks) ”Sometimes I imagine that executing your mission means to keep body and soul together, practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness ….comfortably under all circumstances. Live as a favorable devotee in the ‘greater ISKCON.’ Support the society…after I make my first million, of course. The way I’ll do it is to donate to a well-chosen project in the movement… which doesn’t exist as yet.”

…then (*pratiṣṭhā)* “Or perhaps I should reside in the temple. Become a *sannyāsī*, GBC, and *guru,* collect followers, receive respect and fame, become known as a great preacher. After all, who will know what horrible things pervade my mind?”

…(Greed) ”Am I not the inheritor of the property of my father? There are many guest visiting our temples. Encourage them to take *darśaṇa.* They will empty their pockets, I will give effortlessly as the *mathācārya* …while all around me decays into ruin. “

A ray of “purity” enters…(*Jṅāna)* ”Become free from *anarthas.* Yes, I shall retire to Vṛndāvana, study *Bhāgavatam,* chant fifty rounds a day. When qualified, I will return to save the society from a renegade course.”

…..(Hankering) “But in all circumstances, there must be some arrangement for security, stability, a solid future… Old age pension, mortgage, disciples, the children…No one told me I would have to work for a living if I couldn’t live by preaching…”

…(Envy) ”I’ll tell you why there are problems…ṛ*tviks, gurus,* G.B.C’s, P.R. child abuse, midlife crisis, *saṅkīrtana* tactics, fanatical temple presidents, Deities, devotees, him, that, everything….but not me.”

…(Lust) “…”

…(Anger) ”…”

…(Duplicity) ”…”

I grab for my bead bag…Hare Kṛṣṇa… and that was just scratching the surface.

A moment of sanity: Śrīla Prabhupāda, what was your mission?

Did you want your books translated into all the languages of the world, a complete set in every home in every town and village? Did you not want people to worship Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa in their homes and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa? Was it not your desire to fulfill the will of Lord Caitanya and Rūpa Gosvāmi by transforming this planet into Vaikuṇṭha, with its spiritual capital situated in Navadvīpa, where the ISKCON flag flies atop the Māyāpura Candrodaya Mandir?

Executing properly….

Śrīla Prabhupāda, did you not expect your disciples to at least follow the four regulative principles and chant sixteen rounds, and also to work together to serve your mission under your authorized representatives, the GBC? It seems the greater one’s commitment to “greater ISKCON,” the lesser one’s commitment to your mission.

New-age psychiatrist, hair cut with fringe, dresses as an astrologer, “Prabhupāda” tattooed on his forehead, prescribing pills, rings, slogans, and independence. The purports read on: “Rogues and nondevotees take advantage and immediately begin to introduce unauthorized principles in the name of so-called swamis, *yogīs,* philanthropists, welfare workers, and so on.”

A last effort, the voices echo hollow in the oncoming twilight: “Will have enough time left for my personal *sādhana?* How will I advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness? What about ‘the practical necessities of life’? What about some other effort to ensure them first?”

Your purports continue: ”When one becomes serious to follow the mission of the spiritual master, his resolution is tantamount to seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead… this means meeting the Supreme Lord in the instructions of the spiritual master. Simply by following on that line one sees the Supreme Personality of Godhead….

“If one engages in the service of the spiritual master, he attains liberation….As far as material conveniences are concerned, they automatically come….All material conveniences are simply awaiting him at all stages of life.”

Silence…for some time anyway. Doubts have dispersed, illusions retreat. A moment to reconsider.

The perfect example. Obviously you.

Without a doubt you exemplify perfection within the disciplic succession. There are many exalted souls to whom we could turn for precept. But you are the embodiment of all predecessors for your disciples. Their service is fully reposed within you.

Did you not say on countless occasions that following the order of one’s spiritual master is the principal criterion of a disciple (humbly including yourself)? “My only qualification is that I try to satisfy my spiritual master. “ “I have not changed anything I have heard from my Guru Mahāraja.” “If I have had any success, it is because I strictly follow the order of my spiritual master.”

The results of your service speak for themselves. Worldwide you are recognized.

Someone wanted to challenge in court that you were a *sannyāsi.* The judge replied, “Sir, this is ridiculous. Everyone in the world knows he \[Śrīla Prabhupāda\] was a *sannyāsi.”*

You did it. Such attainment is possible only when one is fully empowered by Kṛṣṇa. Perfect.

Another moment goes by. The peacocks cry in the distance.
