Not long ago, as you were walking on Juhu Beach, a young devotee followed behind, trying to match his every step to your imprint in the sand. It was one of those efforts that was meant to be etched in my mind. I was somewhere else in your ISKCON world, but the picture came to me through sound.

Many time you instructed us, “Just do as I do.” In that scene, following you was simplicity itself.

In time, “doing as you did” was accentuated by your departure, an event that catalyzed my understanding of the need for surrendered intelligence, which unfortunately is still conspicuous in me by its absence.

In an attempt to glorify you, I have simply analyzed my failure. By realizing my insignificance I have sketched a shadow of your greatness. The best way to glorify you would be to follow your orders perfectly, with all sincerity, serving your mission without reservations.

But I cannot.

The remnants of my sins still try to block the way.

But I will try. I will try. If I am capable of anything, I can say with confidence that I will never stop trying-trying to serve you despite blunders and insurmountable ignorance.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that you will please continue to accept my worthless service. Although it is incomplete, please encourage me from within. Keep me under the shade of your lotus feet.

Perhaps someday, in some far distant lifetime, after the poisonous effects of countless sinful lives have diminished, when you bless me with your causeless mercy, perhaps then I can become a real disciple, a perfect disciple, and render some real service at your lotus feet.

Aspiring to become your sincere follower, I remain  
*Your eternal servant,*  
Śivarāma Swami

## Tamāl Kṛishna Goswami

**Pinnacle of Perfection**

*Of all the days, Vyṣa-puja is the heaviest affair;*  
*To venture forth from gravity it’s doubtful few would dare.*  
*Yet who will mind when unity words relieve the tension and pace,*  
*And tickle one’s intelligence and force a smile across one’s face?*  
*There is no better cure for those who suffer from depression:*  
*A malady unique to man, proud pinnacle of progression;*  
*Though he prides himself far better than all other living creatures,*  
*Each one surpasses man with one or more outstanding features.*  
*And so we’ve come a long way ‘round in order to compare*  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda with these creatures by the qualities they share.*  
*In case you think us frivolous, we’ll start comparing those most bless’d*  
*And then proceed to name some others who, in fact, are among regressed.*  
*Always serving, dear to Kṛṣṇa, all the Vedas do avow:*  
*Prabhupāda’s a pure devotee! He’s a wish-fulfilling cow!*  
*Though more tolerant than a tree and more humble than a straw,*  
*He prowled the planet like a lion, purifying by his roar.*  
*While Garuda feasts on elephants, timingilas swallow whales,*  
*This giant Śrīla Prabhupāda with his sharpened words impales*  
*All atheists and voidists, who he hungrily devours,*  
*As tidbits for a giant with such superhuman powers.*  
*His singing charms the songbirds, lulls the owls to sleep at night.*  
*His vision shames the eagles with his futuristic sight.*  
*The swan of men, he takes the best and leaves aside the worst,*  
*Drinking Kṛṣṇa conscious nectar with discriminating thirst.*  
*As a fish becomes caught by allurement of the tongue,*  
*As a dear lies fallen due to deadly tunes a hunter’s sung,*  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda sits motionless, tears gliding down his face,*  
*His senses victimized by Kṛṣṇa’s all-compelling grace.*  
*His birth has lifted Mother Earth form the ocean of depravity*  
*And made all planets orbit her despite the laws of gravity.*  
*The snakes have vowed to mend their ways or else forfeit their skins,*  
*Charmed and swaying to his mantras, smiling their toothless grins.*  
*Giraffes stretch their speckled necks to nibble tops of trees,*  
*While children grasp expectantly to catch the moon with ease.*  
*But only Śrīla Prabhupāda can reach that hidden space*  
*To rouse the soul that lies unconscious by his merciful embrace.*  
*More cunning than a clever fox when it comes to business deals;*  
*More playful with small children than a pool of buoyant seals;*  
*He bristles like a porcupine, guards Kṛṣṇa from offenses.*  
*But like a tortoise when attacked, he withdraws all his senses.*  
*He’s sought by all, one of a kind, like the Chinese panda bear,*  
*And if unicorns are hard to find, then he’s by far more rare.*  
*For none have ever been more loyal to their master’s mission,*  
*Nor followed faithfully behind, so closely to tradition.*  
*And we’re his puppies, barking and howling, trying to catch his tails-*  
*As much as we cuss, and kick up a fuss, barks never sound like hails!*  
*It was our hope that within this scope the quirks of Mother Nature*  
*Would magnify the radii of Prabhupāda’s outstanding stature.*  
*Alas, it seems we’ll have to wait awhile before we hit that mark;*  
*Next Vyāsa-pūjā day we’ll start to pray where we left off-Bark, bark!*

*Bark, bark!*

Tamāl Krishna Goswami

## Toṣaṇa Kṛṣṇa dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories unto you, all-merciful master! Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mercy comes in the form of His pure devotees. They alone are the light in the material abyss. Their very act and word are beacons in the stormy existence of birth and death.

In all this world only you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, had the true compassion and purity to eternally bless me. My debt to you can never be repaid. I can only pray to be your eternal servitor. By attempting to serve you I am confident all the great *acharyas* will protect me. By my accepting you faithfully as my real father, surely all my grandfathers will be merciful to me- and thus your mercy expands.

Without your mercy I will be helplessly washed away into darkness. You alone came and revealed the road which leads directly to Govinda’s lotus feet. All be must do is follow that road-step by step, day by day.

May your instructions be my life and soul.

*Your fallen foolish servant,*  
Toṣaṇa Kṛṣṇa dāsa.

## Vāsudeva dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet again and again. All glories unto you.

I pray that I never forget your kindness upon me. To one so insignificant and fallen, who had no knowledge of spiritual life, you have given the holy name. You have given me the most valuable of all things despite my innumerable disqualifications. This is your causeless mercy, and thus I remain eternally indebted to you. Who can be more merciful than you?

No one can advance in spiritual life by his own endeavor. But if one receives the mercy of the *guru,* then he immediately makes advancement.

Once, during a morning walk in Māyāpura, you asked me, “Do you have a temple like this in Fiji?” I replied, “You have been to Fiji- so now this will happen.”

Now the preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Fiji is also gradually increasing. Wherever you have placed your lotus feet in this world, that place has been transformed into a holy place- *tīrtha-dhāma.* Śrī Kṛṣṇa Kāliya temple is also one of these *dhāmas.* Just as by visiting a holy place one becomes purified of sins, similarly by visiting the Śrī Kṛṣṇa Kāliya temple many people have freed themselves of sinful habits and obtained real understanding.

Although we understand that you are eternal, still, due to your physical absence, we constantly feel the pain of separation from you. When my Godbrothers come to Fiji, in their association I am able to mitigate the pain of separation to some extant.

It is my fervent prayer that you always keep me in your service. Living without your service would be the greatest of all punishments.

Please accept my *daṇḍavats* again and again at your lotus feet.

*Your humble servant,*  
Vāsudeva dāsa

## Vīrabāhu dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, the personification of all auspiciousness for the fallen souls.

In our continuous life-time duty of learning about your transcendental personality, as well as learning to glorify you properly, the very attempt brings us unlimited bliss. It comes from realizing only a drop of the unbounded ocean of your sublime qualities. These are such that even while your servants try to serve you and show gratitude, you further *serve them* by rewarding them with spiritual pleasure.

Today we remembered very vividly the feeling of being in your presence. This recollection came while we were listening to the following morning reading from *Śrī-Namamrta* (quoted from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.30.35*):*

“Whenever pure topics of the transcendental world are discussed, the members of the audience forget all kinds of material hankerings, at least for the time being. Not only that, but they are no longer envious of one another, nor do they suffer from anxiety or fear.”

By your grace, which has taken the form of your precious books, every fortunate soul who has come in contact with your Society has had some personal experience of this. But, with respect to the sacred, outstanding preeminence of your *vāni,* today, in our mind, your *vāni* has performed a special *pūjā* to your *vapu.*

In your purport to the above verse, you wrote as follows: ”Vaikuṇṭha means ‘without anxiety,’ and the material world means full of anxiety. As stated by Prahlada Mahāraja: *sadā samudvigna-dhiyām asad-grahit.* The living entities who have accepted this material world as a residence are full of anxiety. A place immediately becomes Vaikuṇṭha whenever the holy topics of the Personality of Godhead are discussed by pure devotees. This is the process of ś*ravaṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ,* chanting and hearing about the Supreme Lord, Viṣṇu. As the Supreme Lord Himself confirms:

*Nāhaṁ tiṣṭhāmi vaikuṇṭhe yogināṁ hṛdayeṣu vā*  
*Yatra gāyanti mad-bhaktāḥ tatra tiṣṭhāmi nārada*

**‘My dear Nārada, I do not reside in My abode, Vaikuṇṭha, nor do I reside within the hearts of the** ***yogis,*** **but I reside in that place where My pure devotees chant My holy name and discuss My form, pastimes, and qualities.’ Because of the presence of the Lord in the form of the transcendental vibration, the Vaikuṇṭha atmosphere is evoked. This atmosphere is without fear and anxiety. One living entity does not fear another.”**

This was all so natural in your presence. As we were watching your holy *vānī do puja*, a thought appeared, a child of your *vānī:* ”Being in Prabhupāda’s presence would do everything. It would establish the firm understanding that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is everything, the only thing, and that it is easy. How can we produce the atmosphere of being in Prabhupāda’s presence, so that others can have that most powerful and convincing experience? “Immediately I become anxious- while listening to how listening would overcome anxiety. So I listened on:

“By hearing the holy names and glories of the Lord, a person executes pious activities. Śṛ*ṇvatām sva-kathā Kṛṣṇah puṇya-śravaṇa-kirtanah (Bhag.* 1.2.17*).* Thus his material hankerings immediately stop. This *saṅkīrtana* movement started by the Society for Krishna Consciousness is meant for creating Vaikuṇṭha, the transcendental world that is without anxiety, even in this material world. The method is the propagation of the ś*ravaṇam kīrtanam* process throughout the world.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, what can I offer you? I find nothing in me that may be useful, except the following desire, which may be sincere: That I may utilize my countless excursions in this material world to praise your presence and try to make it available to others. This desire directly relates to your instructions in the last portion of the purport:

“In the material world everyone is envious of his fellow man. Animalistic envy exists in human society as long as there is no perforāmance of *saṅkīrtana-yajna,* the chanting of the holy names-Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. The Pracetas therefore decided to remain always in the society of devotees, and they considered that to be the highest benediction possible in human life.”

By your grace, may I too always keep in my heart that understanding, and by remembering your *vapu* may I always do *pūja* to your *vānī.*

*Your insignificant insignificant,*  
Vīrabāhu dāsa

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Sannyasis

## Ānanda Svarūpa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Another year-another painful experience.

Yes, painful because I don’t like service in separation from you, my master, No matter what the *śāstras* say. Those *śāstras* are for pure souls. Me, I want your presence, I want your guidance. Nothing, but nothing, can stand in for you. Without you, everything drifts.

To some-exalted souls- writing a Vyāsa-pūjā offering is an ecstatic experience.

May they bless me with such realizations.

In the meantime, knowing my shortcomings, I find it painful to be brutally honest in the full glare of the peer public’s all too critical eye. Not for nothing did I miss writing for so many years.

I *could* gather a few appropriate verses, some selected panegyrics, or, as one authority politely put it, long, drawn-out, hyperbolic praises…. But even my computer is more expert at that than I! Who needs *that* cop-out?

For me, you set the mood for communication yourself by your personal example:

Once, in Vṛndāvana, I participated in a rare (for me) morning walk with you. You were inspecting the construction of the *gurukula* building. As the walk proceeded, first around the temple grounds, then onto the construction site, in and out of uncompleted rooms, left and right along the walls, up and down scaffoldings, your eyes never missed a beat. On and on, to the rhythmic tapping of your cane, you patiently heard your manager’s monologue effusively describing how "Everything was wonderful.” After twenty minutes of this patent propaganda you stopped the walk and poked your cane into a corner of a newly constructed wall. The plaster immediately crumbled off. You said, “Is this wonderful?” Then you pointed at some cement carelessly left out in the wet: “Is this wonderful?” Next a pile of dirt: “And is this wonderful?”

Applying pressure as only you know how, you made time stop and bear down with all its weight on your inescapable points. The pregnant moments stretched into eons. Any attempt by the devotees responsible for the project to make excuses met with such cutting, transcendentally angry sarcasm that finally it became clear there was nothing left to do but cry. (To his everlasting credit, the local GBC man actually to shed tears). I don’t know if *he* learned his lesson, but I certainly learned mine: Hype doesn’t cut it with His Divine Grace. Talk about the mother teaching the daughter-in-law by teaching the daughter!

So, today I want to keep my Vyāsa-pūjā offering free from fancy flights of the imagination. Sadly, since I commonly spout my share of personal propaganda, some hype will be there; but at least let me not insult your ever-present intelligence by falling too deep into that moronic Madison Avenue trap.

For well I know it that one day will soon come when I’ll again be forced to look you in the eye!

See, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’m such a hapless fool that I fear very little –yet even in my abysmal foolishness what I fear most is your displeasure.

Since you returned to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa thirteen devastating distressing, and heartrendingly unbearable years ago, my hold on service has been tenuous. Exactly like a baby monkey, and in utter illusion, I speciously think that I’m hanging on to your lotus feet.

Ha!

Fact is, you have me in your strong grasp like a fierce, golden Royal Bengal tiger carrying her cub.

We’re all like that, cubs. Frolicking and playing, getting into trouble by making believe we’re so great, so expert, so learned. Speaking for myself, I can hardly see the nose in front of my face most of the time. I’m lucky just to get my rounds done and keep my promise to you, most of the time.

Despite your astute advice that only real preaching will save me, I stubbornly avoid real action and prefer to safely itinerate from temple to temple, trying to impress inexperienced neophytes with my Swami image and my so-called seniority, picking up cheap adoration and distinction- not to speak of money to maintain my worthless existence.

“Do not be allured by cheap disciples. Go on steadfastly rendering service first…. With service activities stopped and all spiritual progress choked up, ….if you simply imitate, everything will be spoiled.” This sound advice notwithstanding, I find ways to avoid service and enjoy sense gratification, even in a city which, by Western standards, epitomizes nonenjoyment: Cairo.

Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvāti Ṭhākura once remarked that the mentality of feeling qualified to accept service from others, to desire or, horror of horrors, actually *demand* service, us un-Vaiṣṇava. It is hellish. Me? Without shame I routinely make so many demands from your institution and its members. Thus I’m trapped into passively cooperating in seeing the temple doors locked tight against “undesirables.” How desirable was I in the nineteen-sixties? I should be locked out of the temple, I’m such a pretentious rascal.

The demons hatch innumerable plans just to cheat the poor, foolish, misinformed populace. Their agri-abuse poisons our food, their ubiquitously insidious advertising machine hooks our children on junk, their ugra-industries pollute our environment, and everyone’s sent to an early, ignominious grave-all in the name of the almighty buck, coined in the absence of a Vaiṣṇava’s mercy.

Despite insurmountable karmic odds, people everywhere are searching for peace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness and fulfill their desires.

Not by saffron cloth alone did I become a *sannyāsī* but by preaching. Without compassionate preaching, there’s no saving grace. I beg you today, please do not let my *sannyāsa* become useless. Let me preach with dedication, at least in this life.

Whatever independence I have, let it be cast into the abyss.

*Your servant,*  
Ānanda Svarūpa Swami

## Bhaktirāghava Sādhu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept the *daṇḍavat praṇāmas* I am offering to you from a distance, since I am trying to practice and develop some humility.

In the process of approaching the Absolute Truth, we are told not to establish our faith whimsically or sentimentally. *Guru, śāstra,* and *sādhu* are there to substantiate our feelings about spiritual things. Another vital element to clearly understand this approach to the Absolute Truth is our own *sukṛti,* or pious activities, which help us seize the opportunity to take up the practice of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, after we meet a pure devotee of the Lord.

Whence does faith arise? And whence faithlessness? First I need to understand why I, the *ātmā,* or self, identify with the various environments I am surrounded by, and also know how my mundane relationships toward other persons and things generate more *karma,* and how I took this birth as a result of my previous *karma.* I need to know how my mundane relationships toward other persons and things generate more *karma*, and how all these material entanglements impede my search for the Absolute Truth. Also necessary is an understanding of how coming in contact with a pure devotee (*guru* and /or *sādhu*) *gene*rates faith in Kṛṣṇa and points the way out of these entanglements. All these points can be understood from *śāstra.*

The fifth element (after *guru, sādhu, śāstra, and ātmā)* and the most important one (being the source of the others), is Śrī Kṛṣṇa. From Him intelligence and forgetfulness come, according to my surrendering mood. In *bhakti* proper I have to depend in Him solely, waiting for His mercy, and this also comes out of His sweet will. I’m meant to become His slave, tied by the sweet ropes of *prema.*

Without the steady and proper pursuit of comprehension of the above-mentioned five elements- Kṛṣṇa, *guru, sādhu, śāstra, and ātmā-* I cannot proceed in healthy Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is a perfect circle, in which every one of its parts is vital to “roll” in a complete realization of spiritual experience. By “rolling” I mean the dynamic aspect of the movement in spiritual life, a movement meant only to go ahead, but to rise higher. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is in essence this :Depending fully first in the first four of the five above-mentioned elements, one develops a healthy faith in the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. As one practices the process, gradually one comes to understand the true, spiritual personality of the self (one’s *sva-rūpa),* an understanding that is based on the understanding of the other four elements.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, somehow, unworthy as I am, I become a recipient of your liberal compassion. I developed faith in you due to Kṛṣṇa’s divine grace, and since are His (Kṛṣṇa’s) Divine Grace, you are as good as He. The faith I received from Your Divine Grace I cannot understand. You know very well that I am not a devotee, but I am patiently waiting for that day to come, and that patience is due to my faith in the *dīkṣā* connection with Your Divine Grace. As the external manifestation if *paramātmā,* you may know what I am talking about; otherwise, He may let you know at His sweet will, since you are so close to Him.

Actually, to be more specific, I think it is She who may keep you informed. Being blessed by the association of pure devotees, such as your father and Śrī Vārṣabhānavī-devī-dayita dāsa (Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura), you are engaged as a servant of Śrīmati Rādhārani, as they wished you to be. Following in their footsteps, and due to your intense love for Rādhā-Govinda in the mood of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you came to the West. Taking the great risk of being censured as a giver of pearls to the hogs, you desired to break open the treasure chest and provide free access to the devotional conception of Śrī Rūpā-mañjarī. It is up to your sincere followers to trace out the essence of what you came to offer.

Only for someone who is experiencing the need of the *gopīs* to meet Their Lord is it possible to do the impossible, to make the highest conceptions of *bhakti* available to even the lowest members of human society. As Nityānanda Prabhu liberally propagated the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu even to the most sinful, you gave the most sinful persons of Kali-yuga access to the highest levels of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In the package of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you brought to the West, everything is included. Your good friend and Godbrother Śrīla B.R. Śrīdhara Mahārāja once said that your magnanimity was in the mood of that very same Nityānanda Prabhu.

As a servant of Śrīmati Rādhārani, you opened to all the long path to achievement of a similar position, but due to our ignorance, we always run the risk of committing *apa-rādhā,* literally “actions against Rādhā. “You explained to us that the name Rādhā derives from the word *ārādhana,* “worship.” Therefore Śrīmanti Rādhārani is the actual worshiper of Kṛṣṇa. After Her and Her expansions, an unlimited quality and quantity of followers are there, and if we accept you as our spiritual master, we must follow them also. This is the *guru-paramparā* system you brought to us, complete with the conception of *dīkṣā-guru and śikṣā-gurus.* Again by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s divine grace, after your physical departure I fortunately perceived some inspiration from within to accept one of Your Divine Grace’s last instructions as my life and soul: to receive *śikṣā* shelter under Śrīla Bhaktivedānta Śrīdhara Mahārāja from Navadvīpa, and Śrīla Bhaktivedānta Nārāyaṇa Mahārāja, from Mathurā.

Demanding your chastisement unlimited times whenever it is required for my spiritual advancement, I also beg your loving affection to make me realize that I am not just a dull piece of stone. Unworthy of seeing your feet, I bow down to the holy shade of your holy foot-dust, and I bow down again to the feet of those Vaiṣṇavas who were able to collect such dust. Furthermore, I fall in full *daṇḍavits* before those sincere souls who not only collected the dust of your holy feet and placed it on their heads, but who also allowed that sacred dust to descend-forty inches only- to the most inner chamber of their hearts.

My eternal compassionate master, I am sure you are in very sweet and blissful company now. What you carried within you was the highest aspect of love for Kṛṣṇa, and this is what attracted me toward you. It is that essence which captivated me, and not so much how it was carried due to time and circumstances, although this was unique also. I revere your transcendental form as you came here to all of us, but I always waiting for your personal constant manifestation somewhere else also- or even everywhere. Bless me to be eternally able to recognize you whenever you want, in this world or elsewhere.

*Your eternal student, even while acting as a teacher,*  
Bhaktirāghava Sādhu Swami

## Bhakti-vikāśa Swami

**If Prabhupāda Had Not Come**

If Prabhupāda had not come (who can imagine?),  
What would have been our position?  
How could we have lived?  
The science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness,  
The ultimate limit of love of God….  
Who would have let the world know?  
From” you are not this body” to  
The secret pastimes in the groves of Vṛndāvana….  
Entrance requires special expertise.  
Who else but Prabhupāda could have  
Been empowered to empower others to understand?  
So, sing, sing again! The glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Making the heart pure and simple.  
In this ocean of birth and death,  
Such a merciful person…..  
We don’t find any other.  
Without singing the praises of Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
How can we survive. What is the point of living?  
This fallen disciple  
Regrets that his heart is like a stone  
And wonders by what misfortune  
He is not attracted to his guru’s lotus feet.

Submissively offered to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedānta Swami Prabhupāda on the occasion of his Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, 1990, by a fallen disciple,  
Bhakti-vikāśa Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. By some good fortune I have met a person of the most divine qualities. Dear Gurudeva, you are guarding me from the tricks of *māyā,* whose propositions are but empty assurances. Please accept this offering of poetry on this Vyāsa-pūjā day, the anniversary of the most blessed event.

**Promises of Gold**

Promises of Gold  
Everywhere,  
But reality tells  
There’s only despair.  
We live this life  
With high, high hopes,  
Mistaking snakes  
For ordinary ropes.  
In sleep or awake,  
Will-o’-the wisps  
Haunt the mind  
With illusory gifts,  
Promise of gold  
In the prime of life.  
Then youth breaks,  
Ensuring our strife.  
So much teased  
By other men’s grass,  
We contemplate deep,  
Till hankering pass.  
The unchaste mind  
Is lustfully fed,  
For everyone’s bearing  
A Rāvaṇa head.  
Ongoing cravings,  
Huge appetites,  
Senses off course  
Like wild flying kites.  
Beauty of flesh  
Vanishes in haste.  
In this paradise  
The fools are all based.  
Yearning and burning  
For happiness peace,  
Counting each blessing  
Till new life lease.  
The poor soul wanders  
From promise to promise,  
Beckoned by glimmer,  
A color like jaundice.  
Promises of gold  
At rainbow’s end,  
A denial of Him,  
The ultimate friend.  
Promises of gold,  
With full guarantee-  
Fulfillment achieved  
By the pure devotee.

*Your servant,*  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances over and over at your divine lotus feet.

As the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is transcendental to time, so are your teachings, and exemplary acts. Definitely you and only take the conditioned souls toward the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, because that is your home and you know the path perfectly.

With your *mṛdaṅga* in the shape of your books, you continue to awaken those sleeping hearts. You deliver them with the message of pure love for Kṛṣṇa.

On the streets and homes of many towns and villages around the world, even in the most distant mountains and jungles, all your wonderful books have been introduced. The newspapers, radio, and TV broadcast Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Everybody has heard about Kṛṣṇa, and each day they talk and ask more and more about Kṛṣṇa. This is the proof of your greatness as a pure servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa. This is the evidence of your capacity as savior of the world.

All the spiritual knowledge descending in the *paramparā* system since time immemorial is included in the Bhaktivedanta commentaries of your books.

Listening to your classes and reading your books are like exploring a world always new and full of transcendental treasures. Your arguments are unbeatable simply because they are nondifferent form Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is unbeatable. The magic of your words can enthuse anybody. You do not trick us into anything that can cause us anxiety. You are always honest in your speech. Without purity it is never possible to transmit the essential aspects of the truth and the feeling of fixed submission to Kṛṣṇa.

Your deep and fresh vision of the scriptures’ knowledge encourages us and allows us to preach successfully. Meditating upon you causes all types of fear to disappear. Your transcendental activities are enough to make us remember Lord Kṛṣṇa and understand immediately the way to serve Him. For that reason you will always be our irreplaceable *guru*.

Following your instructions means to be saved from any danger or hindrance that might appear. Following your instructions grants us the qualification to understand Kṛṣṇa so that we can also convey that knowledge to others.

The way in which you do things is a continuous teaching that protects us. You are pure and transparent and genuinely humble.

Your glory and fame increase constantly and are like a big, strong, and powerful tree under which people seek shelter.

A universe of unlimited gifts comes from you. If by Your Divine Grace someone can appreciate it, he will be blessed.

The source of your powers is Lord Kṛṣṇa. That’s why they are inexhaustible and incomparable.

Since I have been in your service, I have experienced an indefatigable happiness. By thinking of you, I feel that I am part of you, and a bright inspiration is automatically manifested in my heart.

Your movement is different from others. Yours is dynamic, simple, comfortable, pure, and attractive.

Your disciples mature slowly by learning daily how to conduct their own lives and steer your ISKCON ship. Controversies arise all the time, forcing us to study your books in order to find new and exact teachings.

There are some who argue more, while other argue less. In spite of this, everybody agrees to serve your lotus feet.

The ISKCON society is young, and many if its members are somewhat impetuous. Twenty years will give us without a doubt the serenity and poise of an authentic transcendental vision. We are in your hands; we have nothing to fear. Those who will not forget you will always live with you, enjoying your blessings.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda, savior of the fallen souls! I thank you again and again for the undeserved opportunity of serving you one more year.

*Your most unqualified servant,*  
Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

## Bīr Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

I am praying that you relieve me of my greatest problem, that of false pride. I am thinking that I am intelligent, and therefore I am unable to properly hear your instructions.

Lord Caitanya instructs us by His personal example how to approach the spiritual master. In His meeting with Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī He states, “My spiritual master considered Me a fool, and therefore chastised Me. ‘You are a fool, ‘ he said. ‘You are not qualified to study Vedānta philosophy, and therefore You must always chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.’”

Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is the Supreme Lord, yet in order to teach us the proper mood to approach the spiritual master, He acts as an ideal disciple. He does this to check fools from giving up the service of the spiritual master.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you comment on this regard, “Every disciple must consider himself completely unaware of the science of Kṛṣṇa, and must always be ready to carry out the orders of the spiritual master to become competent in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. A disciple should always remain a fool before his spiritual master.”

Every word in your purports has great significance. So, your use of the word ”always” in the last sentence of the previous paragraph must be accepted literally. Always means eternally, or never-ending. I should never think that I am so advanced that I have become intelligent and I am beyond consulting you at every moment.

Consulting you means to see through your words. We have the expression *śāstra-cakṣus,* which means “to see through the eyes of scripture. “I pray that I will always see through your words and not attempt to jump over your head, an act which you inform us is a grievous offense against the spiritual master.

I am completely dependent upon you, not only for instructions but for the strength, intelligence, and humility to hear and follow your instructions. Please, I beg of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, do not let me slip away, but let me remain eternally sheltered in the shade of your lotus feet.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bīr Kṛṣṇa dasa Goswami

## Dhanurdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

In my Vyāsa-pūjā offering last year, I prayed to you to increase my responsibilities. You answered my prayers and also gave me two important realizations.

First, I gained further insight into how accepting responsibility enhances one’s strength in devotional service. Spiritual strength descends to those who are dependent on your grace. Challenges in service certainly increase one’s ability to execute your mission, for they expose one’s inebriates and force one to rely on the source of spiritual power, the Lord and His pure devotee.

Accepting responsibility in your service not only helps us increase our own strength, but it also helps us appreciate your prowess.

For example, when I feel strained in even carrying out a small responsibility in your mission, I just imagine what fortitude you must have possessed to have borne the full weight of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission.

I thing sometimes of 1973 and 1974, when your seemingly diminutive seventy-eight-year-old frame not only carried the multifarious duties of the managerial and spiritual head of ISKCON; not only gave personal attendance to GBC duties in India, including the establishment of your major projects in Vṛndāvana, Māyāpura, and Bombay; not only personally trained thousands of disciples through personal meetings and hundreds of letters; but at the same time completed the entire *Caitany-caritāmrta* in seventeen volumes. And all while traveling constantly as a wandering mendicant!

You once said that one advances in devotional service by appreciating others’ devotional service. When I hear of your capacity to render devotional service, it exposes to me my lack of commitment and inspires me to advance. I feel incapable of bearing more service, because my heart is burdened with lust. I pray this year that you spare no means to burn my material desires to ashes, so that like you I can become fully absorbed in the service of the Lord with not a moment wasted in any other engagement.

*Your humble servant,*  
Dhanurdhara Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have confirmed the importance of the disciple’s offering a Vyāsa-pūjā homage to the spiritual master by comparing it to the required basic training in a military regimen.

Only by properly learning to glorify the Lord’s representative in disciplic succession can the student develop pure consciousness and know that his devotional sentiments are accepted by Lord Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, I consider this annual obligation to be an opportunity mercifully given by Your Divine Grace to help me eradicate my stubborn *anarthas* and thus develop a pinch of the enthusiasm you have for glorifying the Lord.

It is certainly no coincidence that your divine appearance coincided with the auspicious observance day of Nandotsava. You are virtually the personification of Nanda Mahārāja’s spontaneous mood of celebrating the manifestation of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You are unparalleled in the enthusiasm, devotion, and expertise you displayed in broadcasting the holy name, fame, qualities, and pastimes of the Lord. Your entire life was one jubilant ovation of Kṛṣṇa’s glories. You took every opportunity to proclaim to the conditioned souls that God does, in fact, personally descend amongst us and that He is always perceivable to those pure souls who relish *Kṛṣṇa-kathā.*

Some examples:

Even while engaged in your pharmaceutical business in your *gṛhastha-līlā,* you took your Guru Mahārāja’s instructions to heart by preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the English medium of *Back to Godhead* magazine.

After Lord Kṛṣṇa, arranged for your retirement from *gṛhastha* life, you immediately began writing and publishing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*  and personally taking orders from libraries and universities.

As soon as the legal necessities were taken care of and the opportunity for travel presented itself in the form of free passage in a passenger cabin in a Scindia cargo carrier, you left your beloved Rādhā-Dāmodara temple and set sail for America.

You found a way to broadcast the chanting of the *mahā-mantra* even during the impersonalist meetings of Dr. Mishra.

After only a little time in India in 1967, you returned to America as soon as your spiritual body was “fit to serve you.”

You arose in your transcendental consciousness early each morning to work on your life project, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* while everyone else was in deep sleep.

You always held a complete program of *kīrtana* at all public engagements, whether the house was full or near-empty.

While taking your morning massage, you would sometimes launch an impromptu attack on Māyāvādī philosophy, cheating *yogīs,* or foolish mundane scientists.

In Seattle you instructed us how to be ready to preach even to an ant, encouraging it to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and take *prasādam.*

In Los Angeles you held a program for devotees and guest in a different house or donated garage every night.

On a moment’s notice one night in Mexico City, you unhesitatingly accepted the chance to preach on national TV, even though it meant staying up past 2 a.m.

One night, after speaking at several programs throughout day and driving for hours to attend them- all without taking hardly any *prasādam-*  you ecstatically stated over *purīs* and hot milk, “This is the way to be happy- work all day for Kṛṣṇa.”

You continued preaching literally until your last moments with us by instructing us and reciprocating our loving sentiments and by whispering your Bhaktivedānta purports into a hand-held microphone..

In conclusion, unlike the dancer who wouldn’t dance because of a slope in the platform, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you showed us how to preach unconditionally, dancing as you begged Lord Kṛṣṇa to make you “dance, dance, dance.”

*Your eternal servant,*  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on this most auspicious day.

This year, before writing my offering to you, I reread and reflected on some of the offerings written by your sincere followers last year. I am awestruck by their devotion and embarrassed by my lack of it. Unlike me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, these devotees actually love you. Theirs is not simply an offering of “fruits and flowers” but rather an expression of transcendental emotion experienced only by those deeply immersed in your service.

To love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, means to serve you by following your instructions. This is the difference between your sincere disciples and myself. They have dedicated their lives to your service, whereas I have only pretended to do so. So much time wasted and so many opportunities lost to engage wholeheartedly in your service!

It’s not that you have made yourself available to me or have failed in any way to offer me *prema-bhakti.* You have most mercifully offered me-and the world- the touchstone of the holy name, your illuminating Bhaktivedānta purports, and an unlimited variety of other transcendental gifts and engagements, all beautifully packaged in the form of ISKCON and the BBT. Everything is complete. No sane man could ask for more. But fool that I am, although you have kindly offered these gifts, I have ungratefully refused to accept them. Otherwise, how is it possible that after so many years I still have such a strong desire to enjoy material sense gratification?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I must fully surrender to you. I know this; all of your disciples and their disciples also know this about me. But you know, that I cannot do it on my own. Please help me. Being among the most fallen, I require your special mercy. Therefore, I beg these benedictions from you even though they are more than I deserve: Allow me to serve you with the same faithful love and devotion described by your sincere followers year after year in their Vyāsa-pūjā offerings. Let me remain in the shelter of ISKCON, serving all the exalted Vaiṣṇavas. Please empower me to cooperate with others to spread your teaching near and far. And, in the course of things, bless me with the vision to see as you my only shelter and object of worship.

*I beg to remain your eternal servant,*  
Giridhāri Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

**When There’s Naught I Wouldn’t Do**

Beyond the shroud of nescience,  
Beyond passion’s faśade,  
Beyond the veil of goodness  
Shines our dearmost Prabhupāda.  
This blessed day of remembrance  
Is the greatest boom for us,  
For us your servants, good or bad,  
Confront ourselves we must.  
A day for shame, a day for joy,  
Perhaps a combination,  
As we deeply delve within,  
And search for realization.  
I myself can fill a book  
Expressing lamentation.  
But what’s the worth? I’d rather rhyme  
With words of exultation:  
You’ve shown us what is life,  
Through your preaching and your kindness,  
A limitless ocean of mercy,  
Lifting us from blindness.  
An ocean, in fact, so vast,  
It flows to one and all.  
We see this every day.  
Preaching on Capitol Mall.  
This spot is quite unique,  
For millions come to see  
The capital of our nation-  
Where our festival happens to be.  
It’s Lord Caitanya’s plan  
For capturing the masses,  
Especially all the teens  
Who come there with their classes.  
Our chanting and our dancing  
They gather ‘round to see.  
They clap and purchase books,  
Longing to be free.  
Posters, beads, and shirts they take.  
Like us they’d like to be,  
As they stand there on the shore  
Of the sea of ecstasy.  
Then that ocean overflows,  
As we call them in to dance.  
Some hesitate, some go in straight.  
As they partake of the chance  
To dive into that nectar  
Of transcendental sound  
And shed their false identity  
As their teachers they astound.  
And after dancing to the Name,  
They’re set for us to grant  
The final dose of mercy:  
We teach them how to chant!  
And as they sing and dance like mad.  
None want it e’er to end.  
They’ll never be the same,  
With Kṛṣṇa as their friend.  
“Our magic is our chanting  
And our dancing,” you have told.  
The Hare Kṛṣṇa festival  
Brings all into our fold.  
In full appreciation,  
As the kids called out to God,  
A devotee said to me one time,  
“Thank Kṛṣṇa for Prabhupāda!  
“He’s placed us fallen souls  
In the service of the Lord,  
Showing that a life of sin  
Is a life to be abhorred.”  
I’ve heard that said at other times,  
But that time it meant more,  
For I saw your mercy acting  
As I never had before.  
And I stopped to count my blessings  
For being there with you  
When you helped me turn to Kṛṣṇa,  
As these kids were turning, too.  
And I pray to see the day  
When there’s naught I wouldn’t do  
To help all souls as you’ve helped me,  
So their lives can start anew.

*Your servant,*  
Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

This year I write to you from the field, on mission for Your Divine Grace in Western Samoa, deep in the South Pacific….somewhere between heaven and hell. Both are here: the so-called pleasures of the celestial kingdom and the grim reminders of material life. Amidst the flowing palms and sandy beaches a cyclone has just hit, the worst in years, leaving a trail of death and destruction. Much that was here can never be replaced. I have arrived at a time when many have lost all hope. I never imagined I’d come to such a place; when flying in the other day I couldn’t even find it on the map.

And what a desolate place it is! Devoid of *ātma-tattva,* knowledge of the soul, the islanders lament the loss of their loved ones taken by the storm. The immense destruction and threat of disease combine to create an eerie atmosphere here, in which no one knows where to go or whom to turn to in their sorrow.

Yet the timely storm makes preaching potent. Reduced in the opulence of material life, people come forward to hear your message. Books are going out and I’m serving *prasadam.* Many people are coming back for more, unable to resist the comfort they have found in the few brief moments they have taken shelter of your lotus feet.

O Prabhupāda! You are the Lord’s dearest servant in His swift deliverance of souls from this ocean of birth and death! Being here, I see your mercy transcending time. During your presence you saved the lives of thousands. Now, years later, present in your instructions, you continue to give shelter to so many conditioned souls. What is this miracle of yours that unfolds before my eyes?

The risks in being here are obvious; but within your instructions is the grace of God, so what is there to fear? This morning I wondered what would happen to me if I became sick in such a place as this. I thought perhaps I’d be safer at home. But where is home for me? And where is a safe place within this material world? Your service is my only shelter. You wanted that we take all risks to preach and learn to depend fully on the Lord. You said we should never feel alone- we should know that Kṛṣṇa is always with us and that we’ll be protected by His grace.

You once said, “If Kṛṣṇa is providing for all living entities, then won’t He protect His fully surrendered devotee? “ But I know I’m not surrendered, Śrīla Prabhupāda: otherwise I’d feel no fear. Please grant me faith to remove all doubts, so that I may never hesitate in serving your instructions.

Here are also many inconveniences: gnats, mosquitoes, and sweltering heat, and whooping cough is going around. Will I try to avoid it? Or will I push aside my own concerns and first consider your mission? Once, while visiting the Paris temple, you thanked us for all the inconvenience we had encountered on your behalf. Then, after pausing after a moment, you said, “Actually, there is no inconvenience …it is all service to the Lord.” And such service to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is actually our greatest treasure.

In the beginning, I never imagined I could sleep under “a different tree each night.” Now I don’t

know how I could accept another way of life! How quickly the years have passed, leaving little time between our first cautious steps in devotional service and the immense responsibilities we have inherited as your sons and daughters.

And so I realize that actually I have come home. Here on this desolate island are my brothers and sisters-fallen souls to be reclaimed through your books and your instructions. Just see now how they’re lining up for *prasādam!* For them it’s just a little time, and for myself perhaps a little inconvenience, until they too will under-stand: you are the Lord’s dearest servant and your mercy transcends all time.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servant, at home, in Western Samoa,*  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayadhvaja Swami

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to Your Divine Grace. By your mercy we are still here to glorify this auspicious day.

Patience is trying to join Your Divine Grace for more to learn- how your dedication to the Supreme Lord has never failed.

Realization wants to meet you to understand that without Kṛṣṇa consciousness, he will go astray.

Austerity is crying for your help because she has been left without you and is being abused by materialistic men.

Blessing, wandering in different ways, is being misused by the *yogīs* who wants to be the Supreme. “ Where is Śrīla Prabhupāda?” she asks. “Where does he stay?” She wants to take shelter of Your Divine Grace.

Humility, hurt by hypocrisy, has no place to stay; please give her your shelter- the chanting of the holy name.

Unalloyed surrender, your eternal friend, is playing with you in so many ways to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the materialistic West.

Potency, weakened by materialistic demons, has surrendered unto you for service, begging for *guru’s* grace. Without deviation you have fulfilled his wish, giving Kṛṣṇa consciousness – all the world’s full of bliss!

Anger is lamenting that he can’t conquer you. Now he has surrendered to your lotus feet and is waiting to be used by you like a sword to kill the materialistic mentality of the fools and rogues.

Devotion is trying to understand how she will lose her place without serving you and Kṛṣṇa. “*Jaya* Rādhā-Mādhava,” you are singing in the class- crying the name of Kṛṣṇa, you are conquering our hearts.

Association is taking shelter in your transcendental books, but only those who are serious can see the truth. Here is Śrīla Prabhupāda! He has not gone. He is in his books. Don’t miss this opportunity; he wants to talk to you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us understand that we are not the enjoyers but that we are here to serve your transcendental mission with all our love and faith. There is nothing else for us, there is no other goal: your devotees, your ISKCON, and your books. Let us keep you in the center and thus become mature. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu had blessed you. You have done the job that no one else could accomplish- you have given Kṛṣṇa to the world!

*Your fallen servant,*  
*Jayadhvaja Swami*

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In India in 1944, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedānta Swami Prabhupāda, a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa, founded *Back to Godhead* magazine. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s spiritual master had told him, “Spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in English.” Śrīla Prabhupāda started *Back to Godhead* to carry out this command.

Later Śrīla Prabhupāda would succeed in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. But back in the 1940’s he was not yet a world-celebrated teacher but an unknown person struggling to carry out his spiritual master’s order.

Śrīla Prabhupāda began in a humble way, personally writing, typing, and editing the articles for the magazine, getting it typeset and printed, and distributing the copies to the residents of his native Calcutta. People would see him going from house to house, shop to shop, to give people *Back to Godhead* .

They began writing about Kṛṣṇa and printing on a crude second-hand office machine. And over the years *Back to Godhead* grew into a lively, colorful publication.

Now *Back to Godhead* is in transition. And the success of that transition depends on our dedication to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet.

The magazine, we are sure, is destined for success. Śrīla Prabhupāda desired it, and we doubt that Lord Kṛṣṇa would allow His pure devotee’s desire to go in vain.

But will we, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s present followers (and especially his *Back to Godhead* staff), be instruments in that success? Or will we be obstructions, instruments of passion and ignorance, like hyperactive monkeys or dull stones lying in the road?

Everything depends in our devotion to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet. It depends also on intelligence. But if we are constantly and faithfully devoted to Śrīla Prabhupāda, Kṛṣṇa from within the heart will supply the intelligence we need.

One pure devotee dedicated himself to carrying out his spiritual master’s order by broadcasting Kṛṣṇa’s message through *Back to Godhead.* If we keep the vision of that pure devotee with us and dedicate ourselves to carrying out his mission, surely we will receive his mercy, and our desire to serve him nicely will succeed.

We can’t hope that any one person will be as intensely devoted as Śrīla Prabhupāda, as expert, as determined, or as dear to Kṛṣṇa. But by sharing his vision and working together, his tiny servants can serve as instruments for carrying out his command.

Hoping to be a servant of the servants of his feet, the most unworthy  
Jayādvaita Swami

## Krishna dāsa Swami

My dear eternal spiritual master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet.

All glories, all glories, all glories unto you, the most miraculous savior, who were sent by Lord Caitanya to save the world!

Up until a few years ago my preaching activities were curtailed due to my illness, but by your kind blessings and the mercy of Lord Kṛṣṇa, I had a successful heart operation two years ago, and I am once again engaged in preaching on your behalf with all the strength that I have. Over these last two years I have had the privilege of preaching not only in the United Kingdom, but also in South Africa, Mauritius, Kenya and India.

Over the years devotees have repeatedly said that you have performed a miracle in the world. After hearing these words continually, one can sometimes take them for granted, but when I saw with my own eyes what you have achieved in South Africa and Mauritius, it became clear that no other words can describe your greatness. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have performed a miracle in the world. And not only that: your miracle hasn’t fully manifested itself yet- for your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is destined to continue to grow and develop and unfold over the next ten thousand years.

When I was in South Africa, I was wonderstruck at our beautiful Temple of Understanding. I could not stop admiring it; even when I returned late at night from programs, I would stop at the gates and gaze at it. Whenever I was speaking to someone individually or lecturing at a program, I would not miss the opportunity to praise you: ”This temple is the glory of ISKCON; this temple is the glory of Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

Outside India I have not seen any temple that is as beautiful and as majestic as this one.

In Mauritius I came to know of your prediction that it would become the first Kṛṣṇa conscious country in the world. I was overjoyed to hear this, and even more so when I saw how hard and sincerely the devotees there are working to fulfill this prediction.

These were my firsthand experiences in just two countries of the world, so what to speak of the rest. When you first came to India with your American disciples, the country was buzzing with the words “The Americans are coming! The Americans are coming!” Early last year you repeated this scene and gave us yet another glimpse of your great miracle. On this occasion, for the first time in modern history, Russian Vaiṣṇavas came to India on a pilgrimage. Not five or ten or twenty, but fifty in the first party and others later. This time the country was buzzing with the words “The Russians are coming! The Russians are coming! “ Who could have imagined that by making only one disciple in Russia on your one and only visit to that country, this would be the result?

Throughout the world you have thousands of wonderful devotees dedicated to helping you unfold your wonderful miracle. They are rendering you great service. I myself, however, am a completely third-class *kaniṣṭha adhikārī* devotee who has somehow been blessed by you to serve you in my own small way. May I be so bold as to beg you to please continue blessing me so that I can continue to work for you and thus play my insignificant role in your miracle.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it was you yourself who persuaded me to join you in your great preaching movement. That first meeting with you at Bhaktivedānta Manor was the greatest event in my life, as you pulled me out of the complexities of family life. For that great mercy shown toward this lowly soul, let me reaffirm to you that I will continue to serve you to the best of my ability with all my heart, all my mind, and all my soul.

*Your insignificant servant.*  
Kṛṣṇa dasa Swami

## Lokanātha Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious occasion of your appearance day.

During the last year, part of the time I was involved with *dharma-yuddha-* the ISKCON v. Robin George legal case, which is giving us a chance to fight for your ISKCON’s religious rights. Some of us were not sure whether to push on with this fight. However, from the examples of similar cases that you had dealt with personally, it became evident that we had to keep fighting. Once you said. “Kṛṣṇa Himself became important when he killed so many demons, not simply by lying down in the lap of Mother Yaśodā.”

In 1977, when you heard of how our movement was being attacked in the courts and press as a psychologically dangerous brainwashing cult, you were not intimidated by the attack of the anti-cult movement. “As soon as you speak of God, this opposition will come,” you said. “Jesus Christ was crucified. They were so kind- they have not crucified me or my men. But you have to expect all these things. Nityānanda Prabhu was personally injured. Haridāsa Ṭhākura was beaten in twenty-two bazaars. This task is like that.”

You took opposition as an indication of success, for it demonstrated the validity of your movement. You said, “Devotees should be very alert and intelligently defend the Kṛṣṇa movement. The real fight is now. Kṛṣṇa will give you protection. So, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and fight. You don’t sleep. Never did Kṛṣṇa say to Arjuna, ‘I am your friend, I am God, You sleep here. I will do everything.’ No! You must fight! That is wanted. Kṛṣṇa said, ‘Fight and remember me; then I will do everything.’ This is an opportunity to remember Kṛṣṇa always.”

So on your order we continued the battle, and we are in the thick of it. We are in danger of losing five of your temples in America. We are trying our best, trying to work as vigilantly and intelligently as possible. But even if worse comes to worst and we lose some temples, under no circumstances do we want to lose our Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If there is some apparent defeat, it will be like Lord Nṛsiṁhadevaṣ giving Hiraṇyakaśipu a chance to slip from his clutches. Kṛṣṇa consciousness, being an affair of the spiritual soul, is all spiritual, and so the materialists have no weapon to defeat or crush that consciousness.

The verdict of the court may or may not go in our favor. However, we have already received tons of favorable media coverage. Prominent Christian organizations, as well as Hindu ones, are showing sympathy and a willingness to support our movement, and we are receiving financial backing from hundreds and hundreds of individuals across the nation. We foresee many good things coming out of the fight. We take this case as a blessing in disguise.

So, on this auspicious occasion of your ninety-fourth anniversary, we beg you to bless us with the proper consciousness –Kṛṣṇa consciousness – so that we can continue to defend your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement always and everywhere.

This material body in general and the Western world in particular will certainly continue to oppose your movement, because it strongly opposes illicit sex, meat-eating, intoxication, and gambling. The Westerners are feeling threatened and are scared of their kingdom of illusion getting smashed to pieces. We are soldiers of Caitanya Mahāprabhu and you are the commander in chief of our army. We are prepared to give a tough fight to our opponents, the anti-cultists, and Caitanya Mahāprabhu has guaranteed victory for our side: *paraṁ vijayate śri-Kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam.*

This is our chance to prove that we are a bona fide religion. Of course, materialistic civilization will try to uproot us. However, the Western world has no choice but to embrace and accommodate the movement of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. It’s too late. Five hundred years ago Caitanya Mahāprabhu predicted that the holy name would reach every town and village, all over the world. To fulfill this prediction Caitanya Mahāprabhu chose you to be His instrument for taking the gift of the holy name all over the world. The idea of propagating and establishing the holy name all over the world is not your idea; it is God’s. Therefore who on earth can stop it? Now they should simply learn to live with it.

Unfortunately, some of the children of God are really childlike. A child does not realize the value of medicine; so when a physician tries to administer it, the child simply denounces him or sometimes even becomes violent and attacks his well-wishing physician or his parents. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are everyone’s well-wisher; you have no other intention but to make everyone happy. Even hippies became happies by coming in contact with your movement. It is the only movement on the surface of the globe that is fully equipped with the topmost practical and eternal philosophy to respiritualize the entire human civilization, and this is your matchless gift to the world.

We are always amazed by your positive outlook. Here is an example: You said, “This is the beauty of Vṛndāvana: When Kṛṣṇa entered the Yamunā to fight with Kāliya, it was not at all good news for Mother Yaśodā, Nanda, and their friends and families. Not at all. Their lives were lost- but still Kṛṣṇa was the center. This is Vṛndāvana – in everything Kṛṣṇa is the center.

“So our situation is like that. They are making bad propaganda against Kṛṣṇa- this is the opposition- but I am happy that Kṛṣṇa is in the center of it all. This is the beauty of this movement. Although we are put into some difficulty, yet the center is Kṛṣṇa.”

By your grace, I am feeling enthused and inspired to continue to serve your movement. I am very grateful that you have engaged me all these years. I have no aim in life other than to keep serving like this, life after life, eternally. My heartfelt thanks to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Eternally yours,*  
Lokanātha Swami

## Mahānidhi Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Prabhupāda and Kṛṣṇa.**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, being a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa, you are inseparable from the Supreme Lord. Thoughts of your divine activities immediately bring remembrance of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Govinda. Similarly, the more we try to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa, the more we think of you.

You kindly gave us Kṛṣṇa’s holy names. By chanting dutifully, we remember your order, our promise, and the dancing form of Lord Śyāmasundara. It’s true, you told us, that Lord Gopīnātha would dance on the tongue of one with a purified heart. While glorifying Kṛṣṇa we also cry out to you –“Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda” – and see you playing precision *mṛdaṅga* or honey-sweet harmonium for the pleasure of Vṛndāvana-naṭabara, Śrī Kṛṣṇacandra. Prabhupāda, you remain ever attendant wherever Lord Kṛṣṇa dances.

While distributing *Kṛṣṇa* books we proudly present your photograph before showing the spiritual sky to the conditioned souls. We tell them, “ This is Śrīla Prabhupāda, the author of this wonderful book about the superexcellent pastimes of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.”

We begin to conclude our diurnal studies of Kṛṣṇa’s immortal teachings by offering obeisances to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our ever well-wisher. Whatever Kṛṣṇa speaks, Prabhupāda’s purports powerfully preach.

Before commencing *pūjā* we prayerfully petition Prabhupāda’s permission. Śrī Giri-govardhana accepts our offerings through your hands. After spiritually relishing, you distribute the *mahā-prasādam* to nourish our souls and maintain our existence. The undeserved fortune of being able to honor *Kṛṣṇa-prasādam* comes from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. When the spiritual master calls, the disciple may then take *Kṛṣṇa-prasādam.* While we enjoy the Lord’s remnants, our gratitude deepens for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

For Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure, during *maṅgala-ārati* we glorify the *guru* with the *Gurvaṣṭaka* prayers. Singing your morning tune, we sway in saffron to the swami step. Because of you we are awake at 4.00 a.m., singing for your Deity of Kṛṣṇa, in your temple, in your ISKCON, with your disciples, in your eternal home, Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma.

We are forever thankful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us the blissful process of chanting, dancing, and taking *Kṛṣṇa-prasādam* as the easy and sublime method for attaining pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In the purport to the *bhajan* “Gaurnāṅgera Du ‘ṭi Pada” you state, “The heart of anyone who takes to this movement of chanting, dancing, and eating *Kṛṣṇa-prasādam* will surely be cleansed of all dirty contamination.” To emphasize the point you declare in the “*Parama Karuna”* purport: “Lord Caitanya and Nityānanda introduced a process that is simply joyful- simply chant and dance. Don’t think that this chanting and dancing will not lead to the desired goal. It will. It is the assurance of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu that one will get all perfection by this process.”

To serve Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa means to be with you, and to serve you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, means to live forever with Kṛṣṇa. On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, I pray for the privilege to be your eternal servant.

*Your worthless servant,*  
Mahānidhi Swami

## Nava-yogendra Swami

My dearmost beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotuslike feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who have very kindly taken the unlimited trouble to found the International Society for Krishna Consciousness to save humanity from going to the dogs.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is your appearance day, and yesterday was the appearance day of your Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, actually Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is yours. Lord Kṛṣṇa is very dear to you, and you are very dear to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. If you like, you can give Śrī Kṛṣṇa to anyone. In truth, you gave Śrī Kṛṣṇa to the whole world. No one knew about Śrī Kṛṣṇa; even in the land of Śrī Kṛṣṇa-i.e., India- people have forgotten about Śrī Kṛṣṇa. So-called readers of *Bhagavad-gīta* also did not know the real importance of Śrī Kṛṣṇa-that He is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and that He has no other cause because He is the cause of all causes. You are the only one, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who made this known to everyone throughout the whole world . This is very important for everyone to know; otherwise their lives are useless. The purpose of life is to know Śrī Kṛṣṇa, worship Him, become His devotee, and attain His lotus feet. Otherwise life in this world is very risky. If we go back into the cycle of birth and death and spend, on the average, one year in each lifetime, then it may take eight million years to come back to the human life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have made everything clear to us about the purpose of life and the importance of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. There is no doubt about it. Now, on your appearance day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless me so that I can become your true follower. This is possible only by your mercy. Kindly be merciful to this most fallen of all your disciples. Kindly free me from all vices, give me the power of tolerance, and allow me to be a little instrument in your great ISKCON.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing me to make this offering at the dust of your lotuslike feet.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

*Your most fallen servant,*  
Nava-yogendra Swami

## Pāraṅgacit Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Because he is covered by dense ignorance, the living entity cannot see this world in its real perspective. With uncontrolled mind and senses, the soul is forced to suffer in the cycle of birth and death. And this is my own condition. But understanding this situation, you came and took me out of danger, revealing the process of devotional service. This is your mercy, without a doubt.

As Prahlāda Mahārāja says,

*Prāyeṇa deva munahaḥ sva-vimukti-kāmā*  
*Maunaṁ canranti vijane na parārtha-niṣṭhāh*  
*Naitān vihāya kṛpanān vimumukṣa eko*  
*Nānyaṁ tvad asya śaraṇaṁ bhramato ‘ nupaśye*

**“My dear Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva, I see that there are many saintly persons indeed, but they are interested only in their own deliverance. Not caring for the big cities and towns, they go to the Himalayas or the forest to meditate with vows of silence \[*****mauna-vrata*****\] . They are not interested in delivering others. As for me, however, I do not wish to be liberated alone, leaving aside all these poor fools and rascals. I know that without Kṛṣṇa consciousness, without taking shelter of Your lotus feet, one cannot be happy. Therefore, I wish to bring them back to shelter at Your lotus feet.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.9.44*****).***

It is not possible to feel true mercy toward the suffering living entities in this world if we do not feel true mercy toward the suffering living entities in the world if we do not give up selfish desires. And this is your eternal example. Your only wish is that everybody may go back home, back to Godhead, and solely with this purpose you founded the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You came to reveal the Supreme Personality of Godhead and His eternal transcendental pastimes, your purpose being to take everyone out of the darkness of impersonalism. You came to reveal that there is life beyond death, and you also explained the mysteries of the law of *karma.* All this you reveled in your books, and thus by distributing these books to suffering mankind we are performing the highest activity, as you constantly preach in the same books.

You have said that no disciple should think that someday it will be possible for him to repay the debt to his spiritual master. No, such a debt cannot be paid back. Therefore, I beg that during the short period left for this body I can dedicate myself to the distribution of your books. This is the least I can do to please you, because this is the purpose of your appearance. Please give me enough potency and intelligence to be always engaged in serving your eternal mission by serving the servants of the servants of your servants. Distribution of your books for the benefit of mankind is the true light you have given to guide ISKCON in its mission. You gave us the secret to overcome all problems that may appear in the future: profuse distribution of your books, on a huge scale. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, keep me always engaged in your mission.

*Your eternal unqualified servant,*  
Pāraṅgacti Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*Om ajnana-timirandhasya jnananjana-salakaya*  
*Caksur unmilitam yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namah*

In your books you have explained, “The art of focusing one’s attention on the Supreme and giving one’s love to Him is called Kṛṣṇa consciousness. “ Mercifully, you have given us your transcendental books and the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement to help us learn this art and thus rise to the platform of spontaneous love for Kṛṣṇa, whereby our lives will actually become peaceful, happy, and truly successful.

One time, while I was fanning you in San Francisco, I felt the atmosphere ecstatically permeated with your compassion when you said, “ We just want people to become happy.” What is the method of becoming happy? Your books explain, “Satisfaction of the self-realized spiritual master is the secret of advancement in spiritual life.” Who is a bona fide spiritual master? “ A person in the disciplic succession \[who\] can convey the message of the Lord as it is to his disciple.” How to satisfy the spiritual master? “ A spiritual master should be accepted in full surrender, and one should serve the spiritual master like a menial servant, without false prestige… One must be able to pass the test of the spiritual master, and when he sees the genuine desire of the disciple, he automatically blesses the disciple with genuine spiritual understanding.”

Clear understanding of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, obtained from a bona fide spiritual master, will enable us to act in such clear consciousness that Lord Kṛṣṇa will be pleased to sit in our hearts and give us His transcendental directions. It is our primary duty as devotees to learn how to properly render service unto Kṛṣṇa, under the direction of a spiritual master, and thus be recognized by Him.

You are the perfect example, Śrīla Prabhupāda, of a devotee completely attached to Lord Kṛṣṇa and to serving His mission. And the best service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, as you have many times told us, is to preach His message through a Kṛṣṇa conscious cultural revolution –through art, theater, music, and especially by distributing books- a revolution in which we can engage all our energies in trying to convince people that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. If we fight with determination and intelligence in the battlefield of preaching, under the guidance of the great *mahā-rathīs* like you and the Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, surely *māyā* will be defeated and *Kṛṣṇa-prema* will flood our hearts and the hearts of many other suffering conditioned souls in this universe.

*Your servant,*  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rāma-Govinda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Kindly accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this auspicious day.

Although I am unable to glorify you adequately, I want to have a transcendental taste for glorifying your activities. Whatever I speak, however, is always inadequate and very insignificant. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you are the incarnation of the mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, all your activities are liberal and laudable.

It is said in the *Śrīmad Bhāgavatam* (6.17.31),

*Vāsudeve bhagavati bhaktim udvahatāṁ nṛṇām*  
*Jṅnāna-vairāgya-vīryāṇāṁ na hi kaścid vyapāśrayaḥ*

**“Persons engaged in devotional service to Lord Vāsudeva, Kṛṣṇa, have naturally perfect knowledge and detachment from this material world. Therefore such devotees are not interested in the so-called happiness or so-called distress of this world.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the perfect example of the person mentioned above. You wrote to one of your disciples on September 6, 1977, “Now I have come to the West in a broken condition of health. In such condition no one would think of touring. An ordinary man would prepare for death. But I am not afraid of death, so why not let me see once again my beloved disciples and the temples all over the world.”

Of course, as you imply in this letter, you are not an ordinary man. It is said in the *Śrīmad Bhāgavatam (*4.22.15), *tad ahaṁ kṛta-viśrambhaḥ suhṛdo vas tapasvinām*: “I am completely assured that personalities like you are the only friends for persons who are blazing in the fire of material existence. “

Your illness was getting very severe in September 1977, and Your Divine Grace wrote, “In such conditions no one would think of touring.” But you traveled for the benefit of everyone. The *Bhāgavatam* glorifies such mercy as follows:

*Vyaktam ātmā bhagavān ātma-bhāvanaḥ*  
*Svānām anugrahāyemāṁ siddha-rūpī caraty ajaḥ*

**“The Supreme Personality of Godhead is always anxious to elevate the living entities, who are His parts and parcels, and for their special benefit the Lord travels all over the world in the form of self-realized persons like you.” (*****Bhāg*****. 4.22.16)**

Even though Your Divine Grace has gone back to Kṛṣṇaloka, you are still traveling throughout the whole world via the medium of your traveling preachers, who are faithfully spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement to every corner of the world, as is desired by Lord Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu. As one newspaper wrote in Calcutta, “Only Chaitanyaism can solve the world’s problems.” You have founded ISKCON just to do that.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the following line from the *Caitanya-candrodaya-nāṭaka* (9.152), you have revealed to us that renunciation is a goddess: *eṣa eva tarnṇiṁ vairāgya-lakṣmīṁ dadhe.* “Sanātana Gosvāmi) gave up everything just to accept the youthful goddess of renunciation. “ Now, because of your causeless mercy, I have accepted from her the saffron cloth. Now I can offer to you all my heart and, with her help, make for you a beautiful *vyāsāsana* in the innermost chamber of my heart.

Please, Your Divine Grace, sit in that chamber so that I can start offering *pāda-pūjā* to worship your divine lotus feet. Vairāgya-lakṣmī has requested four items from me for washing your lotus feet. These are *karma, mana, vākya, and jīva:* my activities, which should direct everyone to your lotus feet; my mind, which should think of how to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement; my words, which should convince people to take up the chanting of the holy name- the Hare Kṛṣṇa mahā-mantra – to read your books, and to take shelter of your movement ; and my very existence, which should be dedicated exclusively to your service. All these items have to be offered, one after the other, at your lotus feet. So that I may remember this, Vairāgya-lakṣmī has given me a *tri-daṇḍa* to carry all the time. By offering these four items I may someday become pure enough to help you perform your service to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

In this Vyāsa-pūjā ārati, the lamp is offered to you by giving them Vaiṣṇava dress; and the sound of the conchshell is offered to you by beating loudly on the Bṛhat Mṛdaṅga, the nonstop printing and distribution of your books all over the world. The garland offered to you is the lotuslike hearts of the persons who become devotees and offer you their hearts and souls.

I request you to let me be an instrument in your hands for the propagation of the movement of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

*Your lowly servant,*  
Rāma Govinda Swami

## Rohiṇī-kumāra Swami

The sky of my heart  
Was engulfed  
In the most profound darkness.  
Even the tiniest glimmer of knowledge  
Had long ago been extinguished by the cold, oppressive ignorance  
Which had seeped into my heart,  
Filling every nook and cranny.
