Out of desperation,  
I thought that the Buddhist void  
Might provide an escape  
Form this deplorable condition.  
Alas! I found it to be  
Even colder and darker  
Than the ignorance  
In my own heart.  
Then one day  
The darkness of my heart  
Was shattered.  
A beam of light  
Pierced the clouds  
And illuminated  
The four quarters  
Of the land of my heart.  
Your eyes  
Rested upon me,  
Like the sun and moon,  
Bestowing your mercy upon me.  
I was shocked  
Because you saw me  
Not as what I really am –  
A helpless soul  
Floundering in the morass  
Of bodily identification.  
Seeing the great concern for me  
In your brilliant lotus eyes,  
I was obliged  
To offer myself at your lotus feet  
As the unqualified servant that I am.  
You set me in the path for going back home.  
What more can I say?  
With exuberant joy  
Tinged with the subtle flavor  
Of sadness due to separation,  
I marvel at the world’s good fortune  
For your appearance  
On this auspicious day.

*Your servant,*  
Rohiṇīi-kumāra Swami

## Romapāda Swami

My beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. As we come before you each year in this Vyāsa-pūjā day, very powerful feelings awaken in the hearts of your disciples and numberless followers. I, for one, am particularly captured by joy and solemnity. Contradictory characteristics are resolved in the Supreme, we learn from Śrīla Rūpā Gosvāmī. Similarly, in your glory I find the same resolution.

The scriptures teach us that the spiritual master is always to be worshipped with awe and reverence. Especially on the occasion of the spiritual master’s Vyāsa-pūjā, reverence, grandeur, pomp, and majesty know no bounds of the faithful disciple, whose ardor of glorification cannot be excessive. You are the *object* of our homage, it is *you* who we want to see pleased and singularly worshiped; yet *our own* joy, while so engaged, multiplies. Such are the affairs of the heart connected with a perfect person like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your love is selfless.

I want to express a long-overdue appreciation of my relationship with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Although you are *already* my devotional pulse-beat, still, given the message I wish to express, formalizing it in a Vyāsa-pūjā address is perfectly apropos.

For many years, even while you were on the planet manifesting your preaching pastimes, I served you with the mood of separation. “Wherever Śrīla Prabhupāda is now,” I would think, “writing his books or preaching to dignitaries or inspiring his followers, I have one hundred percent conviction that this missionary activities are my life and soul. His pleasure is to be sought therein, and my lifetime dedication to his mission is my life’s perfection.” I felt that merely being engaged by you in this way was your great mercy upon me, and my indebtedness was to be shown by keeping myself wrapped within your words of instruction, dutifully serving your cause.

My constant inspiration consisted of your descriptions, both in your lectures and your books, of the preacher/Vaiṣṇava who travels across the face of the earth only to distribute the mercy of Lord Caitanya. And you, the flawless *ācārya,* set the perfect example.

One curious feature of your instructions and behavior always puzzles me. You taught us that while preaching we should be careful to allow only other Vaiṣṇavas to touch our feet, lest we become ill by having sinful reactions transferred to us from unholy persons. Yet I also can remember your undisturbed poise in permitting admirers to come forward, during *guru-puja* or at any time, to touch your lotus feet. Each time I saw this, I could not help thinking, “ Why don’t these people take the *real mercy* Śrīla Prabhupāda has to offer by responding to his appeal to help him directly in his missionary work? This is where Prabhupāda’s real desire lies. If they actually want his mercy, why do they not take it in the manner that he is most abundantly offering it?” And similarly, when initiated devotees tried to touch your feet or crowd you, pushing and packing themselves uncomfortably around you, I would wonder, “Don’t these *prabhus* know that they have Prabhupāda’s mercy already? Why don’t they recognize the full satisfaction they can derive from taking fully to his missionary work, instead of giving him physical discomfort in these various ways?”

From recent studying of your books, however, new light has been shed on the importance of the dust from the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava. Prahlāda Mahārāja declares that the essential ingredient in making one eligible for pure devotional service is the dust form a pure devotee’s feet. And Rūpa Gosvāmī discusses the same as an impetus for ecstatic love of Godhead: “Those who continuously bear the dust of the lotus feet of the Lord upon their heads, Caitanya Mahāprabhu describes, maintain the position of empowered *jīvas.”*

Also, over the past few months I have been very inspired to enter into a spirit of various daily practices and devotional activities with the hope of passing beyond the routine, mechanical aspects and trying to contemplate more deeply the devotional expression and meanings of those activities. *Guru-puja* is one of them.

Much like Vyāsa-pūjā, *guru-puja* is a regular ceremony for respecting the *guru.* We sing *Śrī-guru karuṇā-sindhu, adhama janāra bandhu:* The spiritual master is an ocean of mercy. He is the friend \[bandhu\] of those who are fallen\[adhama\].” Confirmed. You are pouring down your mercy on all, for you are like an ocean of mercy, giving mercy indiscriminately to everyone. And I am one such entity receiving that mercy. Who can deny it? But, *hā hā Prabhu koro doyā, deho more pāda-chāyā: “* O Master, be merciful unto me. Give me the shade of your lotus feet.” My appreciation of you has always been that you possessed an overflowing, unlimited capacity for mercy for all living entities, all over the face of the planet.

As the bearer of unprecedented *Kṛṣṇa-śacti* for spreading love of God, combined with your dimensionless compassion, you single-handedly started a worldwide spiritual revolution that will continue to transform the lives of millions for generations. Just a spark of your brilliant mercy can bring a fallen soul to fully surrender his life’s energy to the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. I am the recipient of one such spark of your mercy. And I am coming before you today as a greedy soul asking for even more mercy. Please grant me the blessing of allowing me to place upon my head the particles of dust from your lotus feet, so that I may further progress toward life’s ultimate goal, *Kṛṣṇa-prema.* You can consider me your property; you have my ongoing commitment to serve you lifelong, heart and soul. But my growing hankering is to catch up the fullness of your message, the fullness of your mercy, as soon as possible.

For this I need your special favor. Take my life’s dedication and be kind upon this insignificant beggar.

*Your servant,*  
Romapāda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It happens regularly that I have to pause in my service and think: What a wonder your movement is, a practical example of Kṛṣṇa’s presence and a stunning proof of your being Kṛṣṇa’s very dear associate, who have mercifully come to deliver the most wonderful gifts to a lost living entity like me, suffering endless pain in the clutches of birth and death.

You said, “ We will have our ISKCON in the spiritual world.” Kṛṣṇa wants to fulfill the words of his pure devotee-especially your words. That is why your mission is equipped with so much power –outside the realm of *karma,* which creates countries and grinds them to dust. Anyone who is getting in contact with Kṛṣṇa consciousness is immediately empowered with a previously known feeling of joy, confidence, and spiritual strength. (Lately we saw thousands of Roumanians, who had no previous knowledge of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, dance and chant in ecstasy in our programs. Hundreds of Germans are taking up chanting beads. Millions of books are being distributed.)

We also witnessed how Kṛṣṇa prepares the world as a stage for your preaching movement to enact its pastimes by changing the political situation in Russia and Europe in such an amazing way that even the political observers are at a complete loss to explain the forces behind what is happening.

But the transcendental power of your mission does not manifest itself only in such visible successes. No, for ISKCON to go on in the spiritual world we all have to be purified from *māyā’s* influences- gross and subtle. With great awe I can see the purification going on –both on the level of the movement and even in my own muddy heart .

We can practically see how all your prophecies, words, inspirations, etc, are like an illuminating strongly-built highway leading through the dark forest of the material world, with its dangerous inhabitants. If, due to *māyā* which sometimes overcasts our mind by foggy clouds of illusion, we slightly step off the highway guarded by you, we are surrounded almost instantly by mischievous robbers called lust, anger, greed, envy, illusion, and madness, who begin to play their cruel games on us. But like a loving father, you are immediately there if we shout your name. Powerfully you drive away the enemies and dangers and bring us back on the safe road.

As the years go by I can feel my desire to be with you increase, and I want to turn to you. When I read your wonderful books you answer my personal questions, show me how to preach, and, what is more, show me the realm of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, where the miseries of the material world do not exist.

When I hear your tapes, your strengthening voice instills your conviction and enthusiasm into me just as much as the philosophy. When hearing you, I feel close to you.

By seeing your wonderful photos, I feel affection and sometimes chastisement. By hearing from the advanced Vaiṣṇavas who have been elevated and formed by your “magical touch,” I receive clear guidance and great hope.

When I am doing the service you gave me, with my consciousness fixed on you, I feel sheltered and sometimes empowered. (If I could only do it in a way more satisfying to you!)

As you receive this offering, I hope you are a little bit pleased with my imperfect attempts to glorify you from my heart. I am painfully aware that I fall short of your expectations. Therefore on this day I turn to you again with only empty hands and an earnest request.

I know that, just like a bulb that has meaning only in connection with the socket, I am rightly placed only as a humble servant at your lotus feet, attentive to all your instructions, indications, and desires.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, use me as an instrument in your divine preaching mission. Help me see things in your way. Help me decide on matters as you would like me to. Help me guide others to your lotus feet and keep them linked up with you. Help me preach in a way that satisfies you. Please allow me the benediction of not wasting time anymore. And finally, please help me become more pure, so that your bestowing these benedictions on me is not like pouring ghee on ashes.

*Your fallen and humble servant,*  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Satsvarūpa dasa Goswami

**Prabhupāda Gems**

In recent years Śrīla Prabhupāda’s association has become more available to us through printed volumes of his letters, conversations, and lectures. And Prabhupāda’s books are printed in new deluxe editions. The tape recordings of Prabhupāda speaking provide a special intimacy of association. And so there is no reason to lament that we cannot be with Śrīla Prabhupāda through the medium of his instructions.

Still, despite so much access to Prabhupāda’s written and spoken words, we may sometimes feel we lack sufficient time to hear form Prabhupāda. Another challenge is to listen to Prabhupāda with a submissive and clear receptivity. It is the nature of Kali-yuga that we often find ourselves disturbed and pushed along by events. When we do find a little time to read Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books, we sometimes do it at breakneck speed. When I find myself that I am not trying to “finish” Prabhupada books, or to complete a numerical quota of pages. More important than that is the spiritual quality of the time we spend in hearing from Śrīla Prabhupāda. With the proper mood, we can go before Prabhupāda and inquire from him and then listen to him in response, as he speaks to us all through his books and lectures. As we slow down and try to hear better, we find ourselves more deeply impressed with Prabhupāda’s statements, and we remember them better.

I would like to share a couple of Prabhupāda gems, which stood out in my mind during recent attempts to hear from His Divine Grace.

Since I have been trying to understand better how to always pray to Prabhupāda and Kṛṣṇa, I was particularly struck to hear Prabhupāda give essential advice on this. This occurred in a lecture given in Detroit on August 7, 1975. Śrīla Prabhupāda said,

“The only prayer to Kṛṣṇa is, ‘Dear Lord, please give me the strength to serve You.’ Any other prayer, you will never be happy.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for this wonderful prayer!

And as I have been attempting to improve my *japa,* Śrīla Prabhupāda spoke to that need and astonished me once again with the fact that he is speaking exactly to all the needs of his disciples. From a lecture Śrīla Prabhupāda gave on June 15, 1973:

“If you are engaged *satatam* twenty- four hours, without any other engagement, *sarva-dharmān parityajya –* giving up all other nonsense and business-simply if you are engaged in Kṛṣṇa’s business, *prīti-pūrvakam-* with love. Not as hackneyed,\[thinking\] “This is my duty, chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa. All right, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa…” \[Prabhupāda deliberately slurs the *mantra.\]* Not like that –with *prīti,* with love. Chant every name –Hare, Kṛṣṇa- and hear. Here is Kṛṣṇa. Here is Rādhārāṇī. That kind of chanting, quality. Not ‘Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa….’\[Prabhupāda slurs and mumbles again.\] Not like that, not like that. *Prītī. Tesāṁ satata-yuktānāṁ bhajatāṁ prīti.* That *prīti* is required. That is the essential quality.”

Later in the same lecture Prabhupāda said that the real ingredient of any offering is *bhakti.* Therefore when we chant, the real ingredient is our love. Prabhupāda said, “This is *mantra,* real *mantra.* Not so many formalities of *mantra.* Kṛṣṇa does not say, ‘One who offers Me with the Vedic *mantras.’* He never says that. Kṛṣṇa says *bhaktyā –* with devotion.”

With this memorable quote in mind, I may try better, and at least I will not content myself with thinking that mechanical chanting is itself the offering of *mantra*. But real *mantra* means with devotion, and then Kṛṣṇa will be pleased to reveal Himself as *nāma-rūpa.*

I pray to Śrīla Prabhupāda and Kṛṣṇa that I may always stay in the company of their devotees. And when we associate, let us share Prabhupāda gems.

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. You saved me from the miserable existence of speculating over what spiritual life actually is. If you had not come, where would I be now? In the darkness of material existence.

A common idea of spiritual life is that a life of peace and calmness. You showed that peacefulness can be had not by retiring in the forest but by acting for the sake of pleasing *guru* and Kṛṣṇa. If you would have peacefully stayed in Vṛndāvana, I would have been lost. That is your great mercy upon me and the whole world – that you were prepared to undergo so many difficulties in order to fulfill the order of your spiritual master. This great mercy of yours is wonderful to contemplate.

Recently, in the letter books, I read a letter of yours in which you stress this point of endeavoring for the sake of preaching. The words you use are “strenuous endeavor.” And in the conversations book I read the example of the *yogī* in the cold water. A *yogī* claimed he could stay warm in the cold water the whole night. If he managed to do that, a rich man promised to give him some reward. When the morning came and the *yogī* had proven his capability, the rich man avoided paying by some cheap excuse, claiming that the *yogī* had taken heat from a lamp visible very far away. A servant of the rich man proved the rich man wrong by trying to cook his food in a pot hanging from the high ceiling, with the fire on the floor. Of course, that is not possible: if you want to cook something, your pot has to be in the fire or right on top of it. The point you made was that we similarly have to keep ourselves in the fire of devotional service if we want to make any progress in devotional life.

You are the perfect example of someone who did not lay back but instead fully embraced all kinds of difficulties in order to execute the mission of your Guru Mahārāja. Thus we have all been saved from the turmoil of material existence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I prostrate myself at your divine lotus feet on this most auspicious day, begging for your blessings so that I will not be an unworthy disciple of yours.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

## Somaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept a million times my prostrated obeisances unto your divine lotus feet.

My dear lord and master, you are so merciful that you descended from the abode of Kṛṣṇa to save us all from this terrible ocean of birth and death. You came to this horrible Western world while Kali-yuga was in full swing, in this terrible twentieth century, amongst the hippies, drunkards, etc., and built a golden ship to take us out of the ocean of birth and death.

You expertly built the golden ISKCON ship in a little storefront on Second Avenue in New York, and from there you sailed it to every corner of the world. From all the corners more ships came, and in no time you had an immense fleet. All this was done due to your strong desire to save every living entity within this material world.

Simultaneously many strong winds came, opposing your fleet. The winds of atheism, impersonalism, opportunism, communism, etc., all tried to block your way, but you were such an expert captain that you easily defeated all opposition. You always came out victorious, and thus you kept the fleet going by your expert steering of the ship.

Your divine spiritual master, His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Goswami Mahārāja, also had a very strong fleet. His crew was composed of very qualified men, all born in Bhārata-varṣa. They were fired-up, staunch *brahmacārīs,* but upon the disappearance of the captain a strong wind came, and the ships sank.

So, knowing what happens when the spiritual master leaves the planet, you enacted a very wonderful pastime. Apparently you left us, but it was all a trick. Yes! It was all a trick. You never left us: you remained at the helm, thus becoming the invisible captain of the ISKCON ship.

After your apparent disappearance, fire started in the ship and heavy winds came- the winds of inexperience, immaturity, pride, ambition, attachment to name, fame and glory, false prestige, etc. These winds severely shook the ship. For many years the ship was shaking, shaking, shaking, but you, the invisible captain, were there, keeping the ship afloat.

Now almost thirteen years have passed, and the golden ship of ISKCON, the same one you constructed in the little storefront in New York, is still sailing, defeating all kinds of opposition, making new ships manned by new devotee crewmen. It is incredible and wonderful how the ship keeps going despite your apparent disappearance. But we know that you are still with us, guiding the ship.

This battle against *māyā* is a heavy one. Big clashing. So many of your men fell off the ship into the waves of the ocean; so many men are wounded. The winds are blowing harder, the waves are rising higher, but the ship keeps going. How is that? Because you, the invisible captain, are still at the helm.

My dear lord and master, millions of times I bow down at your divine lotus feet and say, again and again, Thank you, thank you for having taken this invisible form, for still staying with us and steering the ship.

Slowly, as time passes, we are becoming wiser, humbler, and simpler, and shaking is diminishing.

Let us all become humble oarsmen, and you, the divine captain, will give us the rhythm with your Bṛhat Mṛdaṅga - “Bam bam, row row!” – and thus we will keep the ship secure. No more shaking, no more fire. Let us conquer the world under the expert guidance of the invisible captain.

By your mercy I am still here, sitting in the far southwest corner of the ship, trying to learn how to row and watching an expert invisible captain steer the ship, keeping it afloat.

Thank you Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you a million times. Thank you for keeping me here, thank you for taking your invisible form to stay at the helm, keeping the golden ship of ISKCON afloat on course.

*Your most insignificant and useless rower,*  
Somaka Swami

## Śrīdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your two lotus feet.

These two lotus feet of yours are wonderful. When I think of my relationship with them, I am reminded of the disciple of Thirumaṅgai ālvār who could place his feet on the shadow of any person and prevent him from going anywhere.

You are also demonstrating this *siddhi* with your two lotus feet. Although after twenty years of struggle I still have no attraction for Kṛṣṇa of His holy name, you are so merciful that you are keeping me in the association of your disciples.

When I see the immense power of *māyā,* I sometimes think that it is only a matter of time before I succumb to her alluring call. How am I maintaining even a semblance of devotional service? My only answer is that you have placed your two lotus feet on my shadow and are keeping me here for your higher purpose.

As the story goes, Thirumaṅai ālvār, with help from four disciples who had *siddhis,* maintained a band of thieves and rogues to achieve his exalted goal of building a temple for Raṅganātha. However, when the band became envious and inimical toward him, they outgrew their utility and were rejected.

You are the most merciful because you tolerate so many offenses by your disciples. I, for one, am trying to serve you, but I simply create disturbance. I am simply amazed by your compassion.

Therefore I pray to you to keep me forever (even if in this material world) in your association through service at your two lotus feet.

*Your humble servant,*  
Śrīdhara Swami

## Suhotra Swami

**Deliverance from a Dark Universe**

In the beginning of time, on a lotus of gold,  
Brahmā, creator, did the darkness behold.  
But hearing the Lord, he reflected within,  
And receiving knowledge sublime, the destroyer of sin.  
Brahmā then spoke, four Vedas to teach,  
The form of the cosmos now within easy reach.  
A universe new-born, full of hope and quite pure,  
Did displace the darkness, as if it would ever endure.  
In those days of yore, magnificence prevailed,  
Vedic truth the foundation that all beings hailed.  
But now the world’s filled with error; what is truth we must guess-  
It seems that men who are evil are the ones who knew best.  
Over a hundred trillion years have past since day one;  
The second half of Brahmā’s life span has already begun,  
In this very moment of his time, which is an age to us,  
Darkness has returned, like a blinding cloud of dust.  
For Brahmā is the father of wicked beings too,  
Like Irreligion and Falsity, from whom descended a brood.  
One rules us now; his name is Kali, or Quarrel.  
Souls we are, eternal, but spun life after life  
Around the wheel of saṁsāra, in torment and strife.  
Human birth is the one chance by which to get free,  
But human life’s just like hell in the Age of Kali.  
Maddened with lust, seeking relief in the sewer  
Of nasty sense pleasures till we feel death’s sharp skewer,  
Which lifts us squirming, dripping the filth of our sins,  
And drops us in the abyss to wear fur, feathers, and fins.  
Yet those who are learned worship this age,  
In which the Lord has appeared, our cruel fate to assuage  
By giving His name, both the end and the means  
Of knowing Kṛṣṇa’s within us as our dearmost friend.  
But I was born far from India, and had not Prabhupāda come  
To the brutal Western world, and this movement begun,,  
How would I who was dumb, have ever spoken the name?  
How see and walk, since I was blind and lame?  
How wondrous this miracle: to be delivered by you  
From ignorance profound, and to have brought in my view  
My spiritual home, where all souls belong,  
Far beyond this aging universe, where everything’s gone wrong.  
You are the pure servant of Kṛṣṇa, and you never fear  
To preach boldly forth, for He’s your charioteer.  
You mix with the fallen, but your motive is pure:  
To give them Mahāprabhu-kṛpa, their offenses you endure.  
O most perfect of sages, you teach sambandha –jṅāna,  
Reversing the tide of a civilization so damned,  
By technology gone mad. But now we can use it for God,  
To print books and expand preaching and expose the whole fraud.  
This revolution you’ve inspired has only just begun;  
We’re only seeing the first rays of the mighty Kṛṣṇa sun,  
Which will rise streaming glory, giving us sight,  
To get free of the confusion of Kali’s dark night.  
To fully know your greatness is beyond my small brain.  
If I could just look in my heart, it all would be so plain:  
Beyond the anarthas, the residue of sin,  
Where the pure soul shines forever, never separate from Him.  
For your greatness is the reality, so simple and true,  
Of the soul’s eternal bond to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the youth  
Who so loves His devotee who can do naught else but serve,  
That He gives him all the help as only the pure soul deserves.  
You’ve come to us, Prabhupāda, just to prove that He’s there,  
Within all our hearts. And the test is quite fair:  
In this dark age of Kali, let us preach as you’ve shown,  
That Kṛṣṇa will help us, and in the end take us home.

*Your servant,*  
Suhotra Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Actually, you have no need of our eulogy or service. Being fully self-realized, you are ātmārāmā, or self-sufficient, but out of compassion you have kindly agreed to accept these. By allowing us to approach you in this way, you have given us the greatest benediction: a chance to escape from this prison house of material suffering by developing our pure love of Kṛṣṇa (God). As Prahlāda Mahārāja states (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.5.32*),*

*Maiṣāṁ matis tāvad urukramāṅghriṁ*  
*Spṛṣaty anarthāpagamo yad-arhtaḥ*  
*Mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo ‘bhiṣekaṁ*  
*Niṣkiṅcanānāṁ na vṛṇīta yāvat*

***“*****Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord, who is glorified for His uncommon activities. Only by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and taking shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord in this way can one be freed from material contamination.”**

You have made it clear to us, although we resisted knowing, that this world is a certified place of misery. Can such a gift of knowledge and the clear means of how to escape this suffering ever be repaid? No, it is not possible. So many famous, powerful, and learned men have come and gone in this world without getting an opportunity to learn this process of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. Why have we been singled out for this chance? What is our credit? Is there anything we’ve done to merit this gift? Speaking personally, I honestly don’t see any such credit. As you are well aware, I am lazy, angry, lusty, to name a few. So then what is our qualification? There is only one that I can see: somehow or other we met you, and by your sweet dealings you obliged us to become attached to your person and to the person and philosophy of Kṛṣṇa. That is our only credit, our only value.

So please allow me, on this most auspicious day of your appearance, to thank you for taking the trouble of making me your disciple. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you.

*Your lowly servant,*  
Trivikrama Swami

## Umapati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

How many times have I offered obeisances to Śrīla Prabhupāda? I couldn’t say. How many times have I done it the way I should? That’s an easier question: I haven’t done it right yet.

But I have made progress. At least I have an idea of what the words mean now, and that’s more than I knew the first time I offered obeisances to him, in 1966. It was on the night of my initiation.

A few bedraggled hippies and some assorted riff-raff were gathered around Prabhupāda to begin a career in… Well, we weren’t really sure in what, but Prabhupāda obviously knew, so we followed.

When it was my turn, Prabhupāda motioned for me to approach. Then he motioned for me to bow. Never before had my conceited head touched the floor at his lotus feet.

Then he told me what to say, word by word, and I repeated it. I didn’t know what I was saying, but I said it because he told me to. And he took me as his disciple.

It was just a short exchange between a great devotee and a reptile taking his first step out of the swamp. Yet, like everything else Prabhupāda did, this one gesture contains more knowledge than I could assimilate in many lifetimes.

I can look back and see the pure devotee carrying Lord Caitanya’s mercy to a forlorn conditioned soul, although I cannot grasp the magnitude of that mercy. I can see Prabhupāda telling me what to say when I didn’t know what to say, telling me what to do when I didn’t know what to do, just as in person and in his books he has told us what questions to ask and given us the answers.

I can see Prabhupāda making me follow the *paramparā* and say the right word, when someone else might have thought, “Why bother?” I can see that for Prabhupāda, the *parampaṛa* is not a bother but life itself.

And I can almost see that I have done nothing right since the beginning, but Prabhupāda had made it right by his mercy.

One should offer obeisances sincerely, but I do it hypocritically, because I think I am the supreme enjoyer. One should offer obeisances with humility, but I do it with audacity, because I have no right to think myself the servant of such a great devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. What claim have I to the dust of Prabhupāda’s lotus feet? I don’t even want to go back to Godhead, but Prabhupāda has grabbed me with his mercy, and he will not let me go. I struggle and struggle to get away, but then I remember his smile and I am defeated.

That’s it. Now I have admitted everything. The next time you see me offering obeisances, you will know what is really going on. May Prabhupāda help this wretched soul!

Umāpati Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Adelaide

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All eternal glories to your lotus feet, which are continuing to lead millions of Kali-yuga devotees back to their spiritual home.

Being the immortal, preeminent śikśa-guru for the next ten thousand years, you have taken on the heavy responsibility of being the most prominent spiritual master for everyone to follow in this fallen age. And although controversies gyrate around individual apostles, no one has ever found any deficiency in your impeccable personality.

You are the recognizable ācārya in disciplic succession from Śrīla Vyāsadeva, unveiling the superhuman activities of Lord Kṛṣṇa to all sincere souls, and you have kindled the divine consciousness of man by unpretentiously injecting transcendental knowledge into receptive ears. Those who are thus taking advantage of your purports are escaping the bondage of this material existence.

Since you’ve shown us the art of utilizing everything to glorify the Lord, the whole world can be turned into a spiritual atmosphere surcharged with devotional service unto God and each other. Then our belligerence will be changed into firm and fixed intelligence upon you, the transparent via medium.

There are selected histories of devotees who have been in direct contact with the Personality of Godhead, and your history belongs among them. Being the indisputably self-realized person Bhāgavatam, you are expanding the chronicles of the Vedanta-sutras by articulating the direct words of God. Therefore your incomparable accomplishments will be eternally glorified by all religious men.

Just by coming in contact with Your Divine Grace once, people have changed their downward hellish course, proving that you are more powerful than the waters of the sacred Ganges. Such is the mercy of the pure devotee, whose only business is to represent the lotus feet of the Lord.

Being the most confidential vicelord of this century, you deserve to be honored equally with the Lord, for you are elevating the most fallen conditioned souls to the highest platform of love of God.

Wherever you went, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you created an atmosphere of wonder surcharged with devotional service to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In your association the illusion of māyā was at once dissipated and everyone’s full energy was transformed from the enjoyment of sense gratification into practical loving service.

We fall down and pray with whatever sincerity we have to be able to have the invaluable benefit of your leadership in our next lives.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us everything that has ever been of any value to us.

*Your servants at Srī Srī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara’s temple in Adelaide, Australia,*

**Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Mahāśṛṅga dāsa

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Pañcaratna dāsa / Mahābuddhi dāsa / Sarvadharman dāsa / Vatsala dāsa / Kṛṣṇacaraṇa dāsa / Dīnabandhu dāsa / Rsi-kumāra dāsa / Daśāvatāra dāsa / Bhakta Gordan / Bhakta Melcolm / Siddharūpa-devī dāsi / Kumārī-kāntā-devī dāsī / Vraja-devī dāsī / Jāhnavī-devī dāsī / Arjunīyā devī dāsī / Sucandrā-devī dāsī

## Ahmedabad

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet, which continuously give shelter to Kirātas, Hūnas, Pulindas, Pulkaśas, ābhīras, Yavanas, Khasas, and other low-class untouchable human beings and purify them to such an extant that they in turn are now purifying the world.

Again and again, the Vyāsa-pūjā day comes, reminding us to resolve to take full shelter of your lotus feet. It is on this auspicious day that the world was blessed by your divine appearance. The world was in much chaos and darkness. *Rajoguṇa* (passion) and *tamoguṇa (*ignorance) were declaring their victory through the industrial revolution in the West. Bhāratavarṣa was crippled and overwhelmed by the cow-killing British Empire. The *brāhmaṇas* were eagerly serving their *mleccha* masters. *Sahajiyās* had become the guardians of the mission of Lord Caitanya.

Seeing such degradation all around, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura prayed to the Lord to send a powerful personality to propagate His mission. Two powerful personalities appeared in response to Śrīla Bhaktivinoda’s prayers” Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura and you, Śrīla Prabhupada. By the power of his intense, pure devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa, fortified by *naiṣṭhika-brahmacārya* and astounding scholarship, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvatī revived the mission of Lord Caitanya, and gave blueprints for the future world-wide movement. Your Divine Grace executed the will of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvatī and carried the flag of Lord Gaurāṅga to every corner of the world. By the time it was your turn to lead the mission of Lord Caitanya, much degradation had come to India. With practically no resources, you tried hard single-handedly to wake up the sleeping Indians. But to your amazement, you found that the Indians were unable to shake off the cultural and moral bondage of their British masters. Lord Macaulay, the British educator, had established the policy “If you want to conquer India, destroy its culture.” Now that the “white-skinned *sahebs* “ were gone, “brown-skinned *sahebs* –Nehru & Co. – began to finish the cultural destruction started by the Britishers.

Only Your Divine Grace could diagnose this serious disease in the Indian psyche. You thought that some Indians would join your mission, and that together you would preach to the Westerners. But Lord Kṛṣṇa did not trust anyone else in the whole universe but yourself to spread His mission to the West unadulterated. So He made you the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. This Kṛṣṇa consciousness mission is literally your monopoly. An *ācārya* is one who is spotlessly clean in his own example and yet farsighted enough to accommodate all kinds of people and engage them in the service of the Lord.

Even though you are situated in the highest ecstasy of *mādhurya-rasa,* you were so merciful that you spent most of your time teaching suffering humanity the basic lessons of spiritual life: “We are not these bodies, we are spirit souls.” This is your magnanimity, and by this magnanimity you got the mercy of your *guru,* Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. His order was “Preach in the English language; print books and distribute them widely.” He didn’t say, “Sit down in one place, send your disciples to get some rice and *dāl*, and talk about the highest conjugal affairs of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.” One doesn’t develop spiritual perfection in this way, or by academic scholarship or even austerity, penance, renunciation, etc. One gets the mercy of the Lord by pleasing a *mahā-bhāgavata* spiritual master. Śrīla īśvara Purī menially served Śrīla Mādhavendra Purī day and night, and that is how he became as mad with the ecstasy of love of Kṛṣṇa as Mādhavendra Purī. It is clear who served Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī most and who fulfilled his mission of a ViśvaVaiṣṇava Rājasabhā. Therefore, it should be understood that you have attained the highest level of *Kṛṣṇa-prema* simply by the mercy of your Guru Mahārāja and Kṛṣṇa.

As Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura points out, Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya are nondifferent. They are the same person, the reservoir of the transcendental mellow of conjugal love. This love has two aspects: transcendental enjoyment (*mādhurya)* and magnanimity (*audarya*).

In Lord Kṛṣṇa’s abode, the sentiment of transcendental enjoyment is prominent, and in the abode of Lord Caitanya the sentiment of magnanimity- mercy - is more prominent. So the sentiment possessed by the pure devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa is generally *mādhurya-pradhān-audārya:* magnanimity is the chief sentiment, and desire for sweet enjoyment is subordinate. When devotees of these two kinds enter the Lord’s eternal pastimes, they each attain two forms by which they can associate with both Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya.

You have exhibited the height of both these qualities by first describing to us Lord Kṛṣṇa’s sweet pastimes, they each attain two forms by which they can associate with both Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya.

You have exhibited the height of both these qualities by first describing to us Lord Kṛṣṇa’s sweet pastimes in Kṛṣṇa book, *The Nectar of Devotion,* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and later you described Lord Gauranga’s pastimes in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.* You have delivered for our highest redemption a complete and perfect spiritual package. Now please give us the strength to appreciate this gift forever.

But even more wonderful is the fact that you didn’t expect us to digest only the highest ideals of spiritual life. You could see the deep-rooted conditioning of our hearts, and therefore you stressed the need of *varṇāśRāmā-dharma.* Even most of your Godbrothers cannot adjust to or appreciate your universal vision. Even if someone has the lowest nature, still you want to engage him, provided he fulfills only a few simple requirements: accepting Lord Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Lord, chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* and following the four regulative principles.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura wrote about *varṇāśrāmā* thus: “Alas! The people of Bhārata-varṣa who were once examples and preceptors to the whole human race, have been reduced to the most contemptible state. This is due not to the divisions of caste but to the establishment of casteism contrary to Vedic principles.

“Their only hope now is that the Supreme Lord, who is the sole propounder of scriptural injunctions and the guide of all living beings, and who alone looks after the well-being of everyone, may, if He so desires, send some empowered personality to reestablish the pristine glory of the *varṇāśRāmā* system.”

Now it is clear you are that empowered personality. You always talked of brahminical culture and the need to make real *brāhmaṇas.* We decided to take a bold step in that direction by inviting twenty-five students of a Vedic *catuspāṭhī,* along with four elderly teachers of the *Sāmaveda and Yajurveda,* to come and live in our *āśrāmā.* They go to *maṅgala-ārati* and chant sixteen rounds of Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* and they also attend evening programs. During the day they learn and recite the *Vedas.* Thus your *āśrāmā* is full of Vedic chants all day. It is very auspicious. The students are all enthusiastic to become devotees. Many of them have voluntarily shaved their heads. Thus your plan always works.

Not just we, your disciples, but whoever loves the Vedic culture, *sanātana-dharma,* will eternally pay his respects to you, for in eleven short years you gave the world what no one else could give in eleven hundred years. One who is fixed in the understanding of how to live a spiritual life in the material world –the life you have given us-will never be bewildered even in the midst of reverses. It is this discrimination, which enables us to ascertain the truth and understand the *siddhānta,* that we beg from you on this auspicious day.

Spiritual subject matter is very confidential, and even the sages and demigods are sometimes bewildered in their attempts to understand it. But by your mercy, *mlecchas* and *yavanas* like us are now discussing the highest topics, which are meant for the *parāmāhaṁsas.*

Please continue to bless us on this auspicious day so that we may render some significant service to your mission. Please bless us to become humble and tolerant. Please bless us so that we may chant Hare Kṛṣṇa offenselessly.

Please accept our worthless prostrations again and again. Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servants at* ISKCON *Ahmedabad,*

## Almviks Gārd

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

This wonderful occasion of your annual Vyāsa-pūjā celebration is the turning point of the year. As I struggle to formulate my feelings, the entire year’s *sādhana* is put to the test. By pleasing you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with sincere glorification, we get the inspiration and Kṛṣṇa conscious intelligence to carry on the whole year long.

I think the turning point of my life was your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration in 1977. You took great pains to travel form India in your old age, coming to England two weeks before Janmāṣṭamī. At that time we were trying to distribute the newly-arrived hardbound Swedish *Bhagavad-gītās.* I was new at Kṛṣṇa consciousness, what to speak of book distribution, and having a hard time adjusting from selling small books for 15 SEK to selling deluxe-edition big books for 35 SEK.

Finally, just before Janmastami it was decided that the *saṅkīrtana* party would go to England. However, Smita Kṛṣṇa dāsa (now His Holiness Smita Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja) stayed back to distribute, having recently been to England. Trying to be renounced, I also decided to stay back, but fortunately Vegavān Prahbu convinced me to go. By the time we arrived at the Manor it was midday Janmāṣṭamī, and we heard that you had gone to Bury Place to see Rādhā-Londonīśvara. Some devotees were going to London, but I remained at the Manor, exhausted from traveling. When Your Divine Grace returned from London, there was such an uproarious *kirtana!*  The next day was your Vyāsa-pūjā and you kindly sat and appreciated the chanting. Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja then spoke about the early days in L.A., before you had a temple, preaching here and there, and you added that one time you even held a program in a garage. After the feast, Amita and Kṛpamāyā Prabhus grabbed me to go distribute. I was guarding your door day and night, and around morning time Harikeśa Mahārāja was going in and out. He patiently explained that I should offer obeisances once a day to a *sannyāsī.* There was a *saṅkīrtana* leaders meeting, where I saw how convinced and determined the European devotees were. Different arrangements were discussed. Maṇidhara Prabhu refused to allow cooking in his party. “Too much *māyā,”* he said. Jaya Gurudeva Prabhu gave us his *mantra* for distributing books, and Pṛthu Prabhu came with us back to Sweden for a couple of weeks to show us how to distribute the *Bhagavad-gītā* despite the language barrier.

How kind you were, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to come to the West for that last Vyāsa-pūjā! Especially for a late-comer like me it was a once-in-a-lifetime experience. Thank you again. Please allow me to serve you in the coming year by serving the devotees. Your pleasure is the goal and perfection of my life.

(written by Yogindra dāsa)

*Your servants in Almvik Gārd, Sweden,*

## Argentina

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Again another year has passed quickly. Like a ray of light this year has gone, and again I am Sitting, trying to get together all my feelings and sincerity to write an offering in glorification of you.

I am asking myself, amazed, “ How can such an insignificant and impure disciple glorify a personality so exalted, so gigantic and extraordinary?”

Surely it is impossible, at least until one is blessed by Your Divine Grace, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and the glorious Vaiṣṇavas. So to start to glorify you properly, I first desire that your mercy enter into my heart (which is so hard) and destroy my unlimited material desires, which are so difficult to cut to pieces. In this way, when I have a pure heart, my dear Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa will bless me so that I can do this difficult task – namely, glorifying such a pure devotee as you and humbly asking the blessings of your exalted and loving disciples and grand-disciples, who are so faithfully serving your ISKCON society.

My realizations are very small, and my mind is covered with lust. Therefore I feel so far away from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Still, I desire to stay under the cool shade of your lotus feet and serve you, allow me to be useful, allow me to always stay in the association of your disciples and grand-disciples, despite being so offensive.

You appeared in this world to execute the mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, a feat that was predicted by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura. Thus your goal was always clear; you never doubted, you always had firm faith in the words of your spiritual master. In this way your life was perfectly exemplary: everything you did served as an example for those who follow you. You are such a great soul! When will I be able to have such association? When will I be so pure?

Still, Śrīla Prabhupāda, even in this lamentable condition let me be a humble servant of your wonderful mission.

(written by Kaivalya-svarūpa dāsa)

*Your servants in Argentina.*

## Atlanta

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

In trying to offer respects to you on this blessed Vyāsa-pūjā day, I am again forced to practice some degree of humility, for you are so dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa and I am extremely insignificant. Your glories are beyond my abilities to comprehend, but still I must make some meager attempt to glorify you. I can just remember the awkward position Dhruva Mahārāja found himself in when he was given the opportunity to glorify Lord Visnu, who had appeared directly before him after Dhruva had successfully executed the required *yoga* practice. Although he was only a small boy without formal training in Vedic philosophy, by the grace of the Lord Himself Dhruva was nonetheless successful in his efforts to eulogize the Lord. Similarly, by your mercy even an unqualified person such as myself can at least be allowed to make the effort to offer some praise to you.

You are the captain leading us across this dangerous ocean of Kali-yuga, and by your mercy this opportunity has been made available to all conditioned souls through your movement and your books. We are ever thankful to you for giving us the service of assisting in this great adventure of spreading the all-blissful *saṅkīrtana* movement. This movement is very sacred and dear to all the *ācāryas,* as well to the Supreme Lord Himself, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who has so kindly inaugurated the *yuga-dharma*, the *saṅkīrtana-yajña.*

At present, human society has become extremely fallen and misguided due to the polluted nature of the atmosphere in Kali-yuga. Perversions of all types have become the norm. People have lost all sense of direction in terms of the meaning and purpose of life and are simply interested in satisfying the urges of the body, just like animals.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you warned us that human civilization was heading toward ultimate destruction, but we were so blinded by our own attachments to this material world that we could not fully comprehend the importance of your instructions, specifically in terms of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which is the only hope for suffering humanity and for ourselves as well.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you allowed us to understand the reality of the spiritual world, you allowed us to understand the reality of the spiritual world, you allowed us to taste the nectar of chanting the holy names, you gave us service that can qualify us for returning home, back to Godhead, you gave us *māhā-prasādam,* which is so dear to all of your devotees, and, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us a reason for wanting to continue living in this world, which is quickly progressing toward hell. How can we think for a moment that anything is more important than your instructions and teachings? We should simply focus on your desires and make them our life and soul. We should never doubt your words and try to rationalize our inability to fully surrender to you. We should know without any doubt that there can be no happiness in this world without full surrender to Kṛṣṇa and His pure representative.

I pray that I can always remember the great contribution you have made to human society, and also the great struggle you underwent to bring the teachings of Lord Śrī Caitanya to the Western countries.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me your blessings so that I might be able to somehow offer some humble assistance to your sincere followers in their efforts to carry out your divine instructions. All over the world devotees are becoming completely inspired to engage in the *saṅkīrtana-yajna* for your pleasure, and when we see their enthusiasm, we have no choice but to take this mission seriously. And most of all, their desire to please you helps us understand that you, in fact, have not disappeared but have instead transferred yourself to the hearts of the devotees.

*Your humble servants at New Pāṇi hāti Dhāma, Atlanta, Georgia,*

## Auckland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmi. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances and forgive us for the offenses we have committed at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

In your purport to the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, text 1.18.22, you define a *parāmāhaṁsa* as one who has “complete faith in the mercy of the Lord, without dependence on the condition of matter.” You would have to be the best example of such a personality. Evidence to support this can be found in the prayer you wrote upon arriving in America on board the ship *Jaladuta,* entitled “Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma, “ especially the first four and the last two verses:

“My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, You are so kind upon this useless soul, but I do not know why You have brought me here. Now You can do whatever You like with me.

“But I guess You have some business here; otherwise why would You bring me to this terrible place?

“Most of population here is covered by the material modes of ignorance and passion. Absorbed in material life, they think themselves very happy and satisfied, and therefore they have no taste for the transcendental message of Vāsudeva. I do not know how they will be able to understand it.

“But I know Your causeless mercy can make everything possible because You are the most expert mystic.

“O Lord, I am just a puppet in Your hands. So if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like.

‘I have no devotion, nor do I have any knowledge, but I have strong faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. I have been designated as Bhaktivedanta, and now if You like You can fulfill the real purport of Bhaktivedanta.”

This prayer clearly indicates how you had complete faith in, and were completely dependent upon, the mercy of Lord Kṛṣṇa to execute the order of your spiritual master, which was to preach the gospel of Lord Caitanya in the English-speaking nations.

The reason you had incredible success in doing this is not a mystery. Once again it can be understood from your own statement: “ We believed in the words of our spiritual master and started in a humble way- in a helpless way- but due to the spiritual force of the order of the Supreme Authority this movement has become successful.” How this statement is an explanation of your success can best be understood when considered in conjunction with two other texts from the scriptures. The first (from the *Śvetāsvatara Upaniṣad, 6.38):*

*Yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve thatā gurau*  
*Tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

And the second (from the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya* 7.11):

*Kali-kālera dharma – Kṛṣṇa-nama-saṅkīrtana*  
*Kṛṣṇa-śacti vinā nahe tāra pravartana.*

The translations of these two verses are as follows:

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed”; “The fundamental religious system in the age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement.”**

When we consider these two texts together in light of your original statement, we can understand that because you believed in the words of your spiritual master, as well as those of Lord Kṛṣṇa, having implicit faith in them, all the imports of Vedic knowledge became automatically revealed to you. Or, in other words, you became empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa to propagate the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

In the *Bhagavad-Gīta* (3.21) Lord Kṛṣṇa says:

*Yad yad ācarati śreṣṭhas tat tad evetaro janaḥ*  
*Sa yat pramāṇaṁ kurute lokas tad anuvartate*

**“Whatever action a great man performs, common men follow. And whatever standards he sets by exemplary acts, all the world pursues.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were certainly one such great man, and your exemplary acts set the standards for pure devotional service. We have but to follow in your footsteps in order to perfect our lives.

In a letter to the residents of New Dvārakā (August 14,1976) you wrote, “If you feel at all indebted to me then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay me.” We pray that we may be able to do this. You further said, “Of course, no one can repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master is very much pleased by such an attitude by the disciple. “ We further pray that we may be able to have such an attitude so that we will always be very much pleasing to Your Divine Grace.

Desiring to be engaged in service to you always, we remain

*Your servants in Auckland, New Zealand,*

## Bali

*Padma-vaktrād amṛtaṁ hi na kadācid adharmaḥ*  
*Sadā paRāmā-dharmāvadat prabhupādāye te namaḥ*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, from your lotus lips emanate the invaluable nectar of your words. Those words never express even a slight bit of irreligion; rather, you always teach *parāmā-dharma,* the supreme and pure religion. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

As soon as we hear your name, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we remember you as our perfect father or grandfather, so close to the heart of your devotees.

In the past, whoever heard the names of Rāvaṇa, Śiśupāla, Hiraṇyakaśipu, or Kaṁsa was immediately frightened. But in the last two decades, whoever hears your name, Śrīla Prabhupāda, feels peaceful at heart and smiles happily, his eyes shining with joy.

Many, many people have been inspired by your books and come to understand Kṛṣṇa consciousness through your teachings. Has anyone in this world not been touched by you? We do not even need to ask about the heavenly planets, for surely you are always being enthusiastically glorified by the residents here. Not do we need to wonder about hell, since it is certain that all the souls suffering there must be very eager to see you come to them and free them from the tortures of the Yamadūtas, as when Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja visited hell and delivered Karṇa and others from suffering.

O Prabhupada, all glories to your lotus feet! Your whole personality is like a magnet for pious souls. It is only those whose hearts are encrusted by the thick rust of sin who are not attracted to you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you visited some countries only once and for a very short time. But after you left, gradually the number of devotees increased, one by one. This was due to your great faith, scholarship, sacrifice, and devotional service to *guru* and Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we Balinese devotees humbly ask you for one benediction: Please do not let us go away, since all of us are struggling to serve your lotus feet and attain detachment from sense gratification and freedom from *māyā.*

We never feel that you are gone from this material world. Your transcendental books, tapes, letters, and photos are still existing and guiding us in performing devotional service unto Śrī Guru and Kṛṣṇa.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to you!

(written by Dāmodara dāsa)

*Your insignificant servants in Bali, Indonesia.*

## Baroda

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our repeated obeisances at your lotus feet.

The remembrance of the divine activities you manifested between 1965 and 1977 in this world never ceases to fill us with wonder. Just as Arjuna, being filled with wonder at seeing the *virāṭ-rūpa* of Lord Kṛṣṇa, offered Him repeated obeisances, we can only offer the same to you-

*namo namas te ‘stu sahasra-krtvaḥ*  
*punaś ca bhūyo ‘pi namo namas te*  
*(Bg. 11.39)*

The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.3.22) teaches,

*Tatra bhāgavatān dharmān śakṣed gurv-ātma-daivataḥ*  
*Amāyayānuvṛttyā yais tuṣyed ātmātma-do hariḥ*

**“Accepting the bona fide spiritual master as one’s life and soul and worshipable deity, the disciple should learn from him the process of pure devotional service. The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Hari, the soul of all souls, is inclined to give Himself to His pure devotees. Therefore, the disciple should learn from the spiritual master to serve the Lord without duplicity and in such a faithful and favorable way that the Supreme Lord, being satisfied, will offer Himself to the faithful disciple.”**

Your teachings are quite clear and unambiguous. You wanted us, your disciples in India, to endeavor to revive the traditional culture, the *bhakti* culture of constant service to the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa. And you also wanted this culture spread abroad. We saw how you cooked, ate, preached, translated, instructed, dressed, chanted, danced, worshipped the Deity of the Lord, observed the festivals of the appearance days of the various incarnations of the Lord, and the Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas* and devotees, and wrote and published books (essential *bhakti-granthas* like *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-Gīta)* as well as *Back to Godhead* magazine.

Yet despite the perfect example you set and the vast treasure chest of instructions you left, we experience difficulty in fulfilling the expectations you had for us. Weak-willed, we are constantly falling victim to the various traps of *māyā,* such as the desire to enjoy the overlordship of others, the desire to become wealthy, and the desire to enjoy the company of women. You taught us the futility of trying to fulfill these desires, but as Prahlada Mahārāja describes in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* we are simply chewing the chewed, again and again: *punaḥ punaś carvita-carvaṇānām.*

On this, the holy occasion of Śrī Vyāsa-Pūjā, we pray to your lotus feet that we may take to heart the aforementioned verse from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam (*11.3.22) hearts to follow your divine teachings and give up our duplicitous nature, which is constantly urging us on to somehow or other enjoy sense gratification. Remind us constantly that adherence to the basic principles you taught is essential to fulfilling your vision of inducing the public in general to become Vaiṣṇavas. Only if the nondevotees are inspired by seeing the spotless behavior of the devotees is there hope that they will take to this movement. If we can only focus our thoughts on your precepts and their practice, only then is there hope that we may give up our attachments to *māyā* and take up the serious business of spreading this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Praying that we may somehow or other obtain a drop of your causeless mercy, we beg to remain

*Your eternal servants in Baroda, India.*

**Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Vasu-ghoṣa dāsa

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Berlin

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble-obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, the most perfect disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmi.

This is the first time I have had the opportunity to write to you. Ordinarily, no one would write to a person who has passed away, but it seems natural to write to you. That is because you are not gone: your presence pervades the whole temple. Your instructions are here, as well as your tapes, your pictures, your devotees, and above all your books. I know you will read this letter (a fact that makes it even more difficult to write something of value). You will sit down somewhere in your typically majestic way and hear the offerings composed in your honor of your appearance day. You will appreciate these offerings not as a materialist would, as food for the false ego (which you don’t have), but rather as an indication that your children and grandchildren have made some advancement on the path back to Godhead by understanding that there is no devotee more merciful than you.

To appreciate your activities means to appreciate Kṛṣṇa. To worship you means to ultimately reach the Supreme Lord. As Lord Kṛṣṇa says to Arjuna in the *ādi Purāṇa,*

*Ye me bhakta-janāḥ pārtha na me bhaktāś ca te janāḥ*  
*Mad-bhaktānāṁ ca ye bhaktās te me bhaktatamā matāḥ*

**“My dear Pārtha, those who claim to be My direct devotees are not actually My devotees, but those who are devotees of My devotees are actually My devotees. “**

As Lord Caitanya enabled us to taste the huge nectar ocean of the chanting of the holy names, so you enabled us to bathe in the nectar ocean of the association of so many advanced devotees. By your mercy you sent us our spiritual master, Harikeśa Swami, who is equipped with your divine intelligence.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I would like to report to you a little bit of our activities here. By your mercy and the mercy of the other devotees, we have established a little center here in Berlin. Although we are not so many devotees (about fifteen), and although most of the devotees are new and somewhat inexperienced, through our varied activities we are able to help you a little bit in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. There is of course, a morning program attended by all temple devotees, regular *harināma,* book distribution, *prasādam* distribution, and selling to shops. We have *Bhagavad-Gīta* classes three evenings a week and an average of one hundred guests on Sunday. From time to time there are also TV programs and other preaching programs, such as a yearly Ratha-yātrā. The potential for making *bhaktas* here is good: this year alone we have already made four. We try to keep everything very clean and simple. It is a great pleasure for us to organize everything according to your desires and standards. And although we are very unqualified, we can see that just by following your order, we are getting some success.

Our present plan is to fulfill your prediction of twenty years ago that Berlin will become the biggest preaching center in Europe. Now we are trying to manifest a big temple, and if you would kindly bless us with your benedictions, we are sure that Kṛṣṇa will help us in our attempt.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, this Vyāsa-pūjā book will be filled with sweet words- poems and realizations of the highest degree. Please excuse me for offering only these simple words. I am not expert in worshiping you with appropriate speech, especially in English. Actually, there is no field in which I could be called expert. And only by the mercy coming from you through your wonderful follower Harikeśa Mahārāja can I take part in this wonderful movement. I don’t have very much to offer, but I have learned how much you appreciate even the slightest amount of devotion. Therefore I will continue to render some service with full conviction that by this service I will one day attain pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Then, finally, my restless soul will find its eternal resting place.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupada, for giving me the wonderful Kṛṣṇa consciousness philosophy, the chanting of the holy name, *prasādam,* and above all the nectarean association of so many pure devotees.

I hope I am able to offer you much more next year, and I beg to remain forever the servant of your servant.

(written by Diviratha dāsa)

*Your insignificant servant in Berlin, Germany,*

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This year, as we worship you seated among us on your *vyāsāsana,* which is decorated like an exquisite setting for the most valuable jewel, and surrounded by a host of blissful devotees, we anxiously strain to understand your directions.

Over the past few years we have brought you news of the attacks by “Kali’s *chelas”* on the preaching at Bhaktivedanta Manor. We would have loved to be able to inform you that these attacks were history, that the matter was resolved and there were no further obstacles to our *saṅkīrtana* efforts.

This is not the case. The government had decided that public worship and festivals must stop within two years. In your physical absence you are placing more reliance and responsibility in our hands. We would feel less guilty if we honestly thought we had done everything possible to protect your temple. But that is not the case either.

In a few short years of worldwide preaching, you took Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every continent. You always thought BIG ISKCON and chastised us for thinking small iskcon. Although you had established the beautiful Deities of Śrī Śrī Londonīśvara in a prestigious location in central London, a feat that no other Vaiṣṇava had achieved, you still wanted a “big, big headquarters” to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the British Isles.

Bhaktivedanta Manor was a key aspect of your plans to uplift the miserable population of this part of the planet – a part not even fit to be inhabited. Yet despite such a forlorn habitat, thousands and thousands of devotees have found happiness in Kṛṣṇa consciousness by taking shelter of the lifestyle, the atmosphere, the association of one of your temples.

And it is all thanks to you. No one else could have pushed so hard against all odds. You showed us how to live, how to worship, how to serve, how to chant, how to hear, how to surrender. You also showed us how to fight against adversity. And that’s what it seems we must do.

When the London Ratha-yātrā was threatened in 1974, you were very strong in spurring us to action: “ We are not going to produce such Vaiṣṇavas, not these dull rascals sitting down… My guru Mahārāja used to say, *prāna-arthe yāñra, sei pracāra:* ‘One who has got life- he can preach ‘. So you become with life, not like dead man. ….If you want to make an easy-going life, showing the Deity and then sleep, then it is a failure movement.”

We are in a desperate situation. On one hand we face a ban on our preaching and inevitable death for our community and project. On the other, we have the potential for a massive expansion of our preaching. At long last we could be building the temple you wanted for Rādhā-Gokulānanda. It seems we have no other option.

At the end of 1972 you were pressing us to secure a new temple outside London. You were not satisfied by the progress, and so you wrote, “Better to take the upper hand and begin very energetically attempting to get some place. By our expanding energy for Kṛṣṇa, that is appreciated- not the results of our energy. But if there is a lack of energy being devoted for some purpose, then everything will be delayed and possibly stopped. Better to seize the iron while the fire is hot- that my Guru Mahārāja used to tell me…… Otherwise Kṛṣṇa may take away this golden opportunity, being disappointed by our lack of enthusiasm and labor.”

There’s certainly much more energy we should be expending for you. Therefore, on this Vyāsa-pūjā day we fully pledge our commitment to your service. Yes, we will do all in our power to save the preaching at Bhaktivedanta Manor and to establish a beautiful new temple for the Deities. Yes, we will put aside all our mundane conceptions and distractions and dedicate ourselves wholeheartedly in a cooperative spirit. Yes, we will take deeper shelter of your instructions and immerse ourselves in the process of devotional service, increasing our hearing and chanting.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we may not have better news for you next year, but we hope we can stand before you more united, more determined, more purified, and more sincere in our efforts. Perhaps then we’ll be coming a little closer to pleasing you as you deserve.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*Your worthless servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor, U.K.,*

## Bologna

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet. As Caitanya Mahāprabhu came with His associates to spread the *yuga-dharma,* similarly, although you came alone, you soon created an association of devotees for properly counteracting the influence of the Kali-yuga. And like Caitanya Mahāprabhu, although you could have all alone accomplished the mission given you by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, for the sake of setting the proper example you instructed us on the potency of unity by using it as a measure of love: “ Your love for me will be demonstrated by how well you will be able to cooperate among yourselves after my departure.”

As time goes by, the truth of this momentous statement of yours becomes more and more evident. You knew that unity would guarantee purity, and therefore you placed it as the base of all other success.

As the word *saṅkīrtana* indicates (it means *saṅga-kīrtana,”* glorification of the Lord in the association of others”), in this age one cannot attain perfection on one’s own; *bhakti-yoga* has to be performed in the association of other Vaiṣṇavas.

To always stay in the association of other devotees of the Lord, one must cultivate the practice of constant loving exchanges, as Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī instructs: distributing and accepting *prasādam,* distributing and accepting gifts, and revealing one’s mind confidentially and listening to other devotees’ realizations.

This art of pleasing Kṛṣṇa’s devotees helps one please the spiritual master – you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And by learning how to please a pure devotee, one learns how to love and please the Lord Himself.

So how can anyone attract the presence of the Lord only on the strength of his own spiritual qualities and advancement? In other word, how can anyone create an atmosphere of Kṛṣṇa consciousness without the presence of other sincere souls who are all glorifying the Lord together? *Saṅkīrtana* means that everyone comes and contributes his good qualities, talents, energies, words, and everything else in his possession in the fire of the sacrifice, and the fruit of this congregational endeavor becomes the nourishment of other souls’ progress. As these living entities surrender themselves to your instructions, they become the channel by which you distribute your mercy.

Let us not think, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we are the only beholders of the truth, that you speak only through us, and that other Vaiṣṇavas haven’t really understood you.

Let us all contribute our understanding, based on your teachings, for the purpose of making the proper decisions and leading your movement securely in the dangerous waters of the Kali-yuga. By forgiveness and tolerance and continuing faith up to the end of our lives, we can emerge victorious over the tricks of Kali.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that my gratitude for what you have done for me will continue to be the motivating force that inspires me to help you in your mission and to struggle to conquer my lust and greed.

Thank you again for giving me the chance to participate in this wonderful movement. I beg your forgiveness for the insignificant service that I’m offering, and for my boldness in expressing my determination to continue being yours up to the end.

(written by Citraka dāsa)

*Your servants at the Bologna, Italy, Hare Kṛṣṇa temple.*

## Boston

Dear Śrīla Prabhupada,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, all the previous *ācāryas,* the Pañca-tattva, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

On this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day of 1990, I desire to thank you for mercifully revealing to me the purpose of my existence in this material world through you *vapu* and *vani*  and for giving me the *mahā-mantra,* which purifies and protects my mind from the onslaughts of *māyā’s* debasements.

After one gets partially purified, to test the extent of one’s purification Kṛṣṇa sometimes suddenly exposes one to circumstances almost beyond one’s ability to handle them without collapse. This has happened to me, and if it were not for Śrī Kṛṣṇa in His *maha-mantra* form- Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāmā, Hare Rāmā, Rāmā Rāmā Hare Hare – I feel I would have fallen over the edge into the hellish abyss.

Thank you again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for explaining through your books and tapes that the Supreme Personality of Godhead is Kṛṣṇa and that we, as parts of His energy, can eternally live with Him happily if we keep linked to Him in loving service to all His parts and parcels, as you have taught us by example.

Here, from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (2.8.24 purport), is just one gem of the many gems of pure knowledge you have given us that enable us to survive in the material world on our way back to Godhead:

“The spiritual master is always prepared to impart knowledge to the disciple and specifically when the disciple is very inquisitive. Inquisitiveness in the part of a disciple is greatly necessary for the progressive disciple. Mahārāja Parīkṣit is a typical disciple because he is perfectly inquisitive. If one is not very inquisitive about self-realization, one need not approach a spiritual master simply to make a show of discipleship. Not only is Mahārāja Parīkṣit inquisitive concerning all he has inquired about, but he is also anxious to know about what he has not been able to inquire. Factually it is not possible for a man to inquire about everything from the spiritual master, but the bona fide spiritual master is able to enlighten the disciple in every way for the disciple’s benefit.”

(written by īśā-devī dāsī)

*Your disciples and granddisciples in Boston,*
