## Brooklyn

**The New Generation**

**Part 1**

From all around the world they came  
To 26 Second Avenue.  
Back in 1966  
They came to be with you.  
They came for the Sunday feast,  
Filling the temple room floor,  
Sitting five people across,  
From front to back, some twenty-four.  
Spilling out onto the courtyard ground,  
And onto the garden’s small ledges-  
To the temple on the Lower East Side,  
And a garden full of hedges.  
A part of eternal history-  
The courtyard you passed through everyday,  
From your second-floor apartment  
To the temple where you’d pray.  
Near the courtyard’s birdbath  
We took your photo – full of glory-  
For the back cover of your first record album,  
Containing the holy name’s story.  
Everyone tasted your love-feast recipes-  
The bharats and cucumber raita.  
There were peas nestled in the fancy rice;  
The fried curd balls made it even better.  
Brahmin spaghetti, gulābjāmuns,  
Were only half the eve.  
Repeating your words about a personal God,  
Trying to get them to believe.  
The hippies, the intellectuals, and the beatniks  
All gave the best arguments they knew,  
But being soundly defeated,  
They surrendered unto you.  
With our newly learned logic and arguments,  
To them we passed your knowledge on,  
Defeating their example of the waterpot-  
Smash! – their Māyāvādī logic gone.  
The Māyāvādīs say the jug,  
While filling, does abound  
With gurgling noise and splashes  
‘Till full –then no more sound.  
They say at the point of fullness  
One becomes silent like a stone.  
One realizes he’s the one God,  
The only – all alone.  
The Māyāvadiṣ view of God-  
Impersonal and formless-  
Their self-realized soul need not talk:  
There’s nothing to profess.  
Our reply to them was simple;  
We’d just heard it that morn,  
In your Caitanya-caritāmṛta class,  
Where truth, for us, was born.  
Youu said then, “ The devotees say,  
With a fullness from within,  
‘Before, we talked all nonsense;  
let our real talk now begin.’”  
A waterpot is not alive;  
There is no similarity.  
We are living force to speak on Kṛṣṇa,  
Twenty-four hours does not suffice.  
A big part of our devotional life  
Was presenting to them your way…..  
Nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-  
Pāśātya-deśa-tāriṇe.

**Part 2**

Now they’re all coming to see you  
From other planets, other nations.  
Triumphantly you’re still manifesting  
Through the third generation.  
We feel your presence  
In their zeal;  
They feel your presence,  
They know you’re real.  
College students, high-school kids,  
Businessmen and punks,  
The straight edge and the hard core  
Becoming undercover monks.  
They’re coming to your temple,  
Having met you in your books.  
They’re writing, drawing, preaching, dancing,  
And becoming Rādhā-Govinda’s cooks.  
From your books you’ve entered their hearts.  
It’s you who’ve brought them here.  
You inspire them to sell your books,  
To take the holy name here.  
You are their spiritual guide;  
Your words make their hearts glow.  
They advance even faster  
When they see your video.  
You wrote a letter to Mālatī  
In 1968:  
“The spiritual master is the principle,  
Not the body’s gate.  
“Anywhere you remember him,  
He’s immediately here,  
Like the principle of relay message,  
As a TV make things near.”  
Now its starting all over again:  
History repeats itself anew.  
The new generation wants to visit  
26 Second Avenue.  
We feel your faithful energy,  
Seeing the new generation deal.  
You’re there in their acts of devotion;  
It’s your encouragement they feel.  
It’s like you told us in ’67,  
Just back from San Francisco, it was spring…..  
“Since Kṛṣṇa’s encouraging you in your heart,  
Your temple is improving.”  
Everything you say is encouraging  
Because you always give  
Kṛṣṇa the reality.  
With Him you let us live.  
The first time we met you,  
In October of ’66,  
In your apartment on the second floor,  
You said something like this:  
‘We are eternal,  
And everything around us is temporary.”  
You sat back, you were self-realized,  
Even hippies could feel your purity.  
You said that God lives,  
And that we could all live with Him-  
“Kṛṣṇa is yours.”  
You have the power to give Him.  
Shouting ‘Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!”  
We’re running after you.  
To give them you  
Is all we have to do.

(written by Jadurāṇī-devī dāsī)

## Budapest

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances on your divine appearance day.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you and your able representatives made our last year most wonderful year of our lives. By your causeless mercy you have flooded us with the transcendental, all-auspicious, blissful waves of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s saṅkīrtana movement.

Since we were recognized by the government of Hungary as a bona fide religion, we were able to establish your divine book distribution mission here. We have experienced a totally new realm of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which has perfectly changed our lives. Please give us your mercy so that we can deeply and completely merge into the bliss ocean of your book distribution and inundate our whole country in this ocean, in this way changing its most inauspicious course and making you, Lord Caitanya, and Lord Nitānanda happy. All glories to *Srī-Kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtana*! All glories to your sincere followers!

*Your fallen and aspiring servants in Budapest, Hungary,*

## Bulgaria

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

We didn’t meet you personally in this life, but at the same time one can meet you everywhere, in everything, and in every action if one wants to follow all the instructions you have given.

It is very merciful of you to have arranged things so that we have nothing to do but serve your servants. We attained this position with the help of your servants, who some years ago arranged for us to obtain a copy of the *Bhagavad-Gīta As It Is,* with your translation and elaborate purports. This book prevented us from committing so many sinful activities. This is your causeless mercy – to give us so many books, which keep us in contact with you forever.

You explain to us in the pages of your books that Kṛṣṇa said His real servant is the servant of His servant. This has made us happy, for it has inspired us to enthusiastically serve the mission of your servants.

Please help us to remain always the servant of your servants. We are in a fallen position, but we have such a nice opportunity now. We were completely lost in this material world, but by your perfect instructions given to our masters, you have arranged for us to act properly and advance on the spiritual path. Thus we wandering souls have found shelter.

This is your matchless gift.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We can’t give you anything in return except our useless attempts to help your servants. Please be kind and accept these efforts.

Once again, we want to thank you for your books and everything else.

*The servants of your servants in the Bulgaria yātrā.*

## Calcutta

In Calcutta, your birthplace,  
You appeared by His grace,  
Boldly preaching with the śāstric mace  
For the benefit of the human race.  
“Chant the holy name, O fallen soul,  
human life is meant for the highest goal.  
Don’t waste your time, hear the call,  
Change your ways or you will fall.  
Birth, death, old age, ‘n ‘ disease—-  
In Māyā’s kingdom you can’t be at ease.  
Don’t be satisfied with a temporary lease:  
Surrender to Kṛṣṇa and your worries will cease.”  
O Prabhupada! You are so kind.  
Your instructions purify our mind.  
More merciful than you we cannot find.  
Keep us together in your eternal bind.  
To us, Calcutta is a special place,  
For you appeared here, Your Divine Grace.  
Allow us to advance with a steady pace;  
Destroy our ignorance, don’t leave a trace.  
On this most auspicious day,  
With fervent devotion to you we pray.  
O lord and master, make us follow your way.  
In our helpless condition, what else can we say?  
Sincere surrender’s the best offering to you.  
Of your greatness we don’t have a clue.  
Drop on us your mercy like the gentle dew.  
Going back to Kṛṣṇa –let that dream come true.  
On your Vyāsa-pūjā this offering we make,  
Hoping that our sincerity you will take.  
We’re working hard day and night just for your sake,  
So that love of Kṛṣṇa in our hearts may awake.

All Glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your worthless servants in the Calcutta temple,*

## Capetown

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Your glories are chanted by great demigods and intimate associates of the Lord in the spiritual sky, who understand your real position as the most confidential servant of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and the *sena-pati* of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. How can our incoherent statements compare to their eloquence? Clearly, there can be no comparison. But we *can* speak of your unfathomable mercy in coming to deliver the fallen souls, ourselves, for your appearance is the only good news in our time.

We say that in this world there is so much information and misinformation being broadcast that is basically nonsensical and useless. Only the pure vibration of transcendental sound that you have brought enables anyone to make sense of this material world.

*Mām upetya punar janma duḥkhālayam aśṣśvatam*  
*Nāpuvanti mahātmānaḥ saṁsiddhiṁ paramāṁ gatāḥ*

This material world is temporary and a place of misery. Every other philosophy encourages people to rush headlong into the pursuit of material desire like moths into a fire. The pure teachings from the *ācāryas* encourage one to tolerate the pushing of material desire and become fixed in consciousness.

*Viṣayā vinivartante nirāhārasya dehinaḥ*  
*Rasa-varjaṁ raso ‘py asya paraṁ dṛṣṭvā nivartate*

One should investigate the source of what lies beyond this temporary material manifestation. *Athāto brahma-jijñāsā.* And that *brahma* is Kṛṣṇa:

*Vadanti tat tattva-vidas tattvaṁ yaj jṅānam advayam*  
*Brahmeti paramātmeti bhagavān iti śabdyate*

Some people, so-called scholars, objected that you put too much emphasis on the Supreme Lord. But you were never affected by their barking, and you were never interested in those who put poison in the milk. You were interested only in giving the cream, the real thing, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* knowing that this is where Kṛṣṇa can be found in this age:

*Kṛṣṇa sva-dhāmopagate dharma-jñānādibhiḥ saha*  
*Kalau naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa purāṇārko ‘dhunoditah*

Everything that is troubling the heart can be practically destroyed by hearing the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* but it takes a pure soul, a pure devotee, to transmit this knowledge intact, without adulteration. Your words, like Arjuna’s arrows, are always on the mark, right to the soul. And your instructions, your books, can lead anyone who reads them and follows them to the supreme perfection, back to Kṛṣṇa, back to home.

Every day we can see the proof, how your books are changing people’s lives because you never changed anything: you presented the Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy purely, as it is.

The pure sound coming down from you abounds.  
Transcendental books give all a look—  
Demons get shook.  
There’s no alteration or designation,  
Just topmost realization.  
The spiritual essence, no nonsense, Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s presence.  
Hear from you, get the clue, there’s a lot to do.  
Serve your mission, take association, requires submission.  
Give up pride before we slide; keep on your side.  
Whenever there’s trouble, before we stumble, become more humble.  
One who hears forgets the jeers, offers the years.  
On this day, what can we say? How to repay.  
Forgive when we stray, allow us to stay, serve another day.  
After all the miles, just to see your smile makes it all worthwhile.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants in Capetown, South Africa,*

Medhāvi dāsa / Śruta-karma dāsa / Jagat Guru dāsa / Yaśomatī-nanadana dāsa / Vīdura dāsa / Bhakta Kavi / Bhakta Graeme / Bhakta Andrew / Lalitā-devī dāsī / Gurusevā-devī dāsī / Vṛndāvaneśvarī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Claudette / Bhaktin Amanda

Kṛṣṇa / Nārāyaṇa

## Caracas

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Before coming to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, while looking for a spiritual guide without real success, I would sometimes wonder why I wasn’t born in Jesus Christ’s time so that I could follow a self-realized soul and in this way become closer to God by loving and serving Him. Time passed until I got the opportunity to meet the devotees and started reading your books. After reading *Śrī Īśopaniṣad,* I started to realize that you were the spiritual master I was looking for.

Your picture in *Śrī Īśopaniṣad,* particularly impressed me. You looked very humble and compassionate and so free from material desires. At that moment I accepted you as my spiritual master in my heart. Then my life changed: I started to chant the holy name with faith. I appreciated how the devotees were living a humble and austere life of dedication to devotional service. I moved into the temple, and after a few months you came to our center.

Everyone was so overwhelmingly enthusiastic. I felt as if the temple were floating on air due to the transcendental atmosphere generated by your presence. In the *kīrtana* we chanted and danced in front of you. Your loving and compassionate glance approved of what we were doing, and I felt as if I were meeting a very old friend and well-wisher.

At the time of initiation I was called to receive the sacred beads from your lotus hands, and you gave me my spiritual name and started to explain it. At one point you looked at me and said, “ You cannot hide from the eyes of God!” I felt as if you had caught me by reading my mind, because I sometimes stole *mahā-prasādam* and hid somewhere to eat it, thinking no one was watching.

You like going to the park every day, and we immensely enjoyed being with you. Once, while we were chanting our *japa* on our morning walk, you sat down, and we sat at your lotus feet on the ground. Everyone became silent awaiting your transcendental instruction. After looking at us for a while you said, “Just chant Hare Kṛṣṇa” and started to walk again. I realized that to chant the holy name without interruption was the most important instruction you were giving us.

Today I went to the same park, chanting on my beads and remembering you – how you walked so regally. The same pond was there, the same crocodile (now older), who you jokingly said was inviting us for dinner as he opened his mouth wide. The same trees were there, the same road, the same groups of ducks about which you laughingly remarked, “Everyone in this material world likes to go in couples. “

Now I feel everything void without your presence, but by your blessings we can again create your Vṛndāvana atmosphere of love and trust. Cooperating and preaching, we hope to please you some day by becoming a humble servant.

O my spiritual master, when will I see you again, chanting your melodious, nectarean *bhajans,* hearing your transcendental lessons, seeing your beautiful smile, your merciful glance that washes away the dirt in our minds.

Please accept me as one of your sons, even if I am an unworthy one.

(written by Jagat-cakṣur dāsa)

*Your servants in Caracas, Venezuela.*

## Chittagong

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Through your infinite mercy we’ve received such matchless gifts-

*Divine knowledge, a purposeful existence,*  
*Freedom from fear, the names of God,*  
*Śastra, sādhu-saṅga, Kṛṣṇa-sevā,*  
*Deity worship, saṅkīrtana,*  
*And so, so much more.*

Your glorious appearance day finds us:

Feeling terrible for all the past missed opportunities to sincerely serve you; feeling still worse as we continue to avoid and miss repeated opportunities to attempt to serve as we continue to avoid and miss repeated opportunities to attempt to serve your lotus feet; feeling so desolate in this world now that you are beyond material vision; hurting as being unable to perceive your eternal presence;

Hoping and praying to be eventually engaged wholeheartedly in your loving service, to be able to humbly render any insignificant service to your lotus feet; desiring to be blessed with the association of your sincere and devoted servants, and to be allowed by them to engage along with them in pleasing you;

Wishing that one day we will attain a glimpse of understanding of your gifts, your mission, and your fathomless mercy; but feeling desolate and inept, overcome with grief due to our stubborn inertness, which obstructs any real and sincere attempts to glorify you;

Enchanted by your divine qualities, sweet instructions, and amazing pastimes; awed and eternally grateful for all the difficulty you undertook to deliver Lord Caitanya’s mercy even to the most unworthy souls; inspired by hearing the services rendered to you by all your real and unflinching servants from all corners of this planet.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, may we somehow, by your divine grace, continue our attempts to repay the unrepayable by repeating your message, distributing your books, following your instructions, chanting sixteen rounds, following the four regulative principles, and giving our all to your divine lotus feet.

*Your most insignificant servants at Śrī Puṇḍakīka Dhāma, Chittagong, Bangaldesh,*

**Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Nistula dāsa

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Christchurch

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Thank you so much for appearing at this time on this planet, even though this world is such a troublesome place. Thank you so much for giving us the opportunity in this human form of life to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. Don’t think we don’t appreciate it. Don’t think we don’t care – we do care very much, even though we sometimes we don’t show it.

Today, your appearance day, is when we pull all other things aside and focus all our time and thoughts on you. We meditate on all you have done, which is so much that the more we think about it, the more amazed we become. It is not that we are unable to find anything to say in glorification of you; it is just difficult to decide which of your many glories we should speak on. For whatever pastime of yours we think about, we find it glorious. Indeed, your whole life is glorious- the beginning, the middle, the end.

But it is not that your glories stopped at the end of your body. No! Your glories continue on. Just as the glories of Kṛṣṇa forever being chanted by the serpentine Śeṣa incarnation are ever fresh, always new, so are yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

And so by meditating on you always, we are always inspired to continue on in our Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to also help others know your glories. For you are worth glorifying, and everyone is looking for that perfect person to glorify. So if somehow we can let people know about you, all their desires will be fulfilled.

Thank you again.

*Your servants at the temple in Christchurch, New Zealand,*

## Costa Rica

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances unto your divine lotus feet.

All glories to you! All glories to your International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

This is the most blessed day, on which we can offer the best of our lives.

Words are too limited and time is insufficient to describe all your qualities. It would not be possible even if we had countless mouths, like Lord Ananta, or the lifetime of Lord Brahmā. Still we could not chant all your glories.

You voluntarily came to this suffering world just to teach all of mankind the correct way to act so as to remain unaffected by the cheating and lying so characteristic here.

You came to teach what the higher taste is—-the wonder of devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa—-and by your own example you taught what a devotee of Kṛṣṇa is.

With your purity you are changing Kali-yuga into Satya-yuga.

With your movement you have offered shelter to all the world.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

You, the perfect *ācārya,* have come to give us the sweet chanting of the holy names of God, making the proper adjustments for the Western regions of this planet, where everybody is affected by passion and ignorance.

By your mercy, thousands of unfortunate souls in the West have become fortunate by listening to the glories of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. And in this way, by your mercy, they have become real Vaiṣṇavas, whom we are trying to serve. Although we are awkward neophytes, we are sure that by your divine grace we will come to know you by serving your disciples who are nicely carrying out your order to preach by publishing and distributing millions of your incomparable books.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

You preached Kṛṣṇa consciousness until the last moment of your life. Please give us your blessings so that we can also preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness until the last moment of our lives.

All glories to you on this blessed day!

Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya!*

*The humble servants of your beloved disciples in Costa Rica,*

## Coventry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful and humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

I am very privileged and honored to have been asked by the devotees at our *nāma-hatta* group in Coventry, England, to write this offering to Your Divine Grace. I feel unqualified to do so, as I have no proper intelligence to choose the words to glorify such a great and pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa as Your Divine Grace. I can therefore only pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa to give me the words that can make this offering worthy.

In Coventry we are certainly blessed by your mercy and the mercy of Lord Caitanya, for nearly fifteen years ago my Godbrother Haridāsa and I came across real diamonds while looking for broken pieces of glass. These diamonds were your special mercy in the form of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* other transcendental books of yours, your devotees, and most of all the *mahā-mantra—-* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. The *bhakti-latā-bīja* was surely planted in us in those years. Although we never actually met you in person (*vapu)*, to this day we feel we are always in your presence through your teachings and instructions (*vānī)*.

In those days our Godsister Vishnupriya-devī dāsī (Mrs, Vina Anand) got inspired to take to a Kṛṣṇa conscious way of life after her visit to Bhaktivedanta Manor, and she converted her house in Coventry into a temple of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vṛndāvaneśvara, where the first *nāma-hatta sat-saṅga* programs took place. Earlier this year she left us to be with Kṛṣṇa.

Since then we have gradually progressed. We’ve begun to understand the purpose of this rare human birth, and how to attain the ultimate benefit it can give us, which is *Kṛṣṇa-prema,* love for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa (*premā pumārtho mahān).*

Every time we read your books, we feel inspired by the clear vision given us by your purports to the verses in *Bhagavad-gīta and Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* Certainly the clouds of illusion are being lifted away.

We therefore pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa and beg for a drop of your mercy so that the roots of our *bhakti-lata,* the creeper of our devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, will become stronger and stronger. We pray that this creeper grows healthy and fructifies and is able to survive the onslaughts of *māyā.*

Our *nāma-hatta* in Coventry has made wonderful progress over the last few years. We have been able to purchase a small building and establish a preaching center dedicated to Your Divine Grace. At present, renovations are being carried out and regular Sunday programs are going on, with lively *kīrtanas, Bhāgavatam* classes, children’s dramas, and *prasādam.* Various families and members from the community participate enthusiastically, offering their services. They are all getting your association and the *darśaṇa* of the Lord Kṛṣṇa, and they are able to learn about *bhāgavata-dharma.* We pray that with your blessings our small center will one day become a very significant temple of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

We are very appreciative of the inspiration and guidance your disciples have been giving us all this time, especially our Guru Maharaja, His Holiness Kṛṣṇadāsa Swami, whom you actually met at Bhaktivedanta Manor and asked to join you in your preaching movement. Since then he has been serving you wholeheartedly.

Your Divine Grace had laid a strong foundation for ISKCON to spread Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* which is the *dharma* for this Kali-yuga. Lord Caitanya said that in every town and village there would be chanting of the glories of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa; that a city like Coventry is touched by His movement is proof that Lord Caitanya’s prediction is coming true. You made this possible by coming to the Western countries at the age of seventy and going through a lot of hardships to establish ISKCON and preach the message of *Bhāgavata-dharma.* You exemplified the perfect teacher, an *ācārya* setting examples in all aspects of *bhakti,* unalloyed devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You even taught your followers to cook, to be clean in mind and body, to play *mṛdaṇga* and harmonium and sing devotional songs, to print and distribute books and magazines, to paint, to manage temples, to worship Deities—Oh! The list is endless. Above all, you gave the formula for peace and happiness. All we can do is follow in your footsteps: *mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthaḥ.*

You have all qualities of a Vaiṣṇava—-*kṛpālu* (merciful), *satyasāra* (truthful), *nirdoṣa* (faultless), *vadānya* (magnanimous), *sarvopakāraka* (doing welfare work for everyone), *śuci* (clean), *karuṇa* (compassionate), *dakṣa* (expert), *maitra* (friendly), *amānī* (without false prestige), *sthira* (fixed),—once again the list is endless.

All the devotees in Coventry feel inspired by the examples you have set and are advancing in their Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I would like to thank them for giving me this opportunity to glorify Your Divine Grace, and I beg their forgiveness for any offenses and mistakes I may have made.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, forgive us for any offenses we may have committed to the dust of your glorious lotus feet. Give us the strength and intelligence to remain in your service and in the service of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

In the service of and on behalf of all the Vaiṣṇavas at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Cultural Center, ISKCON Coventry, U.K. I offer you my respectful obeisances millions of times.

(written by Madhusūdana dāsa)

*Your insignificant servants in Coventry, U.K.*

## Curitaba

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories unto Your Divine Grace.

You are the special personality empowered by Lord Caitanya to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement to every town and village. And we, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are eternally grateful to you.

By your example you showed us how to preach and how to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We are trying to carry on in such a way that will please you, by always distributing the holy name, *prasādam,* and your books.

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us fully adopt the process of devotional service as instructed by Lord Caitanya. You gave us real life, which is *Kṛṣṇa-katha* and *Kṛṣṇa-seva.* We are insignificant, and whatever service we may perform is also very small, but we try to please you by our preaching efforts. We beg to follow humbly in your footsteps and satisfy you by our service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious occasion we pray for your mercy, so that we may, with great patience and enthusiasm, continue to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness and distribute your books.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*Your fallen servants in Curitiba, Brazil,*

## Dallas

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

I have been requested to write your 1990 Vyāsa-pūjā homage for the Dallas *yātrā.* This is your causeless mercy upon me to engage me in meditating upon you. When the disciple remembers his spiritual master, this becomes the greatest satisfaction for the soul. So I am begging your mercy to somehow give me the words to properly glorify you.

Sitting here in your Dallas quarters, my mind flies back to the days when you were physically present with us on this planet. You were constantly traveling from center to center to give us your association as much as possible, because you knew how much we needed it. I remember being here in Dallas in 1972 when we moved into this building, which you later in described as “a nice house”. Because the first *gurukula* was started here, we were always getting letters from you telling us how to develop the school.

And then the devotees here received the sweetest of all nectar, a personal visit by Your Divine Grace. After you landed at the airport and the devotees were driving you to the temple, you glanced at the Dallas skyline and observed, “It appears to be a nice city.” Upon arriving at the temple and walking up the stairs of the older building, you instructed, “These are excellent facilities; now utilize them.”

Not only did you come, but you arranged for the Supreme Personality of Godhead and His eternal consort to manifest. They arrived by trailer via New York shortly after you got here. And the Kṛṣṇa you brought was no ordinary Kṛṣṇa. He was unusually large and stout. He was Kālacandjī, “the dark moon-faced one,” who had been worshipped some five centuries ago in India during the time of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Foolishly, some of the devotees thought to alter the Lord’s features by repainting Him. In response you manifested severe anger, which was a manifestation of your pure love for Kṛṣṇa. After many reprimands, it was arranged that Kālachandjī would be repainted black into His original appearance with some quick-drying paint. The paint dried in just barely enough time for the installation ceremony.

The Lord was simply dressed in a corduroy outfit, and the alter was decorated with corn stalks. You performed the first *ārati* for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālacandjī, and everyone became eager for Their *darśaṇa.*

The great fascination of this Rādhā-Kālacandjī Dhāma is that your pastimes are eternally manifested here in the hearts of your sincere followers, who aspire to remember them. Indeed, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālachandji Dhāma is a worhipable *tīrtha* because of the touch of your lotus feet. You came here on no less than five different occasions. You walked up Gurley Avenue followed by a huge flock of your disciples. You paused before one of the little children who was carrying a toy oatmeal –box *mṛdaṅga* and blessed him with your divine association. You walked along Graham Avenue, transforming the sidewalk into spiritual energy with each step. You stood in front of the building on the corner of Graham and Gurley and posed for a photograph with the *gurukula* children and their teachers. You walked on the western bank of the White Rock Lake in the early morning hours, enlivening your followers with your transcendental instructions.

You would spend long evenings sitting in the garden the devotees made for you behind the parking lot. Seated in your white rocking chair, you explained how Kṛṣṇa was the origin of the sky. Meanwhile the Texas sky gradually faded into the night.

It was here one evening as you sat in that garden that you revealed to the devotees as amazing detail of Kṛṣṇa’ and Rukmiṇī’s pastime – a detail that had not been published in *Kṛṣṇa* book. You explained to the amazed devotees how when Kṛṣṇa snatched away Princess Rukmiṇī in the presence of Śiśupāla and others, she had grabbed the reins of the horses and actually driven the chariot as Kṛṣṇa picked up His bow and arrows and defeated the opposing princes.

One time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you were recalling your quarters in various parts of the world, you said that you had very nice quarters in Dallas, Here everything was situated for you in an enclosed suite: kitchen, bathroom, office, bedroom, and servant’s quarters. One ring of the buzzer on your desk would get your servant, two rings would fetch your secretary.

In this place you manifested your divine anger when the devotees foolishly cut down very large and beautiful tree that had been standing in the courtyard. It had been one of the real opulences of the temple. The heavy winds of a Texas storm had knocked it over so that it was leaning on the roof of the *prasādam* hall at about a sixty degree angle. The devotees, thinking it would eventually come down through the roof, had taken an electric saw and dismantled it limb by limb. You scolded the devotees, saying, “ This is your brute Western mentality: if something is wrong, immediately kill it.” The dent where the tree hit the building is still visible in the metal on the edge of the roof above the *prasādam* hall.

It was here, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you had all the children come one by one by your desk while you personally handed them a morsel of *prasādam.* One little boy, Bhakta Viśvareta, was very expert at chanting prayers and could even lead *kīrtana.* You had spoken with him several times in Los Angeles. However, when he came before you he became very shy and would not say anything, even though you tried to elicit from him a response by pulling his *śikhā* and poking him in the stomach. Finally you had a servant bring a very nice sweet to give him. This brought an enthusiastic response from the boy, as he eagerly accepted the *prasādam.* At this you smiled and stated, “Oh, you are a devotee of *prasādam.”*

There are unlimited manifestations of your divine presence here in Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālacandji Dhāma, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We are just begging from you one ounce of mercy so that we may always remain under the transcendental umbrella of your divine shelter.

Please bless all of us struggling souls here in Dallas so that we may always remember Kṛṣṇa and be absorbed in preaching. Your mercy is all that we are made of.

(written by Saṅkarāṇa dāsa)

*Your servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kalachandji Dhāma, Dallas, Texas,*

## Detroit

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Not long ago, your devotees installed a very impressive brass plaque near the main entrance of your royal residence here in Detroit, the Bhaktivedanta Cultural Center. This plaque reminds all of our visitors that this beautiful temple was your inspiration from the beginning, and that its existence and effectiveness continue to depend on your mercy.

During this past year, more than one hundred thousand people passed through this temple and had a chance to hear about your glories. No doubt, many of our visitors are at first attracted to hear the mundane history of the mansion and its former occupants.

But our tour guides report that by the time each tour is over, many of the guests have undergone a remarkable transformation. They stand respectfully in front of your divine *mūrti* and listen with keen interest to the miraculous story of your preaching throughout the world. Some are overpowered by the sense of your continued presence and whisper to one another, “How does he manage to sit so still?”

After the tour, many enjoy Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī’s *prasādam* in Govinda’s Restaurant. A large number take some of your literature home with them. And everyone takes home a favorable and lasting impression of the spiritual atmosphere you have created here.

There has been another very welcome development this year. A number of your sincere devotees who are respected leaders among the Indians in this area have recently taken on significant responsibility for the maintenance and development of the temple. They are very active in recruiting new members and supporters and have taken a leading role in developing the Sunday Feast program. They have also formed a *saṅkīrtana* party that holds programs in different homes every week. This is having a profound effect in the Indian community.

Your *gurukula* here is also developing nicely. Last fall we opened a beautiful, well-equipped new classroom building under large shade trees in a quiet corner of the property. Enrollment is gradually increasing as devotees see the dedication and competence of the teaching staff and the steadiness of the program – which begins every morning at *maṅgala-ārati* time. Your young students have become strong examples for the entire community by their staunch attendance at the morning program.

Through the efforts of your devotees, a weekly Kṛṣṇa conscious radio show has been started in Windsor, Ontario. The program has created a lot of interest, and there are plans to develop further in this medium. Let the air waves be purified!

You will be happy to hear that by Kṛṣṇa’s grace we have finally installed docking facilities. This was, of course, one of your express desires for this temple. Now, thousands more people will be attracted to visit your temple by boat and to take *Kṛṣṇa-prasādam* in our floating restaurant.

As you predicted, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the whole neighborhood here is gradually being transformed by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. There is a lot of new construction bringing life to the area. And the continued influx of fairly well-to-do neighbors should have a very positive long term effect on our programs.

The work of distributing Kṛṣṇa’s mercy in the poorer parts of the neighborhood is also continuing. Food for Life, set up in its own building not far from the temple, is feeding *prasādam* to thousands of needy people. There are further plans to expand the scope of those activities in the next year by creating an emergency shelter, a clinic, and a food co-op.

There have been other accomplishments this past year, Śrīla Prabhupāda. But we are always aware that whatever has been done so far is insufficient. After all, you desired and planned for nothing less than the conquest of the world by the forces of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Dear master, we pray that you will bless us in this coming year with the energy and commitment to better satisfy you by our service. Be compassionate upon us and remove from our hearts all inauspicious things. We are weak and cripple-minded. Your mercy is all that we are made of. Kindly keep us in some corner of your fragrant lotus feet.

(written by Baṭu Gopāla dāsa)

*Your humble servants in Detroit.*

## Durban

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

You write in the *Bhagavad-gīta* (4.34 purport) that “the satisfaction of the self-realized spiritual master is the key to advancement in spiritual life.“ With this in mind we offer the following poem for your transcendental pleasure:

**The Key Is to Please**

*You came to help us, the wretched souls of Kali’s domain,*  
*Pierced by arrows of pride and arrogance that found their aim.*  
*Protecting us with armor of affection and concern,*  
*You armed us with weapons and the ability to learn.*  
*Your directions and instructions are powerful when properly used,*  
*But, like a sharp knife, devastating when mistakenly abused.*  
*Within your camp there are many faithful to you.*  
*Serving them with humility, we gain strength anew.*  
*As we become more expert in following your command,*  
*You will give us the astras with which to fight fully armed.*  
*An honest desire to satisfy you is the key to success.*  
*Being a menial soldier in your army will deliver us from this mess.*  
*How can we know for sure what you would desire?*  
*This perfection of anxiety will deliver us from maya.*  
*Again we ask ourselves how can we be sure you are pleased.*  
*Until we get some sign, our anxiety will not be eased.*  
*‘The satisfaction of the self-realized spiritual master is the key.”*  
*Soon in Goloka-Vṛndāvana your lotus feet we will see.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, out of your mercy you came to this remote and socially outcast corner of Africa. At that time, in 1975, we numbered no more than half a dozen. You seemed pleased with our meager efforts. A rented house in a middle-class Jewish area. *Harināma*  on Friday and Saturday nights, a few hours of book distribution every week.

You suggested a temple in Durban and encouraged one of your disciples to take a post in the Indian University. Apart from this you did not give any specific instructions.

Now, fifteen years later, your society has grown. You have a million-dollar temple, two smaller temples, a farm, eighty temple devotees, and hundreds of sincere and enthusiastic congregational members. We have many plans to expand by construction, book distribution, and seminar courses.

At present our *yātrā* is going through many changes, and we are hoping that you can advise us and give us some direction. Our only purpose in existing is to give Your Divine Grace some satisfaction, and to achieve this we wish to put aside our personal ambitions and work cooperatively to establish your desire. On behalf of all the devotees, we humbly pray that you accept these meager attempts to please you, and if you desire that we act in a certain way, please direct us accordingly.

(written by Pārtha-sārathi dāsa)

*Your servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānatha Mandir, Durban, South Africa,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Fiji

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Recently, while listening to the glories of Māyāpura-dhāma, we also heard the glories of Nityānanda Prabhu on *parikrāma* of Nava-dvipa-dhāma. In the course of this discussion I suddenly realized the real purport of your name, “Bhaktivedanta.”

On returning to Antardvīpa, Nityānanda Prabhu spoke of a wonderful temple of Lord Gaurāṅga that would rise there. From this wonderful temple the *saṅkīrtana* movement would spread all over the world. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement has already been firmly established by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and you have also initiated the projects for preaching Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission in the future to fulfill Lord Nityānanda’s words. Locaṇa dāsa Thākura mentions in his *Caitanya-man gala* that Nityānanda Prabhu, while speaking to Caitanya Mahāprabhu, said that ‘A great *sena-pati* \[commander in chief\] will come to earth and start the golden age. His instructions will guide the golden age for ten thousand years.”

You are that commander in chief, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and you relish being at the front line in full form. You are still in the front line in the form of your society, your books, and your orders. While giving a lecture in Los Angeles in 1973, on Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s disappearance day, you made this statement : “I was always a leader, even among my friends in school. One time an astrologer saw my hand and said ‘*Hūkuma calega’* \[Your order will be carried out.”\] So the facts are becoming more and more visible to us. You are a leader for Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In the future people will regret how time and *māyā* cheated them so that they could not recognize your greatness during your *prakaṭa-līlā.* However, sincere and submissive souls will not be deprived of your association, for you are a completely spiritual person and are able to instruct them, guide them, and reveal yourself to them eternally.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you brought strict *paramparā* knowledge to expose all kinds of materialistic cheating. Devotees are studying your books, life, and teachings with greater seriousness—individually and collectively—- in institutes like the Vaiṣṇava Institute of Higher Education. We are all beginning to realize the significance of statements of yours such as these: “I have given you all you need to know” and “I have given you enough material for several lifetimes of study.”

Your purports are full of meaning and practical application. Your books are *bhakti-śastras,* practical guides for those who wish to relish devotional service. They are meant for those who have rejected all kinds of garbage in this material world and are eager to repeatedly bathe in the clear lakes of your ISKCON society. In this way the devotees taste the true nectar that Lord Caitanya always desired to bestow—*pūrṇāmṛtāsvādanam.* At this pinnacle of devotional understanding there is no room for compromise or devīant spiritual philosophies.

Upon your arrival in America, at Boston harbor, you prayed in complete surrender, “I have been designated as Bhaktivedanta, and now if you like you can fulfill the real purport of Bhaktivedanta.” Since then, so much has been revealed about you. In the last few years your *Letters* books were released, followed by *Conversations with Śrīla Prabhupāda,* a Vaiṣṇava etiquette course highlighting your dealings and management techniques, and a special course called “Prabhupāda Appreciation.”

Whatever you revealed by your example, words, writings, etc., is so perfect that it does not even accidentally devīate from the *bhaktivedānta* conclusion, i.e., that service to Lord Kṛṣṇa is the goal of all knowledge and the goal of life. This conclusion is thoroughly exemplified by every moment of your life’s devotion. Though your activities are so gigantic, they are personal and in touch with the hearts of the devotees.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, O Lord Caitanya’s heroic commander in chief, O graceful swan, here in Fiji we feel that we have committed a great offense to you. When your Lautoka Temple was attacked by arsonists, we could not protect the temple room, your seat, or your *mūrti.* During the incident the attackers were distracted by you. At the time of the attack there were not enough devotees to take care of the temple and Kṛṣṇa, so you sacrificed yourself out of love for Kṛṣṇa, thus saving the Deities. We beg forgiveness for greatly inconveniencing you and failing to have a proper security system.

Out of your love for Kṛṣṇa you have always been prepared to sacrifice unlimitedly. You neglected and gave up your business for the sake of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You gave up your wife and children due to their indifference to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You took up the life of a mendicant and underwent austere living conditions in Vṛndāvana. Despite failing health and severe heart attacks, you continued your journey to preach in the West. You slept very little and constantly traveled to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness widely. You accepted anxiety for many centers and disciples and struggled with atheistic governments, parents, societies, and so on. You never gave up, simply because of your love for Kṛṣṇa.

By these personal examples, you showed us how to execute Kṛṣṇa consciousness at all times. Because of these unwavering examples we are able to remain strong, even in the midst of blind *sūdra* harassments of all kinds. You are a continuous source of strength for devotees throughout the world. Your thorough understanding of the *paramparā* teachings and your fulfilling of your name, Bhaktivedanta. Thus, following in your footsteps, we are also becoming addicted to Bhaktivedanta service. Please bless us so that we may remain attached to your service forever.

*Your servants and the servants of your servants in Fiji.*

## Finland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. For common people like us, it is impossible to properly glorify a person like you, yet if we do not try, we will feel dry and incomplete. We never met you personally, yet we are convinced of our loving relationship. You love us like a grandfather loves his grandchildren. We lack realization of spiritual life, yet we understand that pure devotional service is the highest goal. In our neighboring country, the USSR, the devotees are distributing your books like mad, yet even though we are distributing but a fraction of the books they are, I think you would still be pleased even with us if we just sincerely endeavor to fulfill your desires in this regard.

We are making so many mistakes, yet we are sure you will forgive us if we repent from our heart and carry on with enthusiasm.

We cannot claim to be great devotees or followers of yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda, yet if every now and then you send one of your intimate associates here to visit us, then we can also become Prabhupādanuga by serving and pleasing your servants.

You are like Lord Kṛṣṇa, who reveals Himself to the same degree that one surrenders to Him. So please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help us get the conviction to surrender everything we possess now and are going to possess in the future to your mission of producing and distributing your books all over the world; help us become so devotional that our cooking and distribution of *prasādam* will make people’s hearts melt; help us become so pure and unmotivated by material desire that when we chant on the streets, people will get faith in the holy name on the spot and want to join this movement of Lord Caitanya’s.

Thank you very much for all your matchless gifts. Whatever mercy you give us, we promise we will immediately distribute it to others. So please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have some mercy upon your servants here in the Finnish forest.

*Your servants in the woods of Finland,*

Paramparā dāsa / Kuru dāsa / Ajamīḍha dāsa / Devavrata dāsa / Hiraṇya Mahāśaya dāsa / Jayada dāsa / Kalpapādapa dāsa / Karandhāma dāsa / Purūrava dāsa / Nandaparthika dāsa / Nimi-devī dāsī / Jagad-ambika-devī dāsī / Bhakti-jananī-devī dāsī / Madana-mohinī-devī dāsī / Tarkalī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Tina / Bhaktin Juha / Bhaktin Hanna / Bhaktin Ulla / Bhaktin Sari

## Fortaleza

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day let us say some words in acknowledgement of your most sublime service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. We cannot measure your glories, for they go far beyond this tiny earth planet, which is inhabited by imperfect beings dominated by the modes of passion and ignorance. “Causeless mercy” are the only words we find to explain your association.

It is wonderful to see how you are so expert at engaging everyone in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and also at teaching everyone to do the same. In this way you are taking us out of the material dark well, taking us back to Kṛṣṇa. Your transcendental link with Kṛṣṇa and the kind desire of your spiritual master made you the biggest warrior in this fight against *māyā.* This no one can doubt.

The proof is there: you traversed the whole planet and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere.

My fear is that with these words of mine I will commit an offense by diminishing your glories. This is most likely, since your qualities are endless and cannot be truly appreciated by those who are slaves of envy, such as myself. Thus I beg your compassion for my lack of devotion.

The planet earth feels much less burden now because many sinners have surrendered unto your lotus feet. You made an infallible program to change the face of the world, and you gave it to us. Now you tell us again and again: “Do not change anything; do exactly as I told you to do.” The sincere work of all your devotees will broadcast your glories still further. We all have to show you a Vyāsa-pūjā full of complete sincerity and not merely present you with a tour of the ISKCON world. Besides, you are our inspiration when we see your determination and your detachment from material comforts. Then we feel ashamed of our laziness and bad will.

You are still guiding us, and everything that happens in ISKCON is a means by which you teach us and sometimes reprimand us. For that we offer our obeisances unto your lotus feet again and again. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, always be kind to us. Give us your transcendental association, for it is everything to us.

## Gainesville

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day, your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā!

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have offered the entire world a way in which to live peacefully by uniting under the banner of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You have done what no one else could have done—turned tens of thousands of *mlecchas* into aspiring devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa. It is understood that by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa one can attain the spiritual master. We also understand that although the spiritual master is not God Himself, he is the transparent via medium to God Himself. With this in mind, we would like to expound upon your godly qualities and in this way remind ourselves of your unlimited magnanimity.

Lord Matsya easily acted like a boat in the form of a giant fish to protect the *Vedas.* Your desire to protect the *Vedas,* Śrīla Prabhupāda, manifested in your effort to push on book distribution – to see that books of transcendental knowledge are distributed in every town and village in the world. This knowledge offers constant protection from our own ideas and leaves no room for error.

Lord Kūrma, in His form as a tortoise, supported Mandara Mountain on His back as the demigods and demons tried desperately to get the nectar they so desired. Your desire is to build a house in which everyone can live and be spiritually supported. Though we try so desperately to find happiness in this material world, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, patiently repeat the words of *sāstra* despite our lack of intellect (and/ or desire) to fully understand.

Lord Varāha rescued the earth from a dirty place at the bottom of the universe after it had sunk in the Garbhodaka Ocean. And you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, came to our filthy cities and also lifted us up—-you lifted us out of the ocean of darkness and ignorance and rescued us so that we might have the opportunity to purify ourselves and, by your causeless mercy, serve the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa.

The fearless half-man, half-lion incarnation, Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva, conquered the demon Kiraṇyakaśipu. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were given the name Abhay at birth, meaning “fearless”. You came to the West, fearlessly conquered the demons, and saved thousands of wayward souls from repeated birth and death.

The dwarf-*brāhmaṇa,* Lord Vāmanadeva, took massive steps to deceive King Bali, who then surrendered unto the Lord’s lotus feet. Though physically small in stature, your steps covered the earth many times as you boldly preached Lord Kṛṣṇa’s glories. We were deceived by Māyā, but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, convinced us to give up our own ideas and become devoted servants of *guru* and Kṛṣṇa by setting the perfect example as a pure devotee of the Lord. We surrender at your lotus feet.

Lord Parasurāma washed away the sins of the world by killing the demonic *kṣatriyas.* From the moment of your arrival in the West, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you immediately began the task of killing the sins of the sinners rather than sinners themselves—- fortunate for us! Your words pierced our hearts, and by their potency we became civilized.

Lord Rāmacandra, in His eagerness to find His beloved consort, Sītā, and kill the demon Rāvaṇa, engaged even tiny creatures like spiders in His service. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your eagerness to serve your beloved Govinda and dispel the illusion of this material world, engaged us all—- even though we were very small and frail—- in loving devotional service.

Lord Balarāma is the wielder of the plow, which the river Yamunā greatly fears. Your plow, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is your spiritual strength, and we wish nothing more than to please you. The mere thought of your displeasure sends us reeling. Please forgive our ignorance and give us the shelter of your lotus feet.

Lord Buddha incarnated at a time when people were most materialistic, and He decried the slaughter of innocent animals. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, came to the West when so many were looking for an alternative to the materialistic way of life. You stated, “Unless one is humble and prideless, one cannot be harmless and nonviolent.” You would not even partake in a cow-shaped cake made for you on one of your appearance days. Your compassion knows no bounds.

At the end of Kali-yuga, Kalki comes to bring about the annihilation of the wicked barbarians. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, carried your sword of words and obliterated any chance the demons had to find a loophole in this great philosophy. There is no room for speculation—- you have spoken “as it is”.

In your physical absence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray for your protection and just a drop of your faithfulness, your exquisite cleanliness, your encouragement, your fearlessness, your total surrender, your purity, your determination, your strength, your compassion, and your causeless mercy.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*Your servants at Gainesville Preaching Center,*

## Gītā-nāgarī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**A Most Blessed Event**

Eventide at the ISKCON farm. An August sunset casts the barn in a soft amber light. The cows welcome the cool dusk. In the side pasture, a couple of milkers tail-whisk the flies. Below the orchard, young and old cows chomp fresh grasses, while the oxen slake their thirst by the creek. And in the woods, a score of old-timers roam their leafy haven and gather under a wide oak tree to ruminate – and wonder: where are the people this evening?

In the garden, marigolds, lilies, and dahlias are in full bloom. Cool-weather crops like spinach, lettuce, radishes, and fall broccoli are in the ground; potatoes and pumpkins are near-ripe; and tomatoes, sweet corn, summer squash, okra, and peppers are ready to pick. Twilight is a peaceful time to weed and harvest, but the garden is empty this evening. Where did the hands go?

In the farmhouse temple, hands move quickly to prepare the products of the land and cows for His pleasure. Hands move nimbly to sew the finishing touches on His new garments. Hands massage and bathe His divine Deity forms. Hands hold prayer beads and praise His holy names, weeding hearts and harvesting love for Him.

It’s Janmāṣṭamī, the appearance anniversary of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and the eve of The Most Blessed Event, the Vyāsa-pūjā Day of Kṛṣṇa’s dearest devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The woods darken as the sun sinks below the horizon. Crickets chirrup above the cicadas’ twang. The gnats give way to mosquitoes.

Swat! Ouch!

A lone devotee misses a mosquito and slaps his own face. Chuckling at the instant rebuke, he climbs onto a woodpile, pops a side button on his overalls, and pulls out his meditation thread. The evening star is about to appear. Time again to remember *guru* and Kṛṣṇa.

Fourteen years. Has it been that long? Seems like yesterday Śrīla Prabhupāda walked these dirt roads, inspected a potato from the garden, toured the barn, raised his eyebrows at the size of Lalitā’s udder and spoke in the evening: “Are you working the bulls?”

Bzzzz!

A fly breaks the reverie. The devotee’s back straightens, but soon his mind is off again, this time to Vyāsa-pūjā 1977. Prabhupāda is in London, planning to come to America (to this Pennsylvania farm, in fact) to start *varṇāśrāma—*- Kṛṣṇa’s sociospiritual organization—- in ISKCON. But….

The thread chafes the devotee’s thumb, the *mantras* long over. Another meditation lost to reverie. He feels a little hopeless and thinks of the mistakes the farm has made—- the people mismanaged, the money misspent. Forget the past, he tells himself. But it’s hard when you keep making mistakes.

He wonders what homage of offer Śrīla Prabhupāda tomorrow and searches hid darkening surroundings. An open pavilion shelters an oxen-powered sawmill. For the past several winters, the oxen have provided the muscle to heat all the farm dwellings with wood. Already they have been cutting logs for next winter—- up to three cords daily—- and delivering by wagon. His heart lifts as he thinks of the six teams he has trained to work. Last spring they plowed, harrowed, and disked twenty-five acres for corn and oats. And shortly before Janmāṣṭamī they cut, tended, and raked their third hay harvest of alfalfa, timothy, and clover.

“Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda,” the devotee thinks, smiling, “we’re working the oxen. “

Yet many of the oxen remain idle. Often we have enough animals to work but not enough devotees trained to work them. Few of us refugees from the cities and suburbs, it seems, understand the urgency of basing our *varṇāśrāma* economy on Dharma the bull. And fewer still have taken up the yoke. To this latter group especially I offer my humble obeisances.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.44) Lord Kṛṣṇa says, *kṛṣi-go-raksya. Kṛṣi* means farming, plowing, cutting the earth. How? By *go-rakṣya,* cow protection. Cows produce milk and more cows, and bulls to work. Trained as an ox, the bull tills the ground to produce food and drops his dung to fertilize the fields. The cow turns the produce of the fields to more milk, which refines our consciousness so we can assimilate the subtle lessons of spiritual knowledge—like the value of the cow protection—- and keep up the complete cycle.

Break that cycle at any point and we have problems. We have problems because we haven’t realized the complete utility of the cow and bull. Conditioned to exploit, we find it hard to just say no to the modern slaughterhouse economy. So we often do only a little bit. We may do a little organic farming, maybe even produce our own seeds. Many organic farmers do. But nearly all have a blind spot: They don’t farm with the bull; they farm with the tractor.

They farm with the tractor because they are producing for profit in a market economy, which is fueled by a finite supply of pollutants. Pollutants to grow, pollutants to harvest, pollutants to ship, pollutants to sell. Which will go first, our oxygen or our fuel? And then how will we get food?

Time and again, history has shown that no society can long sustain itself without Dharma the bull. Dharma, or religious life, which the bull symbolizes is the father of all economic progress, sensory fulfillment, liberation, and ultimately pure love of God. When the Industrial Revolution kicked Dharma out of the fields, all hell broke loose. Now mother earth herself seems bruised and beaten, her children in a biosphere about to collapse.

Yet Kṛṣṇa’s cowherds see not the brink but an eve. A most blessed eve. Lord Kṛṣṇa has appeared in the womb of His movement, and already world events suggest a retreat from the brink, a divine reprieve. The earth begs healing, and Śrīla Prabhupāda has taught us how: work the bulls and protect the cow.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

(written by Sureśvara dāsa)

*Your servants at Gītā-nāgarī-village, Port Royal, Pennsylvania.*

## Gothenburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

We also offer our humble obeisances at the feet of all your faithful disciples, especially to our spiritual master.

Your transcendental glories are increasing every day, as your books are distributed profusely and increasingly all over the whole world. All over the world people are becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious because of coming in contact with your books or with someone speaking from your books. No doubt you are very happy to see that your books are produced and distributed like anything, and we’re confident that the more this is going on, the more pleased you will become. Since you are our grandfather spiritual master and the grandfather is even more merciful than the father, we take the opportunity on this most auspicious day of your transcendental appearance to pray to your lotus feet for your blessings so that we may become souls fully surrendered to your transcendental *saṅkīrtana* mission, serving it life after life with great enthusiasm and determination.

*Your servants ‘ servants in Gothenburg, Sweden*

Premārnava dāsa / Rohiṇīnandana dāsa / Śambhu dāsa / Mānupriya dāsa / Kauśalyā-devī dāsī

## Guwahati

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

In order to uplift the most fallen rascals like us from the lowest stages of material life, you have given a wonderful morning program for us to follow. As a humble offering to you on the auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we would like to make an effort to glorify the daily program.

The day begins with *maṅgala-ārati,* in which the devotees can immediately rise to the transcendental platform by chanting Kṛṣṇa’s holy name and dancing in front of the Deities. This sweet *maṅgala-ārati* is followed by the prayers to Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva, who is the protector of the devotees. Then we announce the *saṅkīrtana* scores of the previous day for the pleasure of the Deities, Your Divine Grace, and the assembled devotees. This is followed by Tulasī-puja, and after circumambulating Tulasī Maharani, the devotees offer their humble obeisances to each other. Then comes the chanting of *japa,* which continues for an hour and a half or two, till 7:00 or 7:30 in the morning. The conchshell is blown, and it is time to greet the Deities and sing *govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi.* And then comes *guru-pūjā,* in which the devotees can awaken their remembrance of you. The devotees offer flowers at your lotus feet and chant the glories of the spiritual master. After this the devotees hear the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness from your brilliant translations and purports on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* Then comes a delicious breakfast of *bhāgavata-prasādam.* The morning program thus comes to an end.

Our morning program is so comprehensive that it includes the essence of *sādhana-bhakti,* covering the various aspects of the sixty-four devotional principles enunciated by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmi in the *Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu.* By following the process of *sādhana-bhakti,* which you have kindly taught us out of your mercy, a devotee is assured of quickly achieving spontaneous devotional service and proceeding toward *Kṛṣṇa-prema.*

We therefore humbly pray to Your Divine Grace that we may remain fixed in strictly following the morning program given by you. Please let us not become foolishly negligent and devīate from it. We also humbly pray that we may acquire a drop of purity so that we can carry your mercy to other conditioned souls like us, who may thus wake up and take to the process of *sādhana-bhakti,* the path of perfection.

*Your humble servants in Gawahati, India,*

Śrī Jīva dāsa / Gauradeva dāsa / Mahotsaha dāsa / Keśava dāsa / Dīna-bandhu dāsa / Dharma-rāja dāsa / Vṛnda-pati dāsa / Jaya Gaurāṅga dāsa / Bhakta Sudipta / Bhakta Jagadisa / Bhakta Laksman / Bhakti Gopal / Bhakta Sankar / Bhakta Sarajit / Bhakta Nabin / Sarvajīveśvari-devī dāsī

## Guyana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet, which alone can give complete relief from the blazing forest fire of material existence. By your causeless mercy we have come in contact with the pure and sublime teachings of that most munificent incarnation of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You are so kindly allowing us all, unqualified and unfit as we are, to participate in your mission.

Even though we are so stubborn and reluctant to give up our futile search for sense gratification, you are giving us the chance to become purified even by the slightest contact with ISKCON.

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, we can simply pray to become selflessly surrendered at your lotus feet. What can one attain in this life that is more valuable than this? Śrīla Prabhupāda, however much we may try to express our appreciation of your glories, our efforts still fall short of your true greatness.

Please allow us to stay in the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement and to be used as your instrument for the rest of our lives.

*Your servants in Guyana.*

## Hare Kṛṣṇa Land, Mauritius

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Another year has passed, and again we are afforded the privilege of making a Vyāsa-pūjā offering. Unfortunately, for the last year our service has been insignificant. Although it is appropriate to offer words of glorification, we fear that they will be only official praises devoid of devotion. Therefore this year we wish to offer something more significant.

In our last days you asked, “ Will the movement go on without me?” This anxiety was not born solely out of your concern for ISKCON’s future in your absence. You were always anxious about the maintenance and development of your tender and growing movement.

Since it is the disciple’s duty to relieve his spiritual master’s anxiety, our offering this year is to relieve his spiritual master’s anxiety, our offering this year is to relieve your anxiety about ISKCON in the areas in which we are serving. We will make sure that the principles you established are maintained and that the preaching programs you wanted are developed. Rather than sleep for another year, we promise to make your anxieties our anxieties.

To further relieve your anxiety, we wish to inform you that you have thousands of fully dedicated disciples and granddisciples throughout the world who are currently working day and night to preserve and expand your movement. Therefore, Sirla Prabhupāda, there is no need to worry about the future of your society. Being a jewellike devotee, you produced many jewellike followers, who, like you, are making all sacrifices to maintain ISKCON. The future of your movement is in good hands and therefore bright. It will grow to be all that you expected of it.

*Your servants at Hare Kṛṣṇa Land, Mauritius.*

## Heidelburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances.

Although most of us are not very advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we are very inspired and happy to serve you in this most wonderful ISKCON.

You are still with us in your transcendental books. Actually, you never died and will always continue to live in your books. As long as we distribute your books and read them, we can have your wonderful association, although we are rather unqualified. We want to distribute more books because in that way we can have more association with you. Because this is what you want us to do, Kṛṣṇa is mercifully helping us. Just a few months ago many East European countries were opened up for book distribution. Right now your *saṅkīrtana* devotees are traveling to Prague, Budapest, Sofia, East Berlin, etc., bringing Kṛṣṇa to the people there in the form of your books. The people are very eager for Kṛṣṇa in the form of your books. They are happily giving half their monthly income just to get one of your books.

The result of book distribution shows us very clearly that service to you is not limited. This proves that you are still with us.

We have full faith that just by following your instructions—- especially to print and distribute your books—- everything will increase. It is just a question of time until everyone will know about your unique qualities and wonderful mission.

We consider it the perfection of our lives to be a part of your mission, although we may play only an insignificant part.

Thank you again and again for accepting our imperfect service.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants in Heidelberg, Germany.*

## Hong Kong

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We feel ourselves incapable of composing timely verses of glorification to Your Divine Grace. Due to our reluctance to engage directly in your service through your instructions and bona fide representatives, some of us are facing moments of setback, swayed by the allurements of *māyā.* Repercussions also come when we try to attack strongholds in atheistic Khasadeśa (China). There are many difficulties to encounter and much opposition to overcome. Therefore, we pray for your mercy and the mercy of Lord Caitanya.

However, book distribution, *prasādam* distribution, and *harināma saṅkīrtana* are still going strong in ISKCON Hong Kong. New devotees are also joining us. In preparation for future reverses, we are establishing a rural farming community, which will be another phase of local development under your divine instructions. This will give us strength and protection.

Sirla Prabhupāda, we pray to always take shelter of your lotus feet.

*Your servants in Hong Kong and Khasadesa,*
