## Vitoria

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. May you always be glorified as one of the most important associates of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the greatest preacher of the *saṅkīrtana* movement in the modern age.

Lord Caitanya asked His personal associates to go and plant the seed of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in many places of India. But the Lord kindly left the rest of the world to you. You are so wonderfully performing this most elevate duty, and the good results are being appreciated all over the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you will always go on preaching through your books, your life, your example, and your ISKCON.

Thank you very much for everything, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

We beg to remain the servants of your servants.

(written by Annantadeva dāsa)

*Your servants in Vitiria, Brazil.*

## Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this holy, blessed day,  
With those around the world we pray,  
Falling humbly at your feet,  
Your sacred glories we repeat,  
Like lamp lit unto morning sun  
Or Ganga’s worship with water done.  
Our mind is stretched beyond and sky—-  
How can we ever glorify  
The mercy that you’ve given?  
But still today we’re driven  
To offer words as tiny measure,  
In vain hope it may give pleasure.  
At three score years and ten  
You left this holy land,  
Where endless waves of bliss  
Are flowing due to this:  
Śrī Rādhikā and Śyāma  
Sport in this sacred dhāma.  
You set sail for foreign land  
With your sanctified command:  
To spread the holy name  
As freely as the rain  
To those who are not fit,  
Entrapped in Kali’s grip.  
Pṛthivīta āche yata  
Nagarādi-grāma,  
Sarvatra pracāra  
Haibe mora nāma.  
Five hundred years have flown  
Since Lord Caitanya made this known.  
But some of them would be blessed  
Were each and ev’ry town  
In Nadia ‘round.  
Or maybe in Bengal—-  
That certainly was all.  
Orissa too, they said,  
Is where the name might spread.  
In Bhārata-varṣa maybe,  
But not across the sea.  
The Thākura did not agree:  
“Why not beyond the sea?  
If Caitanya’s Lord of all,  
Let all lands hear His call!”  
And so he begot a son  
To get the service done.  
A lion among the saints;  
The Māyāvādī faints;  
In you he marked the sign:  
“He’ll do the work in time.”  
He made you this request:  
To get the whole world blessed.  
‘Twas you, our Prabhupāda,  
who crossed the ocean broad.  
To every continent  
The holy name was sent,  
Giving joy to all  
With Lord Gaurāṅga’s call.  
“Tis very great indeed,”  
some of them agreed.  
“The name has crossed the sea  
to the world that’s free.  
But in the Soviet  
They’ll surely say, ‘Nyet.’”  
That day I’ll not forget—-  
Remembering, my eyes are wet:  
The Russians came—-sixty—-  
And danced in your Samadhi,  
Beyond their wildest dream,  
Beside Yamuna’s stream.  
We are so very low;  
The Lord we do not know.  
The Vaiṣṇava, we have heard,  
Is he who should be served.  
But in such a one as you,  
How can we give what’s due?  
We only hope and pray,  
Our debt we can repay,  
That when your servants come  
To your home, Vṛndāvana-dhāma,  
By service of their feet,  
Your mercy we will meet.

*(written by Dīna-bandhu dāsa)*

*The devotees at Śrī Śrī Krishna-Balarām Mandir, Vṛndāvana,*

## Washington D.C.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept the humble obeisances of your aspiring servants who are situated at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-mohana here in Potomac, Maryland. *Oṁ tasmai Śrī-gurave namaḥ.* All glories to you for giving us the highest truth.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai Śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Truth is the foundation of knowledge. The most precious gift one can give or receive is the Absolute Truth. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the *mahā-vadanyāya ācārya,* for you freely distributed the Absolute Truth worldwide. You are thus the perfect via medium of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

As time marches on, we appreciate more and more how you so mercifully showered us with Kṛṣṇa consciousness through all aspects of your presence. Even your momentary glance could melt the heart of a truth-seeker.

You taught us what we need to know—-that this world is not all in all. Our existence here is like a dream, a nightmare from which we wait to be rescued by a bona fide savior. And so you saved us with the *jyāna-dīpa,* the torchlight of knowledge. You taught us how to find ourselves through Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And you freely distributed the sound incarnation of Kṛṣṇa to cure our addiction to *ajñana-timira,* the darkest ignorance. Thus, by our chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare, our eyes are gradually opening to the truth.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as we stand here at New Hastināpura in Potomac, Maryland, on the outskirts of Washington D.C. poised to rally for you in the *dharma-yudha,* the showdown with the forces of ignorance, we seek your blessings to carry on your mission and fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His *sampradāya* to spread the holy name to every place on the planet. Please empower us with courage and strength to represent you honorably, not only to do humble service but also to humbly serve, and to live the sweet words that we speak on your behalf.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for your matchless gifts!

(written by Haladhara dāsa)

*Your servants in Washington, D.C. (Potomac, Maryland),*

Jaya Keśava dāsa / Haladhara dāsa / Gopīśvara dāsa / Nṛsiṁha dāsa / Ekeśvara dāsa / Datta dāsa / Gaura Hari dāsa / Kṛṣṇadeva dāsa / Yaśomatī-nandana dāsa / Dhīrodatta dāsa / Tapasya dāsa / Prahlāda dāsa / Śraddhā dāsa / Bhakta Richard / Bhakta Renato / Bhakta Mike / Janeśvarī-devī dāsī / Prema-rasa-devī dāsī / Ambikā-devī dāsī / Arcanā-devī dāsī / Śyāma-devī dāsī / Gopālī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Tracy / Bhaktin Cassandra / Bhaktin Janira / Bhaktin Lelah / and all the visiting devotees from ISKCON Public Affairs, KrishnaFest, and Padayātrā America

## Wellington

I stand each day remembering you,  
Though you before I have never seen,  
Yet on the vyāsāsana you appear to me  
And kindly reciprocate within my dream.  
My neat attire you observe with approving eyes,  
My etiquettes and pranāms you must surely recognize,  
And with a flower offering body, mind, and words—-  
A madman’s monologue, or was I really heard?  
And while fanning your transcendental form,  
I suddenly want to see you eye to eye;  
But such premature urges must purity deny,  
The likes of me your lotus feet first must glorify.  
By their mercy only this newly formed creeper  
Can bear the fruit nectar of bhakti-rasāmṛta.  
That’s the absolute nature of your divine lotus feet—  
Like gold with fragrance perfectly complete.  
From heaven’s abode Mother Ganges comes swiftly down,  
Carried in countless planes to sanctify our ground.  
Likewise the nectarean dust from your feet transforms  
Into a shower of books, a devotional storm.  
Though the praise of your lotus feet is scarcely begun,  
Spiritually from head to toe you’re absolutely one.  
So, dear master, let us offer praise to your hand,  
Which has scribed Vedic purports for every race of man.  
From your kind hand prasādam is supplied,  
And signals to your leaders to orchestrate our lives.  
Please keep us, dear Prabhupāda, as members of your fold,  
Make us diligent workers for your smile to behold.

Praying at your lotus feet,

*Your aspiring granddisciples in Wellington, New Zealand,*

Kāliya-Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Rāmacandra dāsa / Bhakta Marcus / Niścalā-devī dāsī / Sītarāṇī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Karen

## Bombay BBT

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are delivering the people of the world who are miserable and immersed in the ocean of nescience. Being compassionate unto them, you deliver to them the process of *bhakti,* by which they can realize their eternal constitutional position as servants of the servants of the divine youthful couple of Vṛndāvana, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Although it is certainly not possible to glorify you suitably, allow us to offer a drop of such glorification for your pleasure. May it please those stalwart servants of yours who are very dear to you.

*nṛ-deham ādyaṁ su-labhaṁ su-durlabhaṁ*  
*plavaṁ su-kalpaṁ guru-karṇadhāram*  
*mayānukūlena nabhassvateritaṁ*  
*pumān bhavābdhiṁ na taret sa ātma-hā*

**“The human body, which can award all benefit in life, is automatically obtained by the laws of nature, although it is a very rare achievement. This human body can be compared to a perfectly constructed boat having the spiritual master as the captain and the instructions of the Personality of Godhead as favorable winds impelling it on its course. Considering all these advantages, a human being who does not utilize his human life to cross the ocean of material existence must be considered the killer of his own soul. (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.20.17)**

*The embodied jīva soul—a boat*  
*Stuck in Kali-yuga’s mire*  
*Can it ever hope for freedom,*  
*Roaming through the din and dire?*  
*The yuga’s waves, saṁsāra-sāgara,*  
*So difficult to behold!*  
*Tossing, churning, shifting, swirling,*  
*Say the sāstras—they’re foretold!*  
*Here below’s a slight description*  
*Of Kali-yuga’s degradation*  
*From twelfth canto, Bhāgavatam.*  
*This description invokes compassion;*  
*Transcendence is its purpose—Be it done!*  
*Cleanliness, tolerance, religion, truth—*  
*Much less of this will be.*  
*Life’s duration, mercy, physical strength,*  
*And memory will flee. (Bhāg.* 12.2.1)  
*Obtaining wealth by any means—*  
*Thus character is judged.*  
*Unless you are exceptionally strong,*  
*Law and justice can’t be budged. (Bhāg*. 12.2.2)  
*Common law—“Let’s live together”—*  
*So-called man and wife.*  
*Without the spiritual connection,*  
*It will always end in strife.*  
*Strength for sex, their “marriage connection,”*  
*Thus their hearts ‘knot grows so tight.*  
*And in business, if there’s cheating,*  
*Then it’s perfectly all right.*  
*Kali-yuga brāhmaṇas rave,*  
*“God?—It’s a force! Imperson! Dead!”*  
*Or even “Who cares ? I’m a brāhmaṇa!*  
*Just look and see my thread! “ (Bhāg.* 12.2.3*)*  
*A “spiritual” man*—*or so they think—*  
*By looking at his clothes.*  
*And nothing of yours you’ll keep*  
*Without money—everyone knows.*  
*A real scholar!—How can you tell?*  
*His speech is expertly composed.*  
*But what it all boils down to mean—*  
*That no one exactly knows! (Bhāg.* 12.2.4*)*  
*People will travel to sacred places*  
*So far, but only to bathe.*  
*No questions will be asked of bhaktas.*  
*For this they are like knaves.*  
*For such a bhakta –pure—*  
*Is unattractive to them there.*  
*The “new” young people—*  
*Their conclusions—are certainly contrair’,*  
*For all their so-called beauty’s*  
*Concentrated in their hair.*  
*In Kali-yuga a talker bold*  
*Is accepted as one who’s right.*  
*If one maintains a tiny fam’ly,*  
*He’s expert (by common sight).*  
*The dharma-dhvajīs—religious flag-wavers—*  
*Will wave for only so long.*  
*“Oh, very religious,” they’d have you think,*  
*Yet Kṛṣṇa consciousness they have none! (Bhāg.* 12.2.6*)*  
*Fifty years only they’ll live their lives,*  
*Cut short by irregularity.*  
*Lashed by rain, wind, and scorching heat,*  
*They’ll have nowhere to flee. (Bhāg.* 12.2.11*)*  
*At last so bad they will become—*  
*All rascals dressed as kings—*  
*To smite them down Lord Kalki comes.*  
*It’s so! The Bhāgavat sings! (Bhāg.* 12.2.20*)*  
*This dreadful Kali-yuga’s*  
*Only part of our misery.*  
*Eight million four hundred thousand*  
*Different boats—saṁsāra-cakra’s gallery!*  
*Unless the boat has a captain*  
*Who will guide it through the storms,*  
*There’ll be nothing that will stop*  
*The fettered jīva’s lives reborn.*  
*As Śaunaka and other ṛṣis*  
*Of Naimiśāraṇya fame*  
*Accepted Sūta Gosvāmī as captain,*  
*We also call your name.*  
*O Prabhupāda! At your feet*  
*Ten thousand Prabhus sit.*  
*You’re the captain of all our boats,*  
*Reconditioned by your wit.*  
*Your merciful guidance makes us able*  
*To cross this sāgara’s width—*  
*So much so that some of us*  
*Will tow small boats –we’re fit.*  
*Yet we’ve no knowledge, wisdom, or strength,*  
*And none of prema-bhakti.*  
*You have all this; we’ve seen it clear—*  
*Your wonderful qualities!*  
*In service staunch to Guru and Kṛṣṇa,*  
*Thus confident, we ply aloof*  
*The dark and desperate Kali-yuga sāgara,*  
*Rendered small as a calf’s hoof.*  
*O Prabhupāda! Unto you this day*  
*With each and every word we pray,*  
*Please let us print and distribute your books*  
*And free the jīvas from Kali’s way.*

(written by Mahā-mantra dāsa)

*Your insignificant servants at the Bombay BBT,*

## Indonesian BBT

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

What can we say to glorify you on this auspicious day of your appearance? How can we, conditioned souls, sufficiently glorify you, a “Vaikuṇṭha man “? Your pastimes and qualities have been nicely described by those of your disciples who are near and dear to you, so what can we, your tiny servants in a far-off, forgotten corner of the world, add to that?

However, we can say that your glories in the form of your books are continuing to expand all over the world, even in Indonesia. From common people in far-away islands to big scholars in the University of Indonesia, all are full of praise for your books, which, despite all impediments, we are humbly attempting to publish in the Indonesian language, on your order. Many Muslims are also appreciating your books and taking to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Only some envious caste-conscious individuals object. But we must continue to execute your order to produce and distribute your wonderful books, in this way trying to repay our immense debt to you for saving us from going to hell again. This we must do even if it means we have to sacrifice our lives in the process.

All we can offer is our repeated prostrate obeisances at your lotus feet. Please bless us so that we may be engaged as your tiny servants eternally, though we have no qualification to ask for such a position. At least let us be engaged in the service of your servants in your ISKCON. Please bless us with one drop of your determination to produce and distribute your books without thinking of anything else.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants at the Indonesian BBT,*

Gaura-maṇḍala-bhūmi dāsa / Aṅgalatā-devī dāsī

## North American BBT

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. And please accept this humble poem on the occasion of your glorious Vyāsa-pūjā day.

**The Śikṣā’s Still Coming from You**

The saṁsāra prayers are all chanted to you,  
Your guru-pūjā’s sung by granddisciples too.  
Your praṇāma is the only one during kīrtana in the temple room  
Because the śikṣā’s still coming from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the  
śikṣā is coming from you.  
Your name alone is chanted in melodious tune,  
Along with Their Lordhships’, while mṛdaṅgas boom.  
On the sole vyāsāsana your mūrti shines like a million moons.  
We see that the śikṣā is coming from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda  
The śikṣā’s coming from you.  
To follow your order is the golden rule  
(Without change to avoid certain spiritual ruin):  
To distribute your books to the masses—the boon  
Of the śikṣā that’s coming from you.  
Your books, the lawbooks that will save mankind from ultimate doom,  
Are the only panacea for the next ten thousand years of this age of gloom.  
To study and distribute them immediately stops our entering another womb—  
As long as we understand that the śikṣā is coming from you, Śrīla  
Prabhupāda, the śikṣā is coming from you.

*Your servants at the North American BBT,*

## Spain BBT

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Materialistic people cannot understand that devotional activities are transcendental because they try to understand them according to material criteria. When they see that we distribute books and receive donations, they think we are the Mafia, operating with black money and exploiting innocent people by brainwashing. When they see we have a farm, they think it is a clandestine center for paramilitary training. When they see us chanting and dancing, they think we have been led astray with drugs. When they attack us and discover that our activities are duly supported by the country’s laws, they try to enact new laws to persecute us. They never read our books—or, if they do, they don’t understand them. Recently a Christian magazine reported that we were pantheistic, while the government’s official source of information was saying that we were followers of *advaita* philosophy. The mass media duly spread all this misinformation, and the whole country comes to believe we are intrinsically wicked.

After a little while, what the people remember from all this misinformation is that “the Hare Kṛṣṇas are bad.” But a few days later, their only memory of the whole thing is reduced to “Hare Kṛṣṇa.” When the *brahmacārīs* from the traveling party and the *gṛhasthas* from the temples go out to distribute your wonderful books on *śaṅkīrtana,* they have shaven heads and are dressed in *dhotis* and *kurtās,* and when the innocent people see them they are attracted and filled with curiosity. The people warmly receive them, and after a short exchange of words they forget all their misconceptions about your wonderful Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. The devotees carry a photo album with pictures of our worshipable Deities, Rādhā-Govinda-candra and Gaura-Nitāi; Ratha-yātrās around the world; the Pada-yātrā cart with the oxen; temples in many countries; the Bombay, Māyāpura, and Vṛndāvana projects; the Food for Life program; *guru-kula;* New Vrajamaṇḍala, etc. In one small box devotees keep some secret *laddus* to break the last barriers. In this way, they are successful in the distribution of your wonderful books.

But the devotees still complain. Why? Not because of the bad press the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement gets in Spain, which is easily overcome, but because after years of experience in book distribution, they still have only four or five different titles to distribute. They meet people who would happily receive the whole collection of your books if they were available.In such a situation, we, the BBT devotees in Spain, cannot present you a merely formal Vyāsa-pūjā offering. Without books, all our glorification would sound hollow and false, for your glorification cannot be any sound other than of the *bṛhat mṛdanga* beating strong. Instead of petty words, we should present you with calendars and schedules of work; the poetry should be the time dedicated daily to book distribution met; the love; the sweat, eagerness, and strong endeavor; the flowers, the pages of dictionaries turning to find just the right word for translation; and the whole *kīrtana,* the sound of the devotees typing on their computers and running other machines in the BBT workshops. With this in mind, this year we offer Your Divine Grace the following Vyāsa-pūjā letter:

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

This last year we suffered some reverses, but now the production of your books in Spanish is going on. Finally, this year devotees will be able to distribute and read the whole Third Canto. By the summer they will have the Fourth Canto, Part One, and by December the whole Fourth Canto will be ready to print. It is possible that the South American branch of the Spanish BBT will complete the Fifth Canto at the same time. We have solved some problems in our production line and are doing the needful to speed it up. Last year Māyā took away two very important men and made us waste so much time. On the other hand, Kṛṣṇa give us more experience and vision. *Ta-ka-ta-ghe-da-ta-ka-ta-ghe-dha.* By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the beating of your Spanish *bṛhat mṛdanga* is starting to be heard.

*Your menial servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust in Spain,*

**Disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Vedavyāsa dāsa

**Granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Yadunandana dāsa / Hanumān dāsa / Sucandra dāsa / Jagannātha Mīśra dāsa / Bhāgavata Purāṇa dāsa

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your shelter-giving feet, which are soft just like lotus petals. The dust from your feet has spread all over this world, and those who are fortunate have been blessed and purified from even just a few particles landing on their bodies. Thus they are able to offer you their intimate and heartfelt thanks and praise on this most auspicious of days, the anniversary of your appearance in this dark and miserable material realm.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I hope you will not mind if instead of writing a long eulogy I offer to your followers a glimpse into how you would spend Vyāsa-pūjā. We all know how we spend it, but I think it will increase everyone’s pleasure to hear something about your transcendental pastimes on this day. And if your disciples and granddisciples become spiritually enlivened by hearing about you, then I am certain you will also become increasingly satisfied, because you are *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī:* You are unhappy to see others suffer, and happy when you see that others are happy. And this offering is meant only for you, for your pleasure.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā: Hyderabad 1976

(adopted from Hari-śaurī dasa's diary)

**August 18, 1976**

This was Janmāṣṭamī, and the devotees fasted all day until mid-night. Śrīla Prabhupāda said he would also fast, even though he had been suffering from a touch of dysentery since his arrival in Hyderabad on the sixteenth.

He took his massage early, at 8:45 am, because the Chief Minister of Andhra Pradesh would be coming as the chief guest at the official opening, and Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted to join him for the first *darśana* of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-mohana, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitai, and Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā-devī in Their temple.

Śrīla Prabhupāda chastised me for failing to clean his silver *loṭa* and the small balcony in the corner of his room, which looked out onto the temple room. I had been used to cleaners for each temple we visited doing such chores, so I had become a little complacent. But in Hyderabad there was a lack of manpower, and I should have done it myself. I was becoming a little dull, but Prabhupāda was as sharp as ever.

He called in Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Prabhu and then Mahāṁśa Swami to check with them the arrangements for the opening. He especially chastised Mahāṁsa Swami because Prabhupāda’s quarters were so dirty (construction was still going on and dust was everywhere.) Prabhupāda said that the spiritual master’s room should be ready even before the Deity’s, because this is the higher form of worship.

Śrīla Prabhupāda called the two of them in to complain that there were no decorations of flowers, mango leaves, banana trees, etc, for the temple room, but fortunately they were able to arrange for them before the program started. However, Prabhupāda was obviously disturbed by the lack of proper organization.

The Chief Minister arrived at 11.15am, and Prabhupāda had the *sannyāsīs* receive him at the front entrance to the property and escort him into the temple room. Then Prabhupāda went down. About four hundred people were packed into the temple room, and after various speeches Prabhupāda gave his lecture. Then everyone crowded around as he went to the front of the Deity room with the Chief Minister to await the first *darśana.*

The *pujārīs* were running very late, so the *ārati* was delayed. Eventually some South Indian *brāhmaṇas* who had done the installation ceremonies over the previous few days came to the rescue, and with their help the doors opened at 12.15pm. There was a great rush forward as everyone pushed to the front to see Their transcendental Lordships better. All the Deities were beautifully dressed, and the crowds were very excited to see Them.

After *darśana* the Chief Minister took his leave, and Prabhupāda went back upstairs and remained there for the rest of the day. At about 5.30pm, many of the devotees went out on an elephant parade through the city with the small Gaura-Nitai Deities. Even after they had gone, the temple room remained packed, so I went down and chanted over the microphone for some time, and a few hundred more people appeared for the evening *ārati.* I also gave a short talk on the benefits of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, and then at 6.45pm, the devotees returned. After an ecstatic *ārati,* the devotees chanted *bhajanas* and then showed the film *Spiritual Frontier.* During the chanting Prabhupāda came out onto his small balcony despite the fact that there was no rail and sat for a while, giving *darśana* to all the people from his perch high up in the temple room.

After some time, in mid-evening, he decided to rest, and then he took Ekādaśī *prasādam* at about 10.45pm. We were all amazed that he lasted so long with the fast, in view of his general physical weakness. Finally he took rest at 11.15pm. Just before he did, it began to rain very hard, and he said this was an auspicious sign that everything had been a success. However, because the balcony entrance had no door yet, his room was directly open to the temple room save for a curtain, and it was very difficult for him to rest because of the large crowd that had gathered for the midnight *ārati.*

Although the *pujārīs* were late again–this time by forty minutes–the hundreds of eager worshipers waited very patiently and were well rewarded with an ecstatic *ārati* and a feast afterwards.

**August 19th, 1976**

This was Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā Day, the eightieth anniversary of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance. All the devotees, of course, fasted for half a day, but unfortunately Prabhupāda also virtually fasted due to bad digestion and some mucus problem. He had Pālikā prepare a very small amount of fruit for breakfast and only *kicchari* and yogurt for lunch.

It was exciting to spend Vyāsa-pūjā directly serving His Divine Grace, and I wondered if I should offer him something special as a gift. However, without any resources, I had to be content with offering my usual service, knowing that he was always appreciative of whatever devotional service we did.

He went down at 11am, for the program and as he sat down on the beautiful marble *vyāsāsana,* the devotees bathed his feet and then offered flowers in the Puśpāñjalī ceremony. There was a good crowd, and Prabhupāda had Harikeśa Maharaja stand up and read from his Vyāsa-pūjā book several offerings of leading devotees and temples. When Prabhupāda had first received the book in Bombay just before coming to Hyderabad, he had lain on his back on his *āsana* in his room with his head against one bolster and his feet up on the other and carefully went through it. He had liked it very much, and when I came in he had smiled and given a little exclamation of delight mixed with humble appreciation as he leafed through and said, "Oh, they have put such intimate offerings!” He had looked through the entire book, and as the name of each center came up he had announced it out loud and looked carefully at the photos and read some of the offerings.

Now, on his actual Vyāsa-pūjā day, he had Harikeśa Swami read out all the names of the centers in the book. This was to let the large gathering of people know about the strength of his ISKCON movement.

This reading was followed by short declarations of appreciation by Jayapatāka Swami and Gargamuni Swami, who had arrived that morning from Māyāpura, as well as Mahāṁśa Swami, Yaśodanandana Swami, and Gopala Kṛṣṇa Prabhu. Then Sampat Kumar Bhattacharya also gave a short talk in praise of Prabhupāda. Finally, Śrīla Prabhupāda himself spoke before returning to his room for a massage, *prasādam*, *and rest.*

In the afternoon Jayapatāka Swami and Gargamuni Swami came and gave their report. Prabhupāda was pleased to see them and eager to hear of the progress in Māyāpura. Since the last festival, Jayapatāka Swami had been working very earnestly on trying to get the government to authorize acquisition of about four hundred acres of land to expand the Māyāpura project, but he had not been able to make any headway due to the opposition of one local official who had commented, ‘The British East India Company got twenty acres and controlled the whole of India.” Mr. Choudhari, a man in the Lands Department in Calcutta who visited Prabhupāda in January, was helping to push the purchase through, however. Seventy per cent of the local people were favorable to the plan, but the communists were causing trouble. They disliked our movement and had asked for a government investigation, and on several occasions, they had created very bad publicity about our supposed CIA links. But still the public’s response was favorable. The final report from the two swamis concerned our Albert Road temple in Calcutta, where Abhirāma Prabhu, under Prabhupāda’s direction, was trying to secure the right to build a large monument in the center of the lake opposite the temple.

In the evening Śrīla Prabhupada gave *darśana* to various visitors and then took rest at the normal time.

\* \* \*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this remembrance of your 1976 Vyāsa-pūjā is offered with deepest respect and love and with increasing appreciation that we were so fortunate to have been there. May the time come again when this opportunity manifests, for this is all we live for.

(written by Hari-śaurī dāsa)

*Your servants at Back to Godhead Magazine,*

Jayādvaita Swami / Umāpati Swami / Hari-śaurī dāsa / Nāgarāja dāsa / Yamarāja dāsa / Prabhupāda dāsa / Drutakarma dāsa / Jagannatha-Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Nityānanda dāsa / Śitalā-devī dāsī / Prāṇadā-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Nancy

## Bhaktivedanta Youth Services

**An Ode to Śrīla Prabhupāda**

*We a host of morning flowers scarce bloomed,*  
*Do offer ourselves to thy golden beams—*  
*Some youthful souls, all by thy teachings groomed,*  
*And bathing in thy mercy’s cooling streams.*  
*You are the captain of the ship,*  
*And we, the crew, have staked our lives on thee.*  
*We know you’ll save us, though our oars may slip.*  
*If whirlpools stop us, you will set us free.*  
*We are an army fighting Māyā’s wiles,*  
*And you would ever be our general, lord.*  
*We never lose, though the world beguiles,*  
*For virtue’s our strength, knowledge our sharp sword.*  
*O Prabhupāda, let us serve you, though*  
*Untrained and young, we wretched servants be.*  
*The BYS to thee doth humbly bow,*  
*For through your books you gave us eyes to see.*

(written by Bhakta Krishanu Lahirī)

*Your servants in the Bhaktivedanta Youth Services (Calcutta).*

## Bhaktivedanta Institute, San Francisco

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept our fallen prostrated obeisances at your glorious lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Your glories are spreading unlimitedly as time passes. The transcendental books that you have planted all over the planet are bringing newer and newer results. By your mercy the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa is now popular all over the world. You have great love for Kṛṣṇa and us because you are always subservient to Lord Kṛṣṇa’s supreme will.

We are attempting to see how your divine mercy has engaged us in Lord Kṛṣṇa’s service during the past year. You wrote to His Holiness Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami, “ Do a conference in every major city of the world; I will send all the men and money you want.” We can feel the transcendental strength and truth of this instruction of Your Divine Grace. To fulfill your desires, we organized the first International Conference on the Study of Consciousness Within Science in San Francisco, and by your mercy devotees came from different parts of the world to help us with this conference. By your mercy, the conference itself was a spectacular success. More than four hundred scientists and scholars came from the United States, Canada, the United Kingdom, and even the Soviet Union. They got a chance to hear some aspects of the Vedic philosophy concerning consciousness, intelligence, mind, and body. We arranged for a wholly academic conference, yet some of the delegates, even before learning that the *prasādam* was vegetarian, asked for strictly vegetarian food. We could see that this is the effect of the preaching movement you have left behind for the salvation of mankind for generations to come.

You taught us that the way to win over scientists and scholars is through hospitality. In today’s impersonal Western world, the Vedic tradition of hospitality is a refreshing breath of fresh air; therefore, even the intellectuals will pause to consider the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You taught us that even a stone-hearted man can get purified by honoring *Kṛṣṇa-prasādam.*

Though there are problems even within the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement because of the influence of Kali-yuga, you have proved your infinite wisdom by providing a system of transcendental management. By your mercy we can see that even though there are problems, present-day ISKCON is more unified and facing the challenge of Kali-yuga in a concerted manner.

On this auspicious occasion of your appearance day, we humbly pray that you permit us to become instruments in executing the tasks needed to develop this Bhaktivedanta Institute to your transcendental satisfaction . We also pray that in doing this Your Divine Grace will kindly guide us in our dealings with other Vaiṣṇavas so that we may not commit offenses.

All Glories to Your Divine Grace!

*Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute, San Francisco,*

Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami / Hanumāt Preṣaka Swami / Rasarāja dāsa / Abhimanyu dāsa / Rājahaṁsa dāsa / Bhakta Greg / Bhakta Malai / Dayā-devī dāsī / Haripriyā-devī dāsī

## Fort Myers Beach

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer this prayer to you because you are the torchlight of knowledge forcibly destroying the darkness of ignorance:

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.”** ***(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22)**

Abortion, AIDS, wholesale animal slaughter, state-run lotteries, the idea of legalizing (and thus taxing) drugs, homosexuality—-yes, the ocean of Kali is indeed rocky, and these are only the small beginning waves. However, the boat of the Lord’s lotus feet, of which you are the most able captain, can easily ply these treacherous waters. By your grace we have been given passage on this boat, and when we take shelter of you and your instructions we are truly safe. What can we say of those who do not board such a secure vessal and instead try to cross on their own? It is their foolishness that leads them to search for some other way across. *Tasmād guruṁ prapadyeta jijñāsuḥ śreya uttamam:* “Any person who seriously desires real happiness must seek out a bona fide spiritual master and take shelter of him by initiation.” (SB 11.3.21)

Even more astounding is the fact that some, after reaching such a shelter as this ship, jump overboard into the ocean to struggle again on their own.

Is there any aspect of our life that you have not touched or left us guidance for? Certainly not. As a child your desires were for Ratha-yātrā with *kīrtana and prasādam.* You have fulfilled your childhood desires in a grand way for us, your spiritual children, and shown us how to engage our own children in transcendental activities. As a young boy you were intent on worshiping Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa as you saw your father do. You have also fulfilled that desire on every continent of this globe and thus given us an example to show our children. As a young boy you listened to your teachers give their explanations of history and philosophy, but all the time you understood the real goal of your life, and thus you gave us the *gurukula* so our children would not be misguided. Although you married and engaged in business, you never became overwhelmed by such things. You used your money and time to assist your Guru Maharaja’s mission and to print books for distribution. Your home was always a place for Vaiṣṇavas to gather, as was your father’s before you. In this way we should also use our homes for the association of devotees.

*Tato dhḥsaṇgam utsṛjya satsu sajjeta buddhimān*  
*Santa evāsya chindanti mano vyāsaṅgam uktibhiḥ*

**“One should therefore avoid bad company and associate only with devotees. With their realized instructions, such saints can cut the knot connecting one with activities unfavorable to devotional service.” (SB 11.26.26)**

No, we do not find any part of life that cannot serve as an example for us. As a renunciant you never accepted any personal opulence or wealth. You were satisfied in a simple tent in the mud of Kumbh-mela as you were in the grandeur of your Manhattan skyscraper. You were never bewildered by opulent surroundings, since you understood them to be Kṛṣṇa’s gift for pushing on your Guru Maharaja’s mission. Your life had no secret activities: you gave of yourself completely and selflessly, your only desire being to fill up your transcendental boat to its fullest capacity and train others to be captains of more boats. By and large, though, we remain the unworthy seamen and you the expert admiral of the fleet.

*Guravo bahavo santi śiśya vittapa hārakaḥ*  
*Durlabhaḥ sad gurur devī śiśya santapa hārakaḥ*

**“O Devī, there are many** ***gurus*** **who are expert at plundering the wealth of their disciples, but a real** ***guru*** **who can destroy the threefold miseries of the disciple is very difficult to find.”**

So, just at you gave us so many standards to come up to, now we must come up to the standard of pure-hearted desire to carry on your mission. After meeting Śrīla Bhaktisiddhantā Sarasvatī, you always kept your service to him fixed in your mind. We pray that in whatever stage of life we find ourselves, we may also keep our service to you as our main objective. Let us therefore always associate with you through your books, your lectures, and your disciples.

*Mahat kṛpa vinā kona karma bhakti naya*  
*Kṛṣṇa-bhakti dure rahu saṁsāra nahe kṣaya*

**“Unless one is favored by a pure devotee, one cannot attain the platform of devotional service. To say nothing of** ***Kṛṣṇa-bhakti,*** **one cannot even be relieved from the bondage of material entanglement.”**

Therefore we, your humble servants in Fort Myers Beach, beg you to please be kind upon us and show us your mercy. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servants in Fort Myers, Florida,*

Rasātala dāsa / Mūlakā-devī dāsī, Śyāma, and Dhyāna / Caturātma dāsa / Paśupati-devī dāsī / Nāmānanda, and Mañjulā / Jñānagamya dāsa, Mahāmāyā-devī dāsī, and Rasācārya / Draviṇākṣa dāsa and Nandā-devī dāsī / Paṇkajanābha dāsa / Alambha dāsa / Vatsara dāsa / Bhakta Paul / Bhakta Shane

## ISKCON World Review

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

At *ISKCON World Review,* we live surrounded by Kṛṣṇa conscious news: devotees distribute books in New Guinea, hold a Ratha-yātrā in South Africa, open a farm in Brazil. New comes in and news goes out, all over the world. But the real news is the news behind the news.

The real news is that these activities of devotees all over the world come from the inspiration of Śrīla Prabhupāda: “Distribute my books, hold Ratha-yātrā, open farms.” The real story is that a *mahā-bhāgavata* came to America in 1965, with no resources visible to mundane eyes, and single-handedly spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

And the most sensational news of all is that even though he had apparently left this world, his spirit is still acting here. “Distribute my books”—his words still push his disciples into action with the same words.

And devotees take Prabhupāda’s words all over the world—into a Druze home in Isreal, into a Yoruba shop in Nigeria, a Quechua village in Peru.

Every news editor all over the world is looking for that special story, the story that breaks away from the ordinary, the story that is too good to be true yet is true. *ISKCON World Review* has it. It’s the story of Prabhupāda—-a real-life miracle that never ends.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda’s humble servants at ISKCON World Review.*

## KrishnaFest

To His Divine Grace Oṁ Vishnupāda PaRāmahaṁsa Parivarājakācārya Śrī Śrīmad 108 A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda:

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the Preacher-ācārya  
Bhaktivinoda’s vision, crystal clear,  
The empowered preacher would soon appear.  
Punya-bhūmi, the famed pious land,  
Delivered our master from Kṛṣṇa’s hand.  
O Calcutta, most fortunate one,  
From your precincts rose a brilliant son.  
Praṇāmas to Bhaktisiddhanta, whose fervent request  
Propelled our captain to the wild,wild West.  
Thank you, Sumati, dear noble mother.  
You sponsored that voyage somehow or other.  
Śrī Jaladuta, unequaled ferry,  
Indebted to thee, our savior carried.  
Who ventures out in their seventies,  
With suitcase, cane, and forty rupees?  
Sworn to reach ev’ry village and town,  
While some rebuked with skeptic frown.  
In Bow’ry loft, you lived and taught;  
Bums and addicts shared the spot.  
Boston Brahmins, brahmacāriṇīs too,  
In the smarta camps were all taboo.  
Striyo vaiśyās thatā ṣudrās  
Te ‘pi yānti parāṁ gatim.  
Your pen flowed forth ecstatic emotions,  
Filled books more grave and vast than oceans.  
Poor Mr. Darwin, son-of-an-ape,  
His theory you had to decapitate.  
You punished severe that cruel cunning Nair;  
“’Twas a good fight,” that Bombay affair  
Brainwashing charges came, “Ha!” you said,  
“There are no brains to wash within their head!”  
Anti-cultists took us to court ;  
Your defense plan cut the war quite short.  
“Read all the books; then tell your decision.”  
High Court judged “bona fide religion.”  
Gentle father, guru and friend,  
Saving the earth’s coal-hearted men,  
Trillions of couplets could not begin  
To find your glories—-the thought a sin.  
By their divine grace, your service surpassed  
That of Narada, Nitāi, and also Vyās.  
Jayānanda donated his very last dime;  
Couldn’t bear Prabhupāda’s final pastime.  
Vṛndāvana-dhāma you’re not to blame,  
Even though you called him back again.  
We’ll join you too when we’re all through  
Executing the orders he gave us to do.

*(*written by Dānavīra dāsa Vanacārī)

*Your humble servants at KrishnaFest,*

Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami / Dānavīra dāsa Vanacārī / Gaura dāsa / Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa / Valmīki dāsa / Sanātana dāsa / Kalki dāsa / Kaustabha dāsa / Bhakta Mike / Bhakta Jeffrey / Bhakta Ray / Bhakta Justin / Bhakta Jay / Bhakta David / Bhakta Rob / Bhakta Frankie

## Marco Ferrini Design

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga.

I am taking this very auspicious occasion, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, to try to express my inner feelings about Your Divine Grace.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for having created this wonderful International Society for Krishna Consciousness, this transcendental great family spread all over the world. When touring worldwide, we always feel completely safe, at home. Everywhere we find nice hospitality by your disciples and granddisciples, our Godbrothers and Godsisters. Everywhere we find the same spiritual mood; our primeval father and mother, the Deities; transcendental delicious *Kṛṣṇa-prasādam;* gentle behavior; high education; nice friendship; and good taste for *Kṛṣṇa-kathā.* Everywhere in your temples we find the precious opportunity to take up some devotional service to our beloved Lordships.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your books and preaching work. You did the most wonderful transcendental service to humanity at large. Your Divine Grace gave us spiritual knowledge of life’s true supreme goal.

Even in this present life we experienced some kind of death. We were lost in material madness, overwhelmed by material desires. Led by polluted intelligence, we didn’t know where to go to get real relief from our heavy anxiety. We hardly perceived any dangerous situation, although we were drowning in an ocean of imminent dangers. We mistook sense gratification for real happiness, the body for the eternal self, speculative knowledge for the supreme truth. We were totally blind, serving illusory goals and living with other blind men in a fearful position. Then came Your Divine Grace, bringing spiritual light into this material world, and everything became miraculously clear. Soon we became accustomed to seeing the world through your divine eyes, with knowledge. We discovered the peaceful dimension of *sattva-guṇa* and transcendental service to the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Although I am not, for sure, a very good devotee, still I am feeling a very strong attachment to your divine instructions. I feel my humble self eternally indebted to you and deeply desiring to serve your lotus feet and your devotees, much more than I did in the past.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me. Let me try to keep serving Your Divine Grace forever.

*Your useless servant,*  
Matsya Avatāra dāsa

## Ministry of Vaiṣṇava Studies

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You often quoted the verse *brahma-bhūtaḥ prasannātmā na śocati na kāṅkṣati.* The pure devotee situated in the Absolute is not affected by loss or gain. The pure devotee situated in the Absolute is not affected by loss or gain. Unaffected by advances and retreats, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, delivered the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which transcends the events of history. You taught us that our apparent successes and failures are in themselves not important; rather, the service to *guru* and Kṛṣṇa are essential.

I remember when Aniruddha and I, in our shortsightedness, lost the Los Angeles temple. It was 1969, and in our zeal we attempted to please you with the acquisition of a new temple, but we failed. We both were very eager to participate in the ever-increasing success of the Vaiṣṇava movement, but we were devastated by our apparent failure.

Just one year later we celebrated Śrīla Bhaktisiddhanta’s disappearance day in what was then the grandest temple in the movement, the site of our present-day Los Angeles temple. The manifestation of this temple was a result of your grace.

The history of Śrī Caitanya’s movement has seen many such retreats and advances. In Vṛndāvana, in the late sixteenth century, the Gosvāmīs and their disciples built grand temples, great architecture and spiritual achievements. Then due to the wrath of Aurangazeb and his warriors, the Deities had to leave Their beautiful homes in Vṛndāvana, the homes that the Gosvāmis spent years establishing.

The Deities were taken by the devotees out of Vṛndāvana, west, over an eight-year period, ultimately to reside in Jaipur. By the mid-eighteenth century, the city of Jaipur had been transformed into a Govinda-conscious city: Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda had officially become king and queen of Jaipur. Even to this day, the *ārati* ceremonies that occur six times a day for Rādhā and Govinda are some of the best-attended temple functions in India.

In the first half of the twentieth century, several Vaiṣṇavas preachers attempted to establish the practice and philosophy of Vaisnavism in the West. Some traveled and lived her, some traveled and did not even make it this far. Their vigorous attempts did not succeed, and they demonstrated how hard it would be for anyone to bring living Vaisnaism to the West.

In 1965 you came to New York to flood the world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This was an event that, although recorded as a remarkable part of history, surely goes beyond history. Your appearance in the West reminds us of the beginning of the fourth chapter of *Bhagavad-gīta:* The science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is beyond history. Like Kṛṣṇa, you are transcendental to history. You, as Kṛṣṇa’s associate and agent, appeared here to reestablish the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness on the authority of the *parampara.* You are wonderful and beyond our limited comprehension. Beyond loss and gain, you and the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness will remain absolute, *brahma-bhūta.*

Thus the superficial ebb and flow of the ocean waves of Vaiṣṇava activities throughout history constitute the retreats and advances in establishing the Vaiṣṇava path. But the ocean of Vaiṣṇava service to God remains unmoved by the ebb and flow of its waves. You taught us that steady sincere service to Lord Kṛṣṇa is what must be constant in our lives.

In 1979 this ministry of Vaiṣṇava Studies began with Vaiṣṇava scholars and assistants. There were also academic scholars who were associated with this work through its outside sister organization. Since its inception, the Vaiṣṇava and academic scholars associated with this work have tried to expand, to increase.

One of our goals is to establish Vaisnavism as an independent field of knowledge. To do this, we must record, annotate, collect, preserve, etc., a library of Vaiṣṇava primary and secondary literatures. Also, we must write and publish current books and articles on Vaiṣṇava history, thought, and practice. Ideally we would create university “chairs” of Vaisnavism in departments of philosophy and religion, leaders for the future of ISKCON would be trained.

In spite of our enthusiasm to quickly achieve all these majestic goals, we are working foremost for quality and purity. We attempt to produce results that will bring glory to your name,Śrīla Prabhupāda. In this way we hope slowly but carefully to lastingly participate in an unfolding chapter of the transcendental history of Vaisnavism.

By your mercy we shall be able to offer you a fit tribute to your everlasting glory. But we are nothing without you. We know that you are satisfied with anything we accomplish for Kṛṣṇa.

*Ohe! Vaiṣṇava ṭhākura dayāra sāgara e dāse karuṇā kori.* We pray that you give us a drop of your mercy. Your *saṅkīrtana* movement is our salvation. Give us the six good qualities, control our six urges, purify the six faults, and help us associate with Vaiṣṇavas.

Kṛṣṇa is yours. Please, we are weak, but we pursue you like beggars, crying “Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa! Please give us Kṛṣṇa.”

(written by Dayānanda dāsa)

*Your servants,*

## Padyātrā America

On this happiest of occasions  
Let our offering be sincere,  
Thankful to our revered master,  
Having served him one more year.  
We’ve been to watch the waves to time  
Take form and close like traps on shore.  
We’ve seen the coastline forms of lust  
Transform to figures stretched and strewn.  
This world is not a gentle place.  
Sought, and searched, we found a trace  
Of hope among the graceful ones  
Who soar above the mundane strife  
And beckon us to catch the draft.  
Paupers are proud of their pennies.  
Our hardened hearts and cheated past  
Made us ignore the needed climb  
Till Prabhupāda appeared to show  
The process for the soul to rise.  
The topmost step down to deliver.  
Now systemed steps of love he taught  
We practice ‘fore the public eye  
To start the fortunate others’ climb  
And broaden our perception’s range.  
The means is also the end.  
This every step through every town  
We make to glorify our saint,  
Who has us preach so others join  
And spread the holy name around.  
There is no other way, no other way.

*Your servants on Padyātrā America,*

## Padyātrā India

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“When the Lord visited all those holy places, He covered many thousands of residents. Thus, many people were delivered. Simply by touching the holy places, He made them into great places of pilgrimage. “ (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **9.14*****)***

On board the *Jaladuta,* September 10, 1965 you wrote in your diary, “ My only solace is Śrī Caitanya līlā. I have left Bharatabhumi just to execute the order of Śrī Bhaktisiddhānta Saraswatī in pursuance of Lord Caitanya’s order. I have no qualifications, but I have taken up the risk just to carry out the order of His Divine Grace.”

It is beyond imagination how often you spoke about going on piligrīmage during the few short years you were physically with us, but due to your extremely busy schedule, unfortunately you could not go. And now, due to your unlimited merciful energy, you have been traversing the entire Bhārata-bhūmi to execute the order of your Guru Maharaja, similarly, by that sincerity you have inspired so many others to leave their *bhūmi,* their homeland, just to execute your divine instructions in pursuance of the *guru-paramparā* and Lord Caitanya’s order. And not only in Bhārata-varṣa have you been on pilgrimage for the past six years, but now your introduce-millions-of-bullock-carts-all-over-the-world instruction is finally becoming a reality. You are able to expand your Padayātrā desire all over the planet while at the same time never leaving your dear Bhārata-bhūmi.

Very few souls really realize the great austerities you performed during your advanced years. Constantly sharing your loving self with so many; translating those “priceless gems” for everyone; managing and instructing with precise perfection; and traveling with the speed of sound around and around. In between all of this, you still had great interest in performing Padayātrā. You responded to one person who was also interested in Padayātrā: “ I am immediately ready…I can immediately take up this program… It will be very, very effective…….” And you are immediately ready each and every morning as your Saṅkīrtana Padayātrā rolls down the Indian roads, attracting countless fortunate conditioned souls for your *darśaṇa* and that of Śrī Śrī Nitai-Gaurasundara.

Daily, upon seeing your divine form, several people ask, “ When will he speak or move?” You sit on your *vyāsāsana,* so content being on pilgrimage, even through all the difficulties arising form the constant weather changes and public demand. Even late at night, after the villagers have seen and heard about your glories in your ISKCON films, they desperately want your *darśaṇa* by torchlight! And since you are the most merciful, you tolerate all such inconveniences just to spread the holy name around to every town and village.

O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your loving inspiration you will be leading all the numerous Padayātrās around this planet for the next ten thousand years. This Padayātrā program that you always desired is not a part-time service for a few years, but an eternal pastime of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu going on under your personal guidance.

One hundred thousand kilometers by 1996 for your centennial is just a simple offering of “thank you” for allowing your disciples, granddisciples, and future disciples to be able to walk alongside you and Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Nitai-Gaurasundara. We are eternally indebted to Your Divine Grace and your Padayātrā program. Please forgive us all for our countless offenses, since we do not have any good qualities. We take shelter at your walking lotus feet.

*Your insignificant walking servants on Padayātrā in India,*

## Pyramid Center for Bhakti-Yoga

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The scientists are busy discovering new moons of planets billions of miles away, but as their eyes are busy peering down their telescopes and staring at their electronic data screens, they do not see that personality who has brought them the real moon—-the moon of Lord Caitanya’s mercy. *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! You have risen from the east to spread those cooling rays of the benediction moon to the entire world, and your books and disciples radiate that light which you have ignited.

Throughout history there are only a few names that are remembered against the ravages of time, and some of these names are not famous, but infamous. And among the famous, there are precious few who are recalled for their saintly contributions to all of the mankind. And of those who are pious, just the most minuscule number are recognized as pure devotees of the Lord. Yet this is how we remember you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We think of you as one who loves Kṛṣṇa, loves Kṛṣṇa’s creation, and loves us. Gradually, the people of this world will understand your greatness. They will marvel at your pastimes. They will listen to your instructions. And some will even hear you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, *ki jaya!*

You have given us a new birth, and with that birth has come a new family, a brethren who mostly understand each other more deeply than blood relations. We are the “dāsa” clan, and our common father is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. So it is that we come before you daily to request your mercy, like good children offering respect to their father. We share our thoughts with each other like brothers and sisters, and we help one another stay within the family. Because of your mercy, our family continues to grow. I can travel in practically any country in the world and be welcomed by my devotee family, and my path back to home is lined with well-wishers.

Śrīla Prabhupada, you have said so many things in your books, and I cannot understand them all. I am constantly asking my Godbrothers and Godsisters for advice on so many subjects. I am not able to follow even the simplest of your instructions anywhere near perfectly. I am mainly interested in eating and sleeping. But each day you wake me up with the sound of the conch –your instructions—calling me to battle my lower nature and surrender to Lord Kṛṣṇa. You taught us all how to serve Kṛṣṇa and His devotees, and now we are helping each other carry out your orders. How is that a person is no longer physically present, yet his advice, which is lots of times hard to take, is followed? I don’t know how you could continue to keep me in the family, wake me by your instructions, and allow me to serve you, your disciples, and your mission. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! I hope I can make a feature film about you, too.

I beg to remain your servant eternally,  
(written by Nrsimhananda dāsa)

*Your servants at the Pyramid Center for Bhakti-yoga, Topanga Canyon, California,*

Nrsimhananda dāsa / Śacī-dulāla dāsa / Mohanā-devī dāsī / Paraca-devī dāsī

## Save the Cow

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Everyone who studies the events in your lifetime understands that because of your desire to fulfill the orders of your spiritual master, Kṛṣṇa arranged the perfect time for you to come to the Western countries. Many young people were searching for the truths of life, and also for an alternative lifestyle. Your teachings gave both transcendental information and Vedic culture, and thus many people happily took to Kṛṣṇa consciousness and tried to propagate it.

We also see, several years later, how Kṛṣṇa is continuing to fulfill your ardent desire that Kṛṣṇa consciousness be spread. The Lord is doing this by allowing us, your humble servants, to take advantage of the current mood. Today there is a strong move among sensitive citizens toward animal rights and preserving ecological balance, and there is a simultaneous mood against falsely based economic endeavors that result in so many atrocities.

We are excited to be able to “infiltrate” these groups of thoughtful people with the absolute knowledge presented by you, and to give them a solid basis for their convictions.

However, without practically showing these people the proper lifestyle—one based on cow protection and ox power and having Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy as its guide—what potency will we have? We need to preach by words and example, and our example is currently weak.

We have faith that Kṛṣṇa will fulfill your desire to implement Vedic culture in the form of Kṛṣṇa conscious farming communities. Our participation in this is dependent on our purity and sincerity, and we strongly desire to be a part of this spiritual and cultural revolution.

So, with this humble offering we would also like to submissively pray for the determination to follow all your instructions, especially the basic ones, so that we may attain perfection and be your pure and proper instruments for giving it to others, in spite of our many shortcomings.

*Your servants at Save the Cow, Alachua, Florida.*

Brajendra-nandana dāsa / Kamrā-devī dāsī
